# Bug hunt — 2026-08-14 (full-repo deep sweep)

> **Archive.** Kept as history; the addresses, hostnames and people in it are
> those of the original deployment. Do not copy commands out of it.

Third fleet hunt. 12 Opus hunters over disjoint territories (every tracked
source file in the repo), each section adversarially verified by a second
Opus agent instructed to refute. Hunters were briefed on CLAUDE.md, the
KNOWN_BUGS ledger, and both 2026-08-11 hunt archives, so nothing below
re-reports a resolved or deliberately-diverged item. The working tree at
hunt time included the (uncommitted) 08-11 fix pass.

**Tally: 94 confirmed (10 critical / 46 major / 38 minor), 7 uncertain, 10 refuted.**
Refuted findings are archived at the bottom so the next hunt does not
re-surface them.

**RESOLUTION 2026-08-14 (same day): FIXED, same-day, by an 11-agent Opus fix
pass (disjoint file territories, orchestrator-reconciled, 6 follow-up
dispatches for the cross-territory seams). All 10 criticals are fixed. All 10
suites green (`tools\run_all_tests.ps1`: 0 of 10 failed), ~+250 tests.
NOTHING SHIPPED YET — see KNOWN_BUGS.md for the ship path and R16 for the
eight findings deliberately not fixed (each needs a live spike, a benchmark,
or a two-sided design that must land atomically; per-finding reasoning stays
below in each section's entry).** Deliberate divergences from the suggested
fixes are recorded per-finding in the agents' words at the code sites and
summarized in R16's sibling notes; the notable mechanism choices: one
`expire_lease()` instead of new stop paths (YTDL-WEB-1/-2), sequencer-side
lane arming (SYNC-1), `-o infojson:` scratch redirect verified against real
yt-dlp (COMP-BROLL-1), wedge attribution instead of a bridge rewrite
(COMP-MEDIA-9), and `--min-size 1B` — measured: a bare `1` parses as 1 KiB
(COMP-GUARD-1 defence).

## Critical index

- **COMP-GUARD-1** — A hard kill mid-copy strands a 0-byte file under the FINAL name, and nothing ever sweeps it (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/fixer.py:459`)
- **SYNC-1** — Lane B is latched off forever after any stop/start cycle in managed mode (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:2103`)
- **COMP-MEDIA-1** — Companion proxies never normalize drop-frame timecode — the exact R10 refusal, unfixed on the fleet's own encoder (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ffmpeg_tools.py:527`)
- **COMP-BROLL-1** — `--write-info-json` drops a 40-200 KB file into the Syncthing sendreceive project tree, then deletes it -- the exact `ignoreDelete` immortality bug R15 fix 3 just closed for `.part`/`.ytdl` (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:1159`)
- **SERVER-1** — The SFTP half of every post-swap tree push is unguarded, so a dropped transport still escapes main() as a traceback (the exact OPS-3/OPS-2 hole run_ssh_guarded was added to close) (`server/install_dashboard_app.py:1710`)
- **BROLL-WEB-1** — mode="semantic" returns zero results for every CJK query (`broll/web/app/search.py:1148`)
- **BROLL-IDX-1** — The bare "429" rate-limit marker is matched against EVERY stage's error text, so a filename or path containing 429 aborts and permanently wedges the run (`broll/indexer/broll_index/pipeline.py:618`)
- **BROLL-IDX-2** — The overnight watchdog restarts the PRE-FOLD standalone checkout, silently re-introducing R9 (10-bit previews), R10 (no proxy timecode) and BROLL-1/2 (unrecorded sprite geometry) (`broll/indexer/watchdog.ps1:22`)
- **MUSIC-1** — The text-encoder artefact is written to disk BEFORE it is verified, so a failed check leaves a loadable, drifting artefact behind (`music/indexer/export_text_encoder.py:284`)
- **SHIP-1** — A failed -RebuildOnboard PyInstaller run still publishes the PREVIOUS onboard.exe under the NEW installer version (OPS-7 was only fixed for the companion half) (`installer/build_editor_package.ps1:266`)

## COMP-CORE — Companion core (tray app, config, UI, upgrade)

### COMP-CORE-2 (major, logic) — The stale-bridge self-restart reuses the upgrade's spawn+shutdown machinery without any of apply_upgrade's in-flight-work guards
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:2374`

`apply_upgrade()` (app.py:3021-3056) deliberately refuses to spawn a replacement and shut down while `self._popup_active_lock.locked()` or `self._consolidate_active` is true, with a comment citing AUDIT_2 CORE-H8/H5: "Never swap the exe and exit out from under work in progress: the spawned copy would be killed mid-shutil.copy2, leaving a partial .ccsync-tmp and a project where some clips are relinked and some are not." R12 added `_maybe_recover_stale_bridge()` (app.py:2338-2376), which calls `upgrade_mod.restart_self(request_shutdown=self.shutdown)` -- the same spawn + `request_shutdown()` sequence minus the binary swap -- from the media-tree thread every `media_tree_refresh_interval` (120 s). It checks only `bridge_auto_restart`, a once-per-process latch, `session_state()` and `resolve_is_running()`. Neither `_consolidate_active` nor `_popup_active_lock` is consulted, and the tests (companion/tests/test_app.py:4177-4223) pin only those four guards.

**Failure scenario:** An editor runs tray -> "Copy this project's media in". `consolidate_project()` sets `_consolidate_active = True` and holds `_popup_active_lock` across a `ProgressWindow` for the whole copy + lane-A upload (app.py:1937-2012) -- documented as potentially multi-hour. Partway through the upload phase the editor quits DaVinci Resolve (nothing about consolidate needs it any more). Within 120 s the media-tree thread calls `_refresh_media_tree_once()` -> `_maybe_recover_stale_bridge()`: the bridge has `ever_connected=True`, is now disconnected, and `resolve_is_running()` is False -- all four guards pass. `restart_self()` spawns a replacement and calls `self.shutdown()`, which stops the lanes (killing the in-flight rclone child) and drops `_stop_event`, so the process exits with the consolidate worker daemon thread killed mid-`shutil.copy2`. The editor is left with a vanished progress window, a partial `.ccsync-tmp` in the tree, and a project where some clips are relinked and some are not -- precisely the CORE-H8/H5 outcome apply_upgrade was hardened against.

**Suggested fix:** Add the same two gates to `_maybe_recover_stale_bridge` before it calls `restart_self`: `if self._consolidate_active or self._popup_active_lock.locked(): return` (without setting `_bridge_restart_started`, so the recovery is deferred to the next media-tree tick rather than abandoned). Better still, factor the "is it safe to stand this process down" test out of `apply_upgrade` into one predicate both callers use, so a third caller of `restart_self` cannot miss it again.

**Verifier:** Verified at app.py:2338-2376. _maybe_recover_stale_bridge checks only bridge_auto_restart, the _bridge_restart_started latch, resolve_bridge.session_state() and resolve_prefs_mod.resolve_is_running(), then calls upgrade_mod.restart_self(request_shutdown=self.shutdown) at app.py:2374 -- the same spawn + shutdown sequence apply_upgrade gates at app.py:3032-3041 with `_popup_active_lock.locked()` / `_consolidate_active` and the AUDIT_2 CORE-H8/H5 comment. consolidate_project sets _consolidate_active at app.py:1862 and holds _popup_active_lock across the ProgressWindow for the whole copy + lane-A upload (app.py:1937-2012, with an explicit 'STILL UNDER THE POPUP LOCK, deliberately' note and a 'MULTI-HOUR' comment); neither flag is consulted by the recovery path. It runs from _refresh_media_tree_once (app.py:2175), which the media-tree loop (app.py:2493-2500) ticks every media_tree_refresh_interval regardless of whether Resolve is up, and the call is the FIRST statement, before any Resolve enumeration. Tests at companion/tests/test_app.py:4164-4223 pin only the four documented guards. My COMP-CORE-1 analysis reinforces the harm: process exit is ~1 s after shutdown() starts, so the consolidate daemon thread really is killed mid-copy.

### COMP-CORE-3 (major, bug) — The tray's 2 s refresh thread makes an unthrottled blocking HTTP selection fetch whenever the dashboard is unreachable
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:2682`

`_tray_snapshot` calls `app.removable_projects()` on every refresh tick (tray.py:1637; the loop period is `refresh_interval = 2.0`, tray.py:2040/2117). `removable_projects()` calls `self.selection_client.get()` (app.py:2682), which calls `SelectionClient.fetch()` -- a synchronous `urlopen` with `self.timeout = 5.0` (selection.py:140-187). The 30 s throttle at selection.py:152-160 is keyed on `self._last_response_at`, and that stamp is written **only on success** (selection.py:186). On any failure -- dashboard down, editor off the tailnet, 401 from a rotated token, or simply a machine that has never reached the dashboard -- `_last_response` stays None/stale and every single call falls straight through to a fresh 5 s blocking request. This directly contradicts `_tray_snapshot`'s own contract (tray.py:1586-1594): "gathered ONCE on the refresh thread... any of those stalling stalled menu construction", the rule that the Resolve getters at tray.py:1632-1634 are explicitly written to obey.

**Failure scenario:** An editor takes their laptop off the tailnet (or the NAS dashboard container is restarting). The tray refresh loop now runs: snapshot -> `removable_projects()` -> `fetch()` blocks 5 s on a connect timeout -> returns None -> `load_cached()` -> finish tick -> `time.sleep(2.0)` -> repeat. The icon colour, tooltip (speed/ETA/proxy count) and menu fingerprint therefore refresh every ~7 s instead of every 2 s for as long as the machine is offline -- exactly the state in which the editor is staring at the tray asking why nothing is syncing -- and the companion issues roughly 12,000 doomed 5-second HTTP attempts per day from the UI thread. A pulsing 'red' icon likewise lags its state change by up to 7 s.

**Suggested fix:** Stamp the throttle on the ATTEMPT, not just on success: set `self._last_response_at = time.monotonic()` in `fetch()`'s `except` arm too (or keep a separate `_last_attempt_at` and check it alongside `_last_response_at`), so a failing dashboard is retried at `fetch_ttl`, not on every caller. Additionally, `removable_projects()` is a pure tray-menu convenience -- it should read the sequencer's already-polled selection or a cached snapshot rather than being allowed to originate a network round trip on the refresh thread at all.

**Verifier:** Verified. selection.py:150-160 gates the TTL on `self._last_response is not None and (monotonic - self._last_response_at) < self.fetch_ttl`, and both _last_response and _last_response_at are assigned only on the success path (selection.py:184-186); the except arm (selection.py:174-182) returns None without stamping anything, and load_cached() never populates them either (only untick() zeroes the stamp, selection.py:312). removable_projects() (app.py:2669) calls selection_client.get() at app.py:2682, which calls fetch() unconditionally, and tray._tray_snapshot pulls it on every tick (tray.py:1637) with refresh_interval=2.0 (tray.py:2040/2117). So a failing dashboard means one urlopen attempt per tick with self.timeout=5.0 (selection.py:64). Two caveats that soften the write-up without disproving it: fetch() already short-circuits with no round trip when there is no editor identity (selection.py:161-168), so the 'never reached the dashboard' case only applies to a machine with a name/identity; and 5 s is the ceiling -- a no-route/refused connect fails fast, so the '~7 s per tick' figure is worst case, not typical. The snapshot also runs on the refresh thread, which is where stalls were deliberately moved to, so the tray message pump is not blocked -- the real cost is icon/tooltip lag plus the doomed request rate.

### COMP-CORE-4 (minor, bug) — resolved_local_root has none of the non-str guard its sibling resolved_log_path exists for, and its caller in start() is unguarded
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/config.py:1410`

`resolved_log_path` (config.py:1395-1407) is entirely a defence against a hand-edited non-string value: "Path(5) raises TypeError, and this is called from CompanionApp.__init__, _build_lanes AND setup_logging, i.e. three places where an exception means the windowed exe vanishes silently (AUDIT_2 CORE-H2)". Its sibling `resolved_local_root` (config.py:1410-1411) is a bare `Path(cfg.get("local_root", "")).expanduser()` with no type check and no try. `local_root = 5`, `local_root = true` and `local_root = ["D:/x"]` are all valid TOML, and `validate_config` only stringifies the value (config.py:1022) so it reports "local_root does not exist: 5" rather than a type error. `CompanionApp.__init__` survives -- `TimelineWatcher` and `RcloneLane` both store `local_root` as an opaque value -- so the crash lands later, in `start()`.

**Failure scenario:** An editor (or an installer templating bug) writes `local_root = 5` into ~/.ccsync/config.toml. Construction succeeds; `start()` runs its config-problems branch, starts the reporter, manifest cache, media-tree thread, watcher and proxy generator, then reaches `self._start_lut_link()` at app.py:3538 -- a bare call with no try -- whose first statement is `config_mod.resolved_local_root(self.config)` (app.py:3622). `Path(5)` raises TypeError, which escapes `start()` and `run()`'s inner try (that block catches only KeyboardInterrupt, app.py:4256), so `shutdown()` runs and the process dies before the tray icon is ever created. The editor gets no tray, no toast and no notification -- only a `log.exception("ccsync-companion crashed during startup/run")` line in a log file they have no tray menu item to open.

**Suggested fix:** Give `resolved_local_root` the same shape as `resolved_log_path`: return `Path("")` (or the raw string coerced via `str()`) when `cfg["local_root"]` is not a non-blank `str`, wrapped in a try that never raises. Add `local_root` to `validate_config`'s type check next to `log_path` (config.py:1067-1076) so the message names the real problem, and wrap `_start_lut_link()`/`_start_root_guard()`/`_start_broll_server()` at app.py:3536-3541 in the same per-feature try the calls above them use.

**Verifier:** Verified. config.py:1410-1411 is a bare `Path(cfg.get("local_root", "")).expanduser()` next to resolved_log_path's explicit non-str guard (config.py:1395-1407, AUDIT_2 CORE-H2). load_config() coerces only list fields (config.py:975-983), never local_root; validate_config stringifies it (config.py:1022) and its type check covers log_path only (config.py:1066-1076). Every other local_root read in app.py goes through str(...) (app.py:1096, 1130, 1178, 2057 etc.) or passes it opaquely into the lanes/watcher (app.py:940/999/1027), so construction and the early part of start() survive a non-str value; start()'s config_problems branch (app.py:3444-3450) logs and falls through, and _start_lut_link() at app.py:3538 is a bare call whose first statement, app.py:3622, is config_mod.resolved_local_root(self.config) -- outside its own try. Path(5) raises TypeError, run()'s inner try catches only KeyboardInterrupt (app.py:4256), so the process tears down before the tray icon exists. Low likelihood (needs a hand-edited or mis-templated non-string), but the crash path is real and is exactly the class resolved_log_path exists to prevent.

### COMP-CORE-5 (minor, inefficiency) — Every heavy report serialises its multi-megabyte payload to JSON twice
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/reporter.py:373`

`post_once` calls `_fit_payload(payload)` for every non-light tick (reporter.py:502), whose first statement is `size = payload_size(payload)` -> `json.dumps(payload).encode("utf-8")` over the whole body (reporter.py:107-114, 373). When the payload is already under budget -- the normal case -- that entire serialisation is discarded, and `default_http_post` immediately does `json.dumps(data).encode("utf-8")` again (reporter.py:118). On an over-budget payload `payload_size` is re-run after each shedding step, up to six full serialisations of a ~7 MB structure. The heavy payload carries `local_manifest` (up to 64 projects x 2 file lists x `MAX_PER_FILE_ENTRIES = 2000` entries, manifest.py:36/50) plus `media_tree` (up to 500 clips per project after trimming).

**Failure scenario:** The base rig, whose `local_root` IS the whole NAS tree and which therefore hits the 64-project cap, sends a heavy report every `dashboard_report_interval` (60 s). Each one builds a multi-MB JSON string, measures its length, throws it away, and builds the identical string again inside `default_http_post` -- roughly 2x CPU and 2x transient peak memory per report, ~2,880 redundant multi-megabyte serialisations a day, on the machine that is simultaneously running the proxy encoder and the b-roll indexer.

**Suggested fix:** Serialise once and thread the bytes through: have `_fit_payload` return `(payload, body_bytes)` (or a small `_Measured` holder) and pass the already-encoded body to `_http_post`, changing the `HttpPostFn` seam to accept pre-encoded bytes with a `dict` fallback for the injected test doubles. At minimum, skip the pre-check entirely for light ticks (already done) and reuse the final measurement as the request body instead of re-dumping.

**Verifier:** Verified: post_once calls self._fit_payload(payload) for every non-light tick (reporter.py:499-502), _fit_payload's first statement is `size = payload_size(payload)` (reporter.py:373) which is json.dumps(payload).encode() (reporter.py:107-113), and default_http_post re-does json.dumps(data).encode() (reporter.py:118). Under budget the first serialisation is pure waste; over budget payload_size is re-run after each shedding step. Caps that bound the size are real (manifest.py:35/49: MAX_PER_FILE_ENTRIES=2000, MAX_PROJECTS=64, with the base rig named as the machine that hits 64 first) and the interval is 60 s (config.py:179). Two qualifications: the payload is only 'multi-megabyte' when the manifest is actually large -- the budget is 7 MB (reporter.py:100) but nothing shows a typical body near it, so the 2x cost is proportional to whatever the real size is; and the suggested fix changes the injectable HttpPostFn seam that tests depend on, so it is a contract change, not a local edit.

### COMP-CORE-6 (minor, inefficiency) — The tray's 2 s poll drives a `net use P:` subprocess every 10 s forever, although the only in-process mutator already invalidates the cache
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:2598`

`p_mapping_mode()` memoises for 10 s (app.py:2608-2611) and then calls `drive_swap.current_p_target()` (app.py:2615-2616), which spawns `net use P:` and, when that yields nothing UNC-shaped, a second `subst` process (drive_swap.py:263-284). `_tray_snapshot` reads it on every refresh tick (tray.py:1649, period 2.0 s), so the TTL is guaranteed to expire and be re-populated forever, on every Windows machine in the fleet, whether or not anything could have changed. The cache exists for the watcher's per-mapping-warning calls, not for a fixed-rate poller. Crucially, the only thing in the companion that changes P: -- `swap_p_to_server` / `swap_p_to_local` -- already sets `self._p_mode_cache = None` explicitly (app.py:2653, 2665), so the TTL is buying nothing that the invalidation does not already provide.

**Failure scenario:** A Windows editor leaves the companion running for a working day. The tray refresh thread forces a cache miss every 10 s, so the process spawns ~8,640 `net use P:` invocations (plus a `subst` for every subst-mapped rig, i.e. up to ~17,000 processes) per day to re-derive a value that changed zero times. This is the same class of waste as the accepted MED-9 finding (BPG's CIM probe every 15 s), with a higher rate. It also means `_tray_snapshot` -- documented as the place where nothing may stall -- forks a process on one tick in five.

**Suggested fix:** Raise the TTL substantially (minutes, not 10 s) now that both mutators invalidate explicitly, or better: have the tray read a cached value only (`_p_mode_cache[1] if set else "none"`) and let a slow background refresh -- or the grade-swap action itself -- populate it, the same split `stray_lut_count()` and `proxy_gap()` already use for their expensive scans.

**Verifier:** Verified: p_mapping_mode (app.py:2598) memoises for 10.0 s (app.py:2608-2611) and otherwise calls drive_swap.current_p_target(), which runs `net use P:` and, when no UNC-shaped token comes back, a second `subst` (drive_swap.py:263-284). _tray_snapshot reads it unconditionally every tick (tray.py:1649, refresh_interval 2.0), so the TTL expires and re-populates forever on Windows; off Windows it returns 'none' with no subprocess (app.py:2601-2607). Both in-process mutators do clear the cache (app.py:2653, 2665), as claimed. Caveat on the suggested fix: P: can also change from outside this process (the installer's CCSync-SubstP logon task, a manual `net use`), so a multi-minute TTL trades staleness for the saving -- the tray-reads-cache-only variant is the safer of the two suggestions.

### COMP-CORE-7 (minor, logic) — A returning drive leaves the lanes stamped "your drive is disconnected" when the real blocker is the sign-in gate
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:1321`

When the sync drive is absent at startup, `start()` takes the `_root_absent` branch and calls `_mark_lanes_root_absent()` (app.py:3453-3463), which writes `_LANE_ROOT_ABSENT_DETAIL` ("PAUSED: your Creators Club drive is disconnected -- plug it back in and syncing resumes on its own") onto every lane. That branch deliberately takes precedence over the sign-in gate. When the drive comes back, `_on_root_present` -> `_root_resume_lanes` returns silently at app.py:1321-1322 if `config_problems` or `_login_gate_blocks_sync()` is true -- it never re-stamps the lane detail, unlike every other refusal path in this class (`_start_lanes` calls `_mark_lanes_misconfigured`/`_mark_lanes_root_absent`, `sign_out` and the identity watcher call `_mark_lanes_pending_login`).

**Failure scenario:** A macOS editor with `require_login = true` boots with their Creators Club SSD unplugged and does not sign in. All three lanes read "PAUSED: your Creators Club drive is disconnected". They plug the drive back in; the root guard clears `_root_absent`, `_root_resume_lanes` hits the login gate and returns. The tray tooltip now correctly says "CCSync: not signed in", but all three lane lines in the menu still say the drive is disconnected. The editor unplugs and replugs the drive, checks Finder, and never sees the one sentence that names the fix -- the same "names nothing they can fix" failure `_demote_removable_root_problem`'s docstring was written to eliminate.

**Suggested fix:** In `_root_resume_lanes`, before the early return, restamp the detail to match the actual blocker: `self._mark_lanes_misconfigured()` when `config_problems`, `self._mark_lanes_pending_login()` when `_login_gate_blocks_sync()`, and log which one held it down.

**Verifier:** Verified. start()'s elif chain puts _root_absent ahead of the login gate (app.py:3444-3470), so a boot with the drive out stamps _LANE_ROOT_ABSENT_DETAIL (app.py:554-557) via _mark_lanes_root_absent (app.py:1342-1348). On return, _on_root_present -> _root_resume_lanes (app.py:1314-1322) returns at `if self.config_problems or self._login_gate_blocks_sync(): return` without restamping, while every sibling path does restamp (_mark_lanes_misconfigured / _mark_lanes_root_absent / _mark_lanes_pending_login at app.py:2806-2812). Nothing else rewrites the detail: the lanes are never started, and the identity watcher only stamps on an expiry transition. One correction to the write-up's wording: with _root_absent now False the tray does not print the raw sentence -- _format_lane_line_from (tray.py:748-796) falls through to the last branch and renders 'Lane X: up to date (PAUSED: your Creators Club drive is disconnected -- plug it back in...)', which is self-contradictory as well as wrong, so the substance stands.

### COMP-CORE-8 (minor, inefficiency) — The diagnostics log tail reads the entire 5 MB rotating log into memory to keep 40 lines
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:3118`

`_diagnostic_log_tail` does `fh.readlines()[-lines:]` (app.py:3117-3118) on `self.log_path`, which `setup_logging` configures as a `RotatingFileHandler` with `maxBytes=5_000_000` (app.py:74-76). Every "Copy diagnostics" click therefore decodes and splits up to 5 MB of UTF-8 and allocates a list of tens of thousands of strings in order to keep the last 40. R15 fix 4 documents machines that rotate that 5 MB every ~25 minutes, i.e. the file is routinely at its ceiling. `build_diagnostics()` runs on the tray worker thread while `_popup_active_lock` is being contended for, and `copy_diagnostics` calls it before it even takes the lock (app.py:3278).

**Failure scenario:** An admin walks an editor through tray -> "Copy diagnostics" on a machine whose companion.log is at the 5 MB rotation ceiling. The click reads and splits the whole file to produce 40 lines, spiking memory by ~15-20 MB of Python string objects and adding a visible pause before the toast -- for a tail that `os.stat` + one `seek` to `size - 16384` would answer.

**Suggested fix:** Seek to the end minus a bounded window (e.g. 64 KB, enough for 40 lines of this formatter), read forward from there, drop the first partial line and take the last `lines` entries. Keep the existing `except Exception` fallback so an unreadable/rotating-mid-read file still returns the explanatory placeholder.

**Verifier:** Verified: _diagnostic_log_tail (app.py:3115-3120) does fh.readlines()[-lines:] on self.log_path, and setup_logging configures RotatingFileHandler(maxBytes=5_000_000, backupCount=3) (app.py:74-76); R15 fix 4 (KNOWN_BUGS.md:279-286) does document the 5 MB-per-25-minutes rotation, though that log-spam cause has since been fixed. The waste is real but strictly bounded to the one call site: grep shows _diagnostic_log_tail is used only by build_diagnostics (app.py:3222), which only copy_diagnostics (app.py:3278) and tests call -- i.e. once per manual 'Copy diagnostics' click, not on any loop. Reading and splitting 5 MB is tens of milliseconds, so the claimed 'visible pause' overstates it; treat this as a cheap cleanup, not a user-visible defect.

## COMP-GUARD — Companion guards & Resolve housekeeping

### COMP-GUARD-1 (critical, bug) — A hard kill mid-copy strands a 0-byte file under the FINAL name, and nothing ever sweeps it
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/fixer.py:459`

`_claim_destination_path` (fixer.py:430, called at :715) creates the final destination name up front with O_CREAT|O_EXCL, before the multi-GB copy starts. Two of the three exits clean it up (`CopyAborted` at :748 via `remove_reserved_name`, the generic `except` at :775), but the fourth exit — the process simply dying (self-upgrade shutdown, reboot, Quit, force-quit, power loss) — cleans up nothing. `sweep_stale_tmp_files` (:459) is the crash-recovery sweep and it only matches `TMP_SUFFIX` (`*.ccsync-tmp`); it has no knowledge of reservations at all. Confirmed there is no other caller anywhere in the repo that removes them (grep for `remove_reserved_name` finds only fix_clip and its test). The reservation is a real video-extension file: lane A's filter (`sync/rclone_lane.py:375`, `+ *{ext}` for VIDEO_EXTS) matches it, its mtime is creation time so `--min-age 120s` clears it, and lane A runs with `--ignore-existing` (rclone_lane.py:1049) so once the 0-byte version is on the NAS the real bytes can never replace it. Lane C fans the same 0-byte file out to the rest of the fleet, where the 2026-08-11 `ignoreDelete=true` retrofit makes it effectively immortal (KNOWN_BUGS R5/R15).

**Failure scenario:** Editor runs FIX ALL on `A001_C012.braw`; `B-roll/Editor Added/ruskin/A001_C012.braw` is created 0-byte and the copy starts writing `A001_C012.braw.<pid>-<uuid>.ccsync-tmp`. Ten minutes in, the one-click self-upgrade (or a reboot) kills the process. On restart the tmp sweep reports the partial and, correctly, does not delete it — but says nothing about the 0-byte `A001_C012.braw`. Two minutes later lane A uploads that 0-byte .braw to the NAS. The editor re-runs FIX ALL; `_claim_destination_path` sees the name taken and files the real copy as `A001_C012 (2).braw`, relinking Resolve to that. The project folder — locally, on the NAS, and on every other editor's machine — now permanently contains a 0-byte `A001_C012.braw` that `--ignore-existing` guarantees will never be overwritten.

**Suggested fix:** Give the reservation the same crash-recovery treatment as the tmp: in `sweep_stale_tmp_files`, for every stale `<name>.<pid>-<uuid>.ccsync-tmp` found, also check the adjacent `<name>` and report (and, since a 0-byte reservation is provably ours and provably not user data, offer to remove) it when it is 0 bytes and older than `max_age_seconds`. Alternatively make the reservation self-identifying — reserve `<stem>.ccsync-claim<ext>` and rename into place — so the crash residue is filtered out of every lane by suffix, the way the tmp already is.

**Verifier:** Verified at fixer.py:430 (_claim_destination_path, O_CREAT|O_EXCL on the FINAL name), called at fixer.py:715 before copy_fn. Only two exits clean the reservation: CopyAborted (:748, remove_reserved_name) and the generic except (:773-777, unlink when size==0). A hard process death leaves the 0-byte final name and nothing removes it: sweep_stale_tmp_files (fixer.py:459) matches only names ending in TMP_SUFFIX, and grep across companion/ shows remove_reserved_name has exactly one caller (fix_clip) plus its test (tests/test_fixer.py:1038). app._sweep_stale_tmp_files (app.py:3897) runs only that sweep plus proxy_gen.sweep_stale_partials — neither knows about reservations. The lane-A half of the scenario also checks out: build_filter_rules_up (rclone_lane.py:354-377) includes `+ *{ext}` for VIDEO_EXTS with no size filter, and the periodic up-copy is built with `--ignore-existing` and `--min-age LANE_A_MIN_AGE` (120s) at rclone_lane.py:1047-1057, so once the empty file is on the NAS the real bytes can never replace it. No min-size/zero-byte guard exists anywhere in sync/. Nothing in KNOWN_BUGS.md, docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md or CLAUDE.md documents this residue as accepted — and the code's own CopyAborted comment (fixer.py:740-747) states the exact hazard ("the tmp is filtered out of every lane, but the reservation is not"), which is why it is cleaned on that exit and simply is not on the kill path.

### COMP-GUARD-4 (major, logic) — RootGuard.stop() cannot stop an in-flight probe from firing on_present — which starts lanes in a process that is tearing down
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/root_guard.py:675`

`_loop` (:713) is `while True: probe_once(); if self._stop_event.wait(...): break` — the stop event is only observed BETWEEN probes. `probe_once` (:650) and `_fire` (:675) never consult `_stop_event`, so a probe already running when `stop()` is called still fires its transition callback. `stop()` (:707) joins for only `max(2.0, poll_seconds / 2)` = 2.5 s and then returns regardless. A probe can easily outlive that: on darwin, `find_volume_by_uuid` (:299) spawns `diskutil info -plist` per mounted volume with a 10 s timeout each (:85), so 3-4 mounted volumes is 30-40 s in one probe; on Windows a bare `os.path.isdir()` against a disconnected mapped/loopback drive can block for tens of seconds on the SMB timeout. app.py's own teardown comment at :3981-3983 states the requirement being violated verbatim: "The root guard goes with them: its callbacks START LANES, and one firing during teardown would leave a sequencer and an rclone child running in a process that is exiting." It hands `stop()` that job, and `stop()` cannot do it.

**Failure scenario:** macOS editor's T7 is out, so the guard's state is ROOT_ABSENT and every poll walks /Volumes running diskutil. The editor plugs the drive back in and immediately clicks tray -> Quit (or the one-click self-upgrade fires). `shutdown()` calls `root.stop()`; the guard is 15 s into a probe, so the 2.5 s join times out and teardown carries on stopping lanes. The probe then completes, sees ROOT_PRESENT, and `_fire` calls `app._on_root_present()` — which restarts the sequencer and lanes behind the teardown that just stopped them, leaving a live rclone child and a sequencer thread in a process that is exiting (and, on a self-upgrade, racing the new build's lanes).

**Suggested fix:** Gate the callbacks on the stop event: return early from `probe_once` (or from `_fire`) when `self._stop_event.is_set()`, and check the event again immediately before `self._fire(state)`. While there, do not clear `self._thread` in `stop()` when the join times out — follow `_WindowsShutdownGuard.stop`'s SYNC-15 precedent (shutdown_guard.py:649-658) and keep the reference, so a later `start()` (which clears `_stop_event`) cannot spawn a second poll thread alongside a first that never saw the set.

**Verifier:** Verified in root_guard.py: _loop (:713-719) observes _stop_event only between probes; probe_once (:650-673) and _fire (:675-686) never consult it; stop (:707-711) sets the event, joins for max(2.0, poll_seconds/2) = 2.5s at POLL_SECONDS=5.0, and returns regardless (also nulling self._thread unconditionally, so a later start() can spawn a second poller). A probe genuinely can outlive the join: on darwin the misplaced path calls find_volume_by_uuid (:299) -> volume_uuid per mounted volume with DISKUTIL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10.0 (:85), and the 30s cache does not help the first walk after a replug. The consequence is real: app.shutdown's own comment (app.py:3980-3983) states the requirement being violated, and the callback chain has no teardown guard — _on_root_present (app.py:1165-1198) calls _root_resume_lanes (app.py:1314-1340), which checks _paused, config_problems and the login gate but never _stop_event / _shutdown_started, and calls _start_lanes() plus sequencer.trigger_pass_now(). Nothing in KNOWN_BUGS.md or docs covers this.

### COMP-GUARD-5 (major, logic) — `_handle_non_canonical` runs N blocking Resolve API calls inline on the watcher poll thread — the CORE-M2 failure its sibling was fixed for
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:1484`

The watcher invokes `on_non_canonical` synchronously from `poll_once` (watcher.py:319-321). `_handle_non_canonical` then loops over every item calling `resolve_bridge.replace_clip`, each of which does `ui_state.wait_while_menu_open()` and takes `_API_LOCK` for a real ReplaceClip plus a GetClipProperty round trip. Its sibling handler `_handle_out_of_tree` was explicitly fixed for exactly this, and carries the comment at app.py:1420-1426: "Calling it inline froze the WATCHER thread for as long as the dialog stayed on screen: last_resolve_project stopped updating (the dashboard reported a project already closed), no further out-of-tree detection happened, and _stop_event went unobserved so shutdown/self-upgrade couldn't stop the watcher cleanly (AUDIT_2 CORE-M2)." It hands the work to a `ccsync-popup` daemon thread. `_handle_non_canonical`, added 2026-08-12, does not — and unlike a popup it is unbounded in count: the batch is every newly-seen NON_CANONICAL clip on the timeline, which in the incident that motivated it was 158 clips in one pass.

**Failure scenario:** An editor opens the shared "Energy Transition" project whose 158 clips are stored under `F:\Creators_Club\...`. The first `poll_once` classifies all 158 as NON_CANONICAL and calls `_handle_non_canonical` inline. At even ~0.5 s per ReplaceClip on a project of that size, the watcher thread is parked for 80+ seconds: `last_resolve_project` stops updating so the dashboard reports the wrong open project, no out-of-tree detection happens, and `run()`'s `stop_event` goes unobserved — so a Quit or a one-click self-upgrade landing in that window cannot stop the watcher cleanly, which is the R11 hand-off hazard.

**Suggested fix:** Do what `_handle_out_of_tree` does: dispatch the relink batch to a named daemon thread (`ccsync-canon-relink`), guarded by a single-flight flag so overlapping polls don't stack batches. That also removes the reason `tries=1` exists (COMP-GUARD-2), letting the relinks use `replace_clip`'s verify-with-retry path.

**Verifier:** Verified: the watcher calls on_non_canonical synchronously at the end of poll_once (watcher.py:325-329), on the thread running run()'s loop (watcher.py:381-389), and _handle_non_canonical (app.py:1484-1523) loops inline over the whole batch calling resolve_bridge.replace_clip, which does ui_state.wait_while_menu_open() (capped at 8s per call, ui_state.py:25-36) and takes _API_LOCK for ReplaceClip + GetClipProperty per attempt (resolve_bridge.py:869-892). The sibling _handle_out_of_tree does exactly the opposite for exactly this reason, with the CORE-M2 comment at app.py:1419-1427 and a `ccsync-popup` daemon thread. Batch size is unbounded (every newly-seen NON_CANONICAL clip on the timeline; the media-pool sibling's own docstring cites 200+ from Energy Transition, and _handle_foreign_batch was added precisely because that backlog arrives in one pass). No test, comment or doc records the inline call as deliberate — the docstring only justifies the absence of a POPUP. Caveat on severity: unlike the Tk mainloop, this loop is bounded by the batch and each call is normally fast, so the stall is seconds-to-minutes rather than open-ended.

### COMP-GUARD-6 (minor, logic) — stray_luts' library-containment test is a bare startswith, so a sibling directory whose name extends the library path suppresses every stray
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/luts.py:291`

`if _resolved(path).startswith(library_resolved): continue` compares normcased path strings with no separator boundary. This is the exact trap `canon._is_under` documents and guards against (canon.py:119-122: "The separator matters: 'P:\\Creators_ClubExtra' is not under 'P:\\Creators_Club'"). Here the library is `<local_root>/Assets/Luts`, so any search directory rooted at a path that merely begins with that string — `Assets/Luts Backup`, `Assets/Luts_local`, `Assets/Lutspack` — has every one of its files silently classified as "already inside the library" and dropped, with no log line. The paired guard on the directory itself (`_resolved(base) == library_resolved`, :282) is an exact-equality test and correctly does not fire, so the loop runs and then discards everything.

**Failure scenario:** An editor sets `resolve_lut_dir = "P:\\Assets\\Luts Local"` (or has Resolve's Open LUT Folder pointed there) and drops in three new .cube files. `_resolved(path)` = `p:\assets\luts local\gr film.cube`, which startswith `p:\assets\luts` — so all three are skipped. `find_strays()` returns [], `stray_lut_count()` stays 0, the tray never renders the "N LUTs only on this machine — share with the team" item, and the LUTs are never offered to the shared library.

**Suggested fix:** Use the boundary-aware helper this repo already has: replace the startswith with `canon._is_under`-style logic (equal, or startswith `library_resolved + os.sep`), or call `os.path.commonpath` the way `fixer._dest_dir_is_contained` does.

**Verifier:** The defect is exactly as described at luts.py:291: `if _resolved(path).startswith(library_resolved): continue` compares normcased strings with no separator boundary, while the directory-level guard at :282 is exact equality (`_resolved(base) == library_resolved`) and so does not fire for a sibling — meaning the rglob runs and then silently discards every file, with no log line. canon._is_under (canon.py:119-122) documents this exact trap and fixer._dest_dir_is_contained (fixer.py:707-733) uses os.path.commonpath correctly, so the repo has the right pattern. Reachability caveat the finding does not state: search_dirs() (luts.py:411-417) returns the configured `resolve_lut_dir` if set, else _default_resolve_lut_dir() — C:\ProgramData\Blackmagic Design\... on Windows, /Library/Application Support/... on macOS — neither of which can ever prefix-match the library (<local_root>/Assets/Luts). So the bug is latent: it only bites a machine whose `resolve_lut_dir` is set to a path that string-extends the library path. Real defect, narrow trigger — consistent with the 'minor' severity claimed.

### COMP-GUARD-7 (minor, bug) — current_p_target truncates a UNC containing a space, so a grade-swapped P: classifies as "other" and the mapping-warning suppression fails
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/drive_swap.py:269`

`current_p_target` extracts the mapping with `re.search(r"(\\\\\\\\\\S+)", proc.stdout)` — `\S+` stops at the first whitespace, so a share path with a space comes back truncated. `classify_p_target` (:287) then compares that truncated string for exact equality against `server_unc` (:297) and returns "other". Note that `paths._local_share_target` (paths.py:144) solves the identical parsing problem correctly with a trailing-anchored regex that tolerates internal spaces, so the fix pattern already exists in the codebase. The consequence is not cosmetic: `app._handle_mapping_warning` suppresses BAD_PREFIX toasts only when `p_mapping_mode() == "server"` (app.py:1466), and the watcher classifies every canonical clip as BAD_PREFIX while P: points at the server tree.

**Failure scenario:** An admin sets `server_p_unc = "\\\\100.65.15.123\\TheCreatorsPool\\Creator Profiles"` (or any share path with a space — project and asset folder names in this tree routinely have them). The editor grade-swaps P: to the server; the swap itself succeeds via WNetAddConnection2W. `net use P:` reports `\\100.65.15.123\TheCreatorsPool\Creator Profiles`, the regex returns `\\100.65.15.123\TheCreatorsPool\Creator`, `classify_p_target` answers "other", and the editor is now told "Resolve is looking for media on P:\ but that path doesn't land in your sync folder... Nothing will sync until this is fixed" — once per distinct clip path — for a state they deliberately chose. The tray's swap-state rendering is wrong for the same reason.

**Suggested fix:** Anchor the UNC capture to end-of-field rather than to whitespace, e.g. `re.search(r"(\\\\\\\\\\S.*?\\S)\\s*$", line)` per line (the shape `paths._local_share_target` uses), and strip trailing whitespace only.

**Verifier:** The parsing defect is real: drive_swap.current_p_target (:263-273) does re-search(r"(\\\\\S+)", proc.stdout) over the whole `net use P:` output, so \S+ stops at the first space and a UNC containing one comes back truncated; classify_p_target (:287-299) then does an exact normcase comparison against server_unc and answers "other"; app.p_mapping_mode (:2598-2623) has no persisted swap flag to fall back on (it re-derives from this parse, cached 10s), and _handle_mapping_warning (app.py:1465-1470) suppresses the BAD_PREFIX toast only on "server". paths._local_share_target (paths.py:124-151) does show the correct trailing-anchored pattern. Two corrections to the finding's likelihood argument: (a) the subst fallback branch (:275-283) already tolerates spaces, so only the UNC branch is affected; (b) the UNC in play is the TREE ROOT, not a project folder — derive_server_unc (drive_swap.py:462-483) builds it from dashboard_url's host plus remote_root beyond /mnt/<pool>/, and the shipped config is remote_root = "/mnt/tank/TheCreatorsPool/Creators_Club" (config.example.toml:55) → \\host\TheCreatorsPool\Creators_Club, no space. So this only bites a deployment whose server_p_unc/remote_root contains a space; the "project and asset folder names routinely have spaces" justification does not apply. Real latent bug, narrower than described.

### COMP-GUARD-8 (minor, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — Path canon is enforced reactively per-clip after import; Resolve's own MappedRoot could make a non-canonical import impossible on Windows
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_prefs.py:422`

GOAL: every clip a Windows editor imports must be STORED in the project database as `P:\...`. CURRENT APPROACH: let the import happen however the editor browsed, then detect and repair it afterwards — `paths.classify_path` returns NON_CANONICAL, the 3 s watcher poll (or the 120 s media-tree sweep) picks it up, and `app._handle_non_canonical` issues a ReplaceClip per clip. CONCRETE COST: the repair path is lossy in three independent ways this review already documents — one un-retried attempt (COMP-GUARD-2), a once-per-process latch with no re-arm (watcher.py:255-257), and an inline loop that stalls the poll thread (COMP-GUARD-5) — plus it cannot run at all on a machine that is off, which is how the R12 incident accumulated 200+ broken clips and needed a hand-written relink script. BETTER APPROACH: Resolve's media-storage `MappedRoot` makes the canonical spelling the one Resolve WRITES, so there is nothing to detect or repair. The machinery is already in this file — `ensure_media_storage(root, mapped_root)` at :422 writes `Site.1.FS.<n>.MappedRoot` and `IoFsMappedMount_<n>` in both preference files, honouring Resolve's trailing-entry rule — and `installer/macos_bootstrap.sh` already uses exactly this for the whole tree on Mac. On Windows it is used only by `stills.py`, which deliberately passes an empty mapped_root (stills.py:64-68, "on Windows the drive letter IS the map"). That premise holds for imports made THROUGH P:, but R12's Windows half was 47 clips imported via `W:\Creators_Club\...` and strays on `Z:`, `I:` and a Desktop — browsed around P:, which is exactly what a MappedRoot on the local tree root would have caught at write time.

**Failure scenario:** Today: an editor drags media in from `F:\Creators_Club\Projects\Energy Transition\...` (the same tree, local spelling). Resolve stores `F:\...`; the clip plays perfectly here and is offline for everyone else. Repair depends on the companion being up, the watcher reaching that clip, one ReplaceClip landing on the first try, and the process not restarting first — and when any of those misses, the clip is silently stranded (R12, 200+ clips, discovered only by an audit). With `ensure_media_storage(root="F:\\Creators_Club", mapped_root="P:\\Creators_Club")` asserted at install/startup, Resolve itself writes `P:\Projects\Energy Transition\...` for that same drag, and the whole NON_CANONICAL class stops being producible on that machine.

**Suggested fix:** Extend the LUT/stills preference reconcile to also assert a media-storage entry for the editor's real `local_root` with `mapped_root = canonical_prefix` on Windows (not just macOS), reusing `ensure_media_storage` unchanged, behind the same Resolve-must-be-quit gate. Prove it first on the base rig against the four-point live check the proxy work already owes: import from the local spelling and confirm the stored File Path comes back canonical. Keep the reactive relink as the safety net for pre-existing projects and for foreign paths — but it stops being the primary mechanism.

**Verifier:** I could not establish that the proposed mechanism actually works, which is what an 'upgrade' verdict turns on. The premise is that a Windows media-storage MappedRoot makes Resolve WRITE the canonical spelling — but this repo has never established that even for the macOS analogue it cites: docs/GOTCHAS.md:460-465 says the whole macOS section is "code-complete and unvalidated", and docs/macos-first-run-2026-08-04.md:210-220 lists it as OPEN question B2 ("If Resolve hands back Mapped-Mount-resolved local paths... there is no bug. If it hands back the stored P:\ strings — which is what SPEC.md's path-canon design expects..."), i.e. even the read direction is unanswered; the mapping helper had not been run in write mode on the one live Mac (:335-360), which also found the two preference files disagreeing in a way the helper's assumptions get backwards. The finding concedes as much ("prove it first on the base rig"). Other things a viability check must clear that the finding does not address: (1) SPEC.md:36 makes the P: drive letter the sanctioned Windows canon mechanism and stills.desired_entry (stills.py:62-72) deliberately passes an empty mapped_root on Windows; (2) the largest R12 class was scripted imports, already fixed at source by canonicalize-at-import (resolve_bridge._canonicalize_imported, youtube_import/b-roll/music worker per KNOWN_BUGS R12) — a MappedRoot would not obviously change what a scripted AddItemsToMediaPool(path) stores; (3) R12's Z:\, I:\ and Desktop strays are OUT_OF_TREE and untouched by any MappedRoot; (4) ensure_media_storage (resolve_prefs.py:422-497) renumbers entries, and GALLERY_FS_KEY is an index into that list (resolve_prefs.py:66-71, set_gallery_filesys :498-515), so inserting an entry has a stills-location interaction the proposal does not account for. The cited costs (COMP-GUARD-2's latch, COMP-GUARD-5's inline loop) are also partly overstated — see those verdicts.

## SYNC — Companion sync lanes

### SYNC-1 (critical, bug) — Lane B is latched off forever after any stop/start cycle in managed mode
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:2103`

`RcloneLane._stop_event` is only ever cleared by `start()` (which installs a fresh Event) or, for lane A only, by `start_watchdog_only()`. In managed mode `app._start_lanes()` (app.py:2873-2896) starts lane C, the sequencer, and `self._lane_a.start_watchdog_only()` — it never touches lane B, because lane B is driven purely by `sequencer.run_once(subpath)`. But `app._stop_lanes()` DOES call `self._lane_b.stop()` (app.py:2938-2939, added by the 2026-08-11 SYNC-1 fix). So once `_stop_lanes()` has run, `lane_b._stop_event` stays set for the life of the process, and every subsequent `run_once()` hits the guard at rclone_lane.py:2103 (`if self._stop_event.is_set(): log.debug(...); return self.status()`) and returns the STALE LaneStatus without spawning rclone. Nothing logs above DEBUG. `consolidate`'s `self._lane_b.run_once(subpath)` (app.py:2115) is dead the same way. The `test_start_after_a_timed_out_stop_starts_a_fresh_generation` / `test_slow_run_then_stop_then_start_resumes_syncing` tests only cover the legacy `start()` path, so nothing catches this.

**Failure scenario:** Managed editor, require_login=true. Editor clicks tray → Sign out (app.sign_out → _stop_lanes → lane_b.stop() sets _stop_event), then Sign in again (on_signed_in → _start_lanes, managed branch). The sequencer restarts and calls lane_b.run_once('Projects/2026/FF5/Energy Transition') every rotation; every call returns at line 2103 having spawned nothing. No proxy is ever downloaded again until the companion is restarted, and lane B's dashboard/tray status keeps reporting whatever it last held (typically `idle — transferred N file(s)`), so the fleet grid shows the lane green. The same happens on a token expiry (app.py:3948-3954) followed by a re-sign-in.

**Suggested fix:** Give lane B a managed-mode re-arm symmetric to lane A's: either add an `RcloneLane.arm()` (install a fresh cleared `_stop_event` when no periodic thread is alive) and call it for lane B in `_start_lanes()`'s managed branch, or have `start_watchdog_only()`'s event-reset logic be a direction-independent helper both lanes call. Add a test that drives `_stop_lanes()` → `_start_lanes()` in managed mode and asserts `lane_b.run_once()` actually spawns.

**Verifier:** Real. rclone_lane.py:2103 returns self.status() unchanged whenever _stop_event is set, and the only two clearers are start() (rclone_lane.py:1864-1866, installs a fresh Event) and start_watchdog_only() (rclone_lane.py:1875-1899), which returns immediately for DIRECTION_DOWN ('if self.direction != DIRECTION_UP or self._observer is not None: return'). app.py:2872-2887 (managed branch of _start_lanes) starts lane C, the sequencer and _lane_a.start_watchdog_only() only; app.py:2934-2941 (_stop_lanes, managed) does call _lane_b.stop() with the SYNC-1 (2026-08-11) comment. So after any managed stop/start cycle lane B is latched off: sequencer._run_lane -> lane.run_once(subpath) (sequencer.py:1011/1038-1040) returns the stale LaneStatus at DEBUG. Live entry points exist: sign_out (app.py:2983-2995) then on_signed_in -> _start_lanes, and the identity-expiry watcher (app.py:3948-3956) then on_signed_in; require_login defaults True (config.py:178). Note the asymmetry is explicit elsewhere — lane A re-arms its express latch in _start_watchdog ('Re-arm express for this generation (stop() latched it off)', rclone_lane.py:2581-2582) and gets a fresh _stop_event in start_watchdog_only; lane B has neither. Tray Pause in managed mode goes through sequencer.pause(), not _stop_lanes (app.py:1298-1305, 3415-3434), so pause/resume does NOT trigger it — sign-out/re-sign-in and token expiry do.

### SYNC-3 (major, logic) — The express in-flight exclusion only covers one of the two orderings, so the duplicate-upload race it was written for is still the common case
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:1803`

`_build_command` snapshots `express_inflight_paths()` once, when it writes the filter file, and rclone reads `--filter-from` exactly once at startup. `_express_run` claims its paths before spawning (line 3112-3115), which closes the ordering the comment names — 'a periodic pass that builds its filter file in the gap between spawn and claim'. The opposite (and far larger) window is open: if the periodic lane A run for a project is already in flight when express claims a path, that path is not in the already-parsed filter file, and the periodic rclone will upload it when its walk reaches that directory. The periodic run lasts up to `project_rotation_seconds` (600 s default) while an express window is `watch_debounce_seconds` (10 s) plus the 120 s stability/min-age gate — so express almost always starts DURING a periodic run, not before it. Both runs are `copy --ignore-existing` with disjoint `--partial-suffix`, so the result is not corruption, but it is exactly the measured 2026-08-02 symptom the exclusion exists to remove: the same .mov climbing on two concurrent SFTP connections and splitting a saturated uplink.

**Failure scenario:** Sequencer starts lane A for `Projects/2026/FF5/Energy` at T=0; the run will take ~8 min. At T=140 s the editor's card ingest finishes a 12 GB `A001.braw`; the express window closes at T≈150 s, `_express_run` claims `Projects/2026/FF5/Energy/B-roll/A001.braw` and spawns its own rclone. The periodic run's filter file (written at T=0) does not exclude it; at T≈300 s the periodic walk reaches `B-roll/`, sees the destination still lacks the file (its traverse listing predates the express upload's completion), and uploads the same 12 GB in parallel with express — double the bytes on the editor's uplink.

**Suggested fix:** Track the periodic run's in-flight scope (subpath + start time) under `_proc_lock`, and have `_express_partition` defer any path under a covering in-flight periodic subpath (the periodic pass covers it anyway, so nothing is lost). Alternatively, let express reuse `_run_lock` in `try_acquire` form for paths inside an active periodic subpath. Pin whichever with a test that starts the periodic filter build first and then claims.

**Verifier:** The one-directional nature is real. _build_command snapshots express_inflight_paths() while writing the filter file (rclone_lane.py:1789-1812) and rclone parses --filter-from once at spawn, so a claim made by _express_run AFTER the periodic child started (claim at rclone_lane.py:3110-3117) cannot exclude anything from the run already in flight; the comment at 1795-1801 only reasons about the reverse ordering. Nothing else serialises the two: _express_run_lock is deliberately NOT _run_lock (rclone_lane.py:1686-1691, 'Two concurrent rclones on the same tree are safe here *because* both are upload-only copy --ignore-existing'), the sequencer never pauses express around lane A, and both use disjoint --partial-suffix (build_express_command, rclone_lane.py:1208) so the in-progress express file is invisible to the periodic run's --ignore-existing check. Windows line up as claimed: project_rotation_seconds=600 vs express_debounce_seconds=10 (config.py:145, 204). Impact is bandwidth only (the module already accepts concurrency as safe), so 'major' is generous, but the gap the exclusion was written to close is genuinely still open in the common ordering.

### SYNC-5 (minor, logic) — stop() mid-transfer paints the lane red; there is no stand-down path for a terminated child
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:2256`

`stop()` joins the periodic thread with a 5 s timeout and then calls `_kill_running_process()`, which `terminate()`s the live rclone. The run thread is still blocked in `proc.wait()` inside `_run_popen`; it returns the terminated exit code, and `_run_once_locked` falls through to `elif returncode != 0:` (line 2256) and sets `STATE_ERROR` with `last_error` = whatever rclone managed to log (often just `rclone exited 1`). `SpawnCancelled`/`_stand_down_status()` only covers the pre-spawn cancellation, and there is no `_stop_event` check on this branch — so a deliberate stand-down is reported as a lane failure. This compounds SYNC-1: for lane B in managed mode the ERROR is the last status the lane will ever publish, because the guard at line 2103 makes every later `run_once` return `self.status()` unchanged.

**Failure scenario:** Managed editor mid-way through a lane B proxy pull clicks tray → Sign out. `_stop_lanes()` → `lane_b.stop()` terminates rclone; the sequencer's worker thread finishes `_run_once_locked`, sees returncode != 0, and sets `lane_b_proxy_down = error: rclone exited 1`. The dashboard fleet strip shows that editor's lane B red, and (per SYNC-1) it stays red after they sign back in even though nothing is wrong.

**Suggested fix:** On the non-zero branch, check `self._stop_event.is_set()` first and return `_stand_down_status()` instead of STATE_ERROR — the same reasoning the SpawnCancelled path already documents ('a lane that was told to stop did not fail').

**Verifier:** Real. The non-zero branch at rclone_lane.py:2256-2261 sets STATE_ERROR with no _stop_event check; the only stand-down path is SpawnCancelled -> _stand_down_status (rclone_lane.py:2175/2189, 2359-2373), which by its own comment covers only pre-spawn cancellation ('stopped before this pass started'). stop() (rclone_lane.py:1901-1915) joins with timeout=5 and then calls _kill_running_process -> _terminate_child, while the run thread is still in proc.wait() inside _run_popen, so it returns rclone's terminated exit code and falls straight through the RCLONE_EXIT_MAX_DURATION and _is_max_delete_abort special cases into STATE_ERROR. The stated design intent is on the other side of the argument ('a lane that was told to stop did not fail', 2361-2363). Visibility caveat: after sign_out the reporter stops posting, so the red status surfaces on the tray immediately and on the dashboard after the next sign-in — and, per SYNC-1, for lane B it then never changes again.

### SYNC-6 (minor, inefficiency) — _reassert_folder_policy rewrites .stignore and re-fetches the folder config every turn, even when nothing is missing
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/sequencer.py:1264`

`_reassert_folder_policy` POSTs `/rest/db/ignores` with the full `STIGNORE_LINES` unconditionally on every project's lane C turn (line 1264), then calls `ensure_versioning(slug)` (1272) and `ensure_ignore_delete(slug)` (1281) with no `folder` argument — so each of those does its own `GET /rest/config/folders/<id>`. That is one config-write-timeout POST plus two redundant GETs per project per pass, forever, on every editor. The sibling code for the shared asset folders does the opposite and says why: `SharedFolderManager._ensure_ignores` reads first and writes only when a line is actually missing ('steady state costs one GET and no config write'), and `_reconcile_one` passes the already-fetched `folder` into both `ensure_*` calls. The sequencer already has `admin.get_ignores` wired (it uses it in `_verify_startup_ignores`) and `missing_ignore_lines` to compare with, so the cheap shape is available and unused.

**Failure scenario:** Editor with 6 ticked projects and a 60 s idle interval: every pass issues 6 unconditional `POST /rest/db/ignores` (each rewriting the folder's .stignore on disk and forcing Syncthing to re-evaluate its ignore matcher) plus 12 `GET /rest/config/folders/<id>` that could be 6. Steady state, in which no folder is missing a single pattern, costs ~2,600 pointless .stignore rewrites and ~5,200 redundant config GETs a day per editor.

**Suggested fix:** In `_reassert_folder_policy`, `get_ignores(slug)` + `missing_ignore_lines(...)` first and only `set_ignores` when the list is non-empty (treat a failed GET as 'missing', keeping the fail-closed posture). Fetch the folder once with `admin.get_folder(slug)` and pass it into both `ensure_versioning(slug, folder)` and `ensure_ignore_delete(slug, folder)`, exactly as `SharedFolderManager._reconcile_one` does.

**Verifier:** Mechanics check out. _reassert_folder_policy (sequencer.py:1240-1295) calls admin.set_ignores(slug, STIGNORE_LINES) unconditionally — syncthing_admin.py:470-477 is a bare POST on config_write_timeout with no read-first — then ensure_versioning(slug) and ensure_ignore_delete(slug) with no folder argument, and each of those does its own get_folder() when folder is None (syncthing_admin.py:348-349, 370-371). It runs once per project per pass (_lane_c_turn, sequencer.py:1183). The cheaper shape is already proven in-tree: SharedFolderManager._ensure_ignores reads first and writes only on a missing line ('steady state costs one GET and no config write', shared_folders.py:167-193), and sequencer.py already imports missing_ignore_lines and uses admin.get_ignores in _verify_startup_ignores (sequencer.py:37, 653). Two caveats on the write-up: the unconditional re-assert is a deliberate fail-closed retry path (the B5 rationale in the docstring and ignores_confirmed's 'the sequencer re-asserts the ignores once per turn anyway', syncthing_admin.py:479-486) — a read-first version must keep the 'GET failed = unconfirmed' posture; and the ~2,600 rewrites/day figure is inflated, since a pass with 6 projects includes _wait_for_folder_sync per project and cannot recur every 60 s.

### SYNC-7 (minor, inefficiency) — _unpause_all sweeps the whole selection with a config PATCH per project at the end of every pass
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/sequencer.py:495`

`_run` calls `self._unpause_all(self._last_selection)` after every pass (line 495), and `_unpause_all` issues `_set_paused(slug, False)` — a `PATCH /rest/config/folders/<id>` on the 30 s `config_write_timeout` — for every valid selection item, unconditionally. Under the default `lane_c_pause_scheme = "none"` the sequencer never pauses anything, so all of those writes are no-ops by construction. The same blind sweep also runs from `_startup_unpause` (which `_park_paused` re-runs on every tray Resume), `pause()` and `stop()`. This directly contradicts the design of its sibling `_release_paused_folders`, which was deliberately scoped to `_paused_by_us` 'so in steady state it costs zero config writes rather than one per project per project' — and `_paused_by_us` is exactly the record needed to scope this one too.

**Failure scenario:** Editor with 6 ticked projects, `lane_c_pause_scheme` at its default 'none' and `sequencer_idle_seconds=60`: each pass ends with 6 config-mutating PATCHes to the local Syncthing, each taking its config lock, for folders the sequencer never paused. ~8,600 needless config writes a day per editor, in a module whose own comments cite config-commit churn (~1000/hour) as the reason the rotate scheme was retired.

**Suggested fix:** Scope the between-passes sweep to `self._paused_by_us` (i.e. reuse `_release_paused_folders`) and keep the full-selection sweep only for the genuine leak-recovery entry points (`_startup_unpause`, `stop()`), where a previous process's paused folders are not in `_paused_by_us`.

**Verifier:** Factually accurate: _run calls _unpause_all(self._last_selection) at sequencer.py:495 after every pass, _unpause_all (sequencer.py:782-808) calls _set_paused(slug, False) for every valid, ignores-confirmed item, and set_folder_paused is an unconditional PATCH /rest/config/folders/<id> on config_write_timeout (syncthing_admin.py:326-329) with no read-first or state check. With the default lane_c_pause_scheme='none' (sequencer.py:205, PAUSE_SCHEME_NONE) nothing was paused, so those writes change nothing, and the scoped sibling _release_paused_folders exists two functions below with the exact rationale quoted (sequencer.py:811-825). Two qualifications: the sweep is not purely pointless — it is the only in-run release for a folder paused by something other than this sequencer (accept_folder creates folders paused, syncthing_admin.py:390-400, and such a folder is not in _paused_by_us), so the suggested scoping to _paused_by_us loses that coverage; a state check (skip the PATCH when the folder is already unpaused) preserves both. The ~8,600 writes/day estimate is also inflated for the reason given in SYNC-6.

### SYNC-8 (minor, inefficiency) — Every Syncthing REST call re-parses both config.xml files from disk to rebuild the API-key candidate list
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/syncthing_admin.py:266`

`_api_key_candidates()` (syncthing_admin.py:259-271, duplicated in syncthing_lane.py:230-242) short-circuits only when `syncthing_api_key` is configured — and config.py:150 / config.example.toml ship it as `""`, so on every real editor the fallback path runs. Even when `_active_api_key` is already known and will be accepted on the first attempt, the loop still calls `read_api_key_from_config(path)` for each of `default_api_key_paths()`, i.e. an `ET.parse()` of up to two config.xml files, on EVERY request. `_request`/`_get` call it once per HTTP call, and the callers are hot: `SyncthingLane._poll_loop` makes roughly `3 + 2N` requests every 15 s, and `Sequencer._wait_for_folder_sync` polls `folder_status` every 5 s for up to a 600 s turn.

**Failure scenario:** Editor with 6 ticked projects: lane C's 15 s poll alone is ~15 requests → 30 config.xml parses every 15 s, and a single 600 s lane C turn adds 120 `folder_status` calls → 240 more parses. Order 150-200k XML parses and file opens per machine per day to re-learn a key that has not changed since boot.

**Suggested fix:** Try `self._active_api_key` alone first and only build the full candidate list (re-parsing the config.xml files) after a 401/403; alternatively memoise `read_api_key_from_config` per path on mtime. Apply the same change to `SyncthingLane._api_key_candidates`, which is a byte-for-byte copy.

**Verifier:** Real and duplicated as described. _api_key_candidates (syncthing_admin.py:259-271) short-circuits only on self._configured_api_key, which comes from cfg['syncthing_api_key'] (app.py:726-729) and defaults to "" (config.py:150); otherwise it loops default_api_key_paths() calling read_api_key_from_config(path) — an ET.parse of each config.xml (syncthing_lane.py:123-158, up to two paths) — on EVERY _request/_get, even when _active_api_key is first in the list and will succeed. _request calls it once per HTTP call (syncthing_admin.py:274-292), and syncthing_lane.py:230-242 is a byte-for-byte copy on the lane C poll path. Note config.xml on a rig with many folders is not a small file, so the cost is per-request XML parsing of the whole Syncthing config. The suggested memoise-on-mtime / expand-only-on-401 fix does not disturb the documented reason the multi-home fallback exists (syncthing_lane.py:123-134), since the fallback still runs on 401/403.

## COMP-MEDIA — Companion Resolve bridge & proxy pipeline

### COMP-MEDIA-1 (critical, bug) — Companion proxies never normalize drop-frame timecode — the exact R10 refusal, unfixed on the fleet's own encoder
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ffmpeg_tools.py:527`

R10 established that Sony rtmd tags print the COLON (non-drop) form for drop-frame material and that at 29.97/59.94 that reading is a different absolute frame, so LinkProxyMedia refuses the proxy; the fix (`dropframe_normalized`, broll/indexer/broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py:102, applied in build_proxy at :240 and in fix_proxy_timecode.py:92) landed only on the b-roll indexer side. The companion's rehomed copy of ffmpeg_tools has no equivalent: `_timecode_from` (:302) returns the tag verbatim, `probe_video` (:410) hands it through, and `proxy_gen._build_cmd` (proxy_gen.py:1326) passes it straight to `own_proxy_cmd`, which writes it with `-timecode` unchanged. probe_video already computes `fps` (:398), so every input the normalizer needs is on hand and unused. This is the module that makes the fleet's EDITING proxies — the ones the carryover ship-blocker ("Proxy generator, live-attach proof") is about — so the whole own-footage tier is affected, not the archive previews R10 swept.

**Failure scenario:** An FX3/FX6 clip shot at 59.94 drop-frame lands in a project. probe_video reads `format.tags.timecode` (or the tmcd stream) as "03:40:27:12" — the colon form Sony writes even for DF. own_proxy_cmd emits `-timecode 03:40:27:12`, so the proxy claims a non-drop start while the original counts drop-frame. Resolve's adjacent-Proxy auto-link and every LinkProxyMedia call (proxy_relink.apply_relinks, resolve_bridge._attach_adjacent_proxy) then REFUSE the pairing — proven live 2026-08-12, where remuxing the identical bytes with the semicolon form flipped the same link to accepted. The proxy sits beside the original, is synced to every editor by lane B, and is never used by anyone.

**Suggested fix:** Port `dropframe_normalized(tc, fps)` from broll/indexer/broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py (NTSC_DF_RATES 29.97/59.94, colon→semicolon on the last separator, pass-through when the tag already has a `;`) into companion/src/ccsync_companion/ffmpeg_tools.py and apply it in `probe_video` before returning `timecode` (fps is right there), so every caller — own_proxy_cmd and any future one — gets the normalized form. Add the indexer's regression test (test_ffmpeg_tools.py:156) to the companion suite so the two copies cannot drift again.

**Verifier:** Verified. companion/src/ccsync_companion/ffmpeg_tools.py has no dropframe_normalized anywhere (grep across the repo returns hits only under broll/indexer/ and KNOWN_BUGS.md). _timecode_from (:302-330) returns the tag verbatim, probe_video returns it at :410, proxy_gen._build_cmd passes it as timecode= at :1326, and own_proxy_cmd emits it unchanged at ffmpeg_tools.py:527 (`cmd += ["-timecode", str(timecode)]`). git confirms the drift is accidental, not a decision: the rehome landed 2026-08-10 and the R10 second-half fix (commit 1643880, 2026-08-12, "proxy timecode: normalize to drop-frame at NTSC rates") touched only broll/indexer/broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py, fix_proxy_timecode.py and tests/test_ffmpeg_tools.py — the companion file is not in the commit. KNOWN_BUGS R10 states the mechanism as general ("both the encoder and the sweep normalize") and exempts the companion only for the R9 10-bit re-encode, never for timecode. fps is already computed in probe_video (:398), so the port is trivial. Caveat: the scenario needs actual 29.97/59.94 drop-frame source on the fleet — that part is asserted from the b-roll evidence, not re-proven here.

### COMP-MEDIA-2 (major, bug) — Two originals with the same stem share one proxy name: one is silently declared covered, and the parallel drain has both ffmpegs writing the same .partial
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/proxy_scan.py:195`

`partial_path`/`expected_proxies` key the proxy purely on the STEM (`Proxy/<stem>.mp4`, and `expected_proxy_paths` accepts `<stem>.mov` or `<stem>.mp4` for either original). Two originals in one directory that differ only by container therefore map to an identical proxy name and an identical coverage set. Rule 2 of proxy_gen's design ("NEVER TWO WRITERS ON ONE PROXY") is enforced against BPG (`is_bpg_busy`, proxy_scan.py:198) and against lane B (`_publish`'s re-check, proxy_gen.py:1255) but NOT against the generator itself since the drain went parallel (2026-08-11): nothing keys in-flight work by output path — `_encoding` (proxy_gen.py:400) holds SOURCE paths, and `_still_needed` (proxy_gen.py:1284) never looks for an existing `.partial`.

**Failure scenario:** A folder holds `A001.mov` (camera original) and `A001.mp4` (a transcode/second deliverable), both unproxied. scan_project queues both; on an NVENC rig the drain runs 4 wide, so worker 1 and worker 2 pick them up within the same second and BOTH spawn `ffmpeg ... -f mp4 <dir>/Proxy/A001.mp4.partial`. The two encoders interleave writes into one file; worker 1's `_verify` decodes a file worker 2 is still writing, and whichever publishes first `os.replace`s a corrupt/truncated proxy into `Proxy/A001.mp4` (the loser silently discards). The single-worker case is just as wrong but quieter: once BPG has written `Proxy/A001.mov` for the .mov, `expected_proxies('.../A001.mp4')` matches it, so the .mp4 original is counted as covered forever, is never queued, and never appears in the gap report even though no proxy of it exists.

**Suggested fix:** Make the generated name unambiguous — include the source extension, e.g. `Proxy/<stem>.<srcext>.mp4` (or `<stem>_<ext>.mp4`) — and have `expected_proxy_paths` return the stem-only names only for the matching source extension. Failing that (the convention is shared with BPG/Resolve), add a process-wide in-flight set keyed on the partial path: claim it under `_lock` in `encode_once` before `os.makedirs`, refuse the clip if it is already claimed, and release it in the same `finally` as `_clear_encoding`.

**Verifier:** Verified both halves. proxy_scan.partial_path (:183-195) and _proxy_dir_and_stem key purely on the stem, and expected_proxies -> proxy_relink.expected_proxy_paths (:124-135) returns Proxy/<stem>.mov AND Proxy/<stem>.mp4 for ANY original, so A001.mov and A001.mp4 in one dir share both the coverage set and the .partial name. scan_project (:283-380) does no stem de-dup, so both are queued when neither proxy exists. Nothing keys in-flight work by output path: _encoding (proxy_gen.py:400-402) is {thread ident: SOURCE path}, _still_needed (:1284-1305) checks only the final names and is_bpg_busy, never the .partial, and _drain_worker/_drain_queue (:1504-1560) run width>1 workers off the same queue. Rule 2's protections (is_bpg_busy, _publish's re-check at :1244-1270) are both against OTHER writers only. Note the suggested rename conflicts with the documented shared BPG/Resolve convention (proxy_scan.py:17-20, SPEC.md:13), so the in-flight-claim variant of the fix is the applicable one.

### COMP-MEDIA-3 (major, bug) — BPG is launched with the frozen companion's PYTHONHOME/PYTHON3HOME inherited — pointed at a _MEI dir that is about to be deleted
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/bpg.py:688`

`resolve_bridge._pin_frozen_python3_home()` sets PYTHONHOME and PYTHON3HOME process-wide to `sys._MEIPASS` on every connect() in a frozen build, and the module states in PINNED_PYTHON_ENV_VARS (resolve_bridge.py:50-53) that these "must not be inherited by children" because the bootloader deletes that directory — which is why `sanitized_child_env()` exists and is used by app.py:441, music_server.py:195, tray.py:811/814, upgrade.py:765, ytdlp_manager.py:311, ytdl_executor.py:220 and shutdown_guard.py:1378. `bpg._spawn_detached` is the one child spawn that skips it, and it is the worst one to skip: the child is Resolve.exe itself, whose fusionscript locates and LoadLibrary()s a Python by exactly these variables (the mechanism documented in _pin_frozen_python3_home's own docstring, whose failure mode is a 0xc0000005 in the loading process).

**Failure scenario:** On a frozen editor build the bridge has connected at least once, so PYTHONHOME=PYTHON3HOME=C:\Users\<u>\AppData\Local\Temp\_MEIxxxx. proxy_gen's tick finds 6 BRAW gaps and calls `maybe_launch`, which Popens `Resolve.exe -pg` with the inherited environment (no `env=` argument). BPG/Resolve initialises its embedded Python against the companion's PyInstaller extraction dir — a tree with no matching stdlib — and when the editor later exits the tray the directory is deleted underneath the still-running BPG. The visible result is the R14 symptom returning: the launch "succeeds", the 30-minute cooldown suppresses retries, and the BRAW gap never closes.

**Suggested fix:** Pass `env=resolve_bridge.sanitized_child_env()` in `_spawn_detached` (bpg.py:688), the same way tray.py:811 does for its detached spawns. Add the same for `press_start`'s PowerShell and `_cim_command_lines` for consistency, and consider a cross-module test that asserts every Popen in the package either sanitizes or is explicitly listed as exempt.

**Verifier:** Verified. bpg._spawn_detached (bpg.py:678-697) calls subprocess.Popen with no env= argument, so it inherits PYTHONHOME/PYTHON3HOME, which _pin_frozen_python3_home (resolve_bridge.py:105-146) sets process-wide to sys._MEIPASS in frozen builds. resolve_bridge.py:47-52 states outright that these "must not be inherited by children" (AUDIT_2 CORE-M6), and every other spawn in the package obeys it (app.py:441, music_server.py:195, tray.py:811/814, upgrade.py:765, ytdlp_manager.py:311, ytdl_executor.py:220, ytdl_server.py:150, shutdown_guard.py:1378) with tests asserting it (test_music_server.py:342-359, test_ytdl_server.py:358-368). bpg's is the only unsanitized one, and the child really is Resolve.exe (find_bpg_command:189-202 returns [Resolve.exe, -pg]; bpg.py fact 1). No comment or doc exempts it. Caveat: that Resolve/BPG actually mis-loads Python from the _MEI dir is inferred from the same fusionscript mechanism, not measured — the env leak against a documented rule is what is proven.

### COMP-MEDIA-4 (major, logic) — Failure-capped clips consume the per-project 500-clip scan budget, so a project can go permanently 'nothing-to-do' with thousands of encodable clips left
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/proxy_gen.py:956`

`scan_project` caps the per-project clip list at MAX_QUEUE_PER_PROJECT=500 newest-first (proxy_scan.py:379) and the cap is applied BEFORE proxy_gen knows anything about failures; `scan_once` then removes capped clips from the flat queue (proxy_gen.py:956). Capped clips therefore still occupy the 500 slots the scan is allowed to report, and nothing ever asks the scanner for the next 500. `_failures` is only cleared by `request_run()` (proxy_gen.py:763, the MED-6 fix) or a companion restart, so the unattended path has no way out.

**Failure scenario:** The base rig's Energy Transition tree has ~1,040 unproxied clips in one project. A bad night (the 0.6.1 muxer failure is the recorded instance, but a GPU in a bad state or a batch of truncated downloads does the same) fails the newest 500 three times each. From then on every 900 s scan returns exactly those 500 newest clips, `scan_once` filters all 500 out, the queue is empty, `_gate` publishes STATE_NOTHING_TO_DO, and the ~540 perfectly encodable older clips are never even offered to ffmpeg — while the tray still reports the full missing count. The machine sits idle all night with the work available; only a restart or the tray's "Make the missing proxies now" recovers it.

**Suggested fix:** Make the cap aware of the cap-list: either pass a `skip_paths`/`is_capped` predicate into `scan_project` so capped clips are excluded before the 500-slot truncation, or, in `scan_once`, when filtering leaves the queue empty while `totals['missing']` is non-zero, drop the oldest cap entries (or re-scan with the capped paths excluded) so the drain always has real work. Logging "N clips are capped and M remain unscanned" would also have made the 0.6.1 night visible.
