**Verifier:** Verified the whole chain. proxy_scan.scan_project truncates to MAX_QUEUE_PER_PROJECT=500 newest-first (:96, :379-381) with no knowledge of failures; scan_once builds the flat queue and only then filters (proxy_gen.py:956, `queue = [clip for clip in queue if not self._is_capped(clip)]`). _failures is written only by _record_failure (:1097) and cleared only by request_run (:763-764, the MED-6 tray retry) — grep shows no other clear, and the comment at :414-417 confirms it is in-process-only with no expiry. So a project whose 500 newest clips are all capped reports queue=[] forever while totals['missing'] still counts the older encodable clips, and the gate publishes NOTHING_TO_DO. request_run's own comment ("the 0.6.1 muxer night capped all 1040 clips on this rig") shows the mass-failure precondition has actually occurred. The scan does set gap['truncated'], but nothing acts on it to fetch the next 500.

### COMP-MEDIA-5 (major, inefficiency) — A proxy Resolve refuses is re-offered every 120 s forever — no memory of refusals, one fusionscript call and one WARNING per clip per sweep
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/proxy_relink.py:206`

`plan_relinks` suppresses a repeat only when the clip is ALREADY pointed at the same path (`old_proxy` non-empty and equal, :206). A refusal leaves nothing stored on the clip, so `proxy_path` stays "" and `proxy_state` stays "None", and the identical op is regenerated on the very next pass. `apply_relinks` (:227) has no failure memory either, and app._relink_proxies_once (app.py:2465) is called from the 120 s media-tree refresh with the whole media pool. Every other repeating path in this codebase has a brake for exactly this — proxy_gen's `_failures` cap, the watcher's warn-once, R15 fix 4's per-watcher dedupe set — and this one has none.

**Failure scenario:** A project holds 200 camera clips whose adjacent `Proxy/` files carry a mismatched timecode (COMP-MEDIA-1's output, or the legacy timecode-less archive previews R10 describes). Every 120 s the media-tree thread plans 200 ops, takes `_API_LOCK` 200 times for LinkProxyMedia calls Resolve refuses, and writes 200 `proxy relink: Resolve refused ...` WARNING lines. That is ~144,000 log lines a day into the single shared `~/.ccsync/companion.log` — the same 5 MB-per-25-minutes rotation that destroyed the upgrade history during the R15 investigation — plus a permanent stream of GIL-holding native calls competing with the tray and the watcher.

**Suggested fix:** Give the relink pass the same negative memory the rest of the companion has: keep a per-session dict keyed on (file_path, new_proxy, proxy mtime/size) of refusals and skip planning those ops (re-arming when the proxy file changes or the companion restarts), and demote the repeat log line to DEBUG with a per-pass count summary, exactly as R15 fix 4 did for the watcher.

**Verifier:** Verified. plan_relinks suppresses only the already-pointed-here case (proxy_relink.py:204-210); a LinkProxyMedia refusal leaves the clip's proxy_path/proxy_state untouched, so the identical op is regenerated next pass. apply_relinks (:227-262) logs a WARNING per refusal and keeps no memory. app._relink_proxies_once (app.py:2465-2490) is driven by _media_tree_loop at media_tree_refresh_interval, default 120 s (app.py:747, config.py:194/648), over the whole media pool, and emits an INFO count line per pass on top. The contrast the finding draws is exact and in the same file: the piggy-backed _classify_pool_once one screen up (app.py:2378-2436) keeps _pool_offered_non_canonical/_pool_warned_foreign sets precisely because "a refusal must not retry every pass". The comment at :2470-2473 ("Costs nothing in the steady state") is only true when links SUCCEED.

### COMP-MEDIA-6 (major, logic) — BPG can never be launched on a machine where BPG has never been opened (its Preferences directory does not exist yet)
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/bpg.py:386`

`ensure_watch_folders` refuses to create the settings file unless its parent directory already exists ("or a machine with no proxy generator would collect a stray config"), returning reason "no BPG settings directory". `_ensure_watch_folders` maps any non-ok outcome to False and `maybe_launch` then returns "watch folders could not be set" WITHOUT setting a cooldown (bpg.py:626-630), so the launcher retries and refuses forever. But `%APPDATA%\Blackmagic Design\Blackmagic Proxy Generator\Preferences` is created by BPG itself on first run, not by the Resolve installer — so the precondition for launching BPG is that BPG has already been launched. The stray-config worry the guard exists for is already covered one line up the call chain by `find_bpg_command`, which requires Resolve.exe to be present.

**Failure scenario:** A remote editor's machine has Resolve installed but has never opened the proxy generator (the normal state — R14 is precisely about editors not knowing to run it). BRAW clips arrive with no proxies; proxy_gen counts them and calls `maybe_launch` every 15 s. `settings_path()` resolves, the directory is absent, ensure_watch_folders returns not-ok, and BPG is never started on that machine — while the log line says only "watch folders could not be set". The whole R14 feature is inert on every machine that most needs it.

**Suggested fix:** When `self.command` is set (Resolve is installed, which is the real "is there a proxy generator here" test), create the Preferences directory with `os.makedirs(..., exist_ok=True)` and write the seed `[General]`/`watchFolderList` file, instead of refusing. Keep the refusal only for the case where `find_bpg_command` returned None.

**Verifier:** Verified, including the empirical premise. ensure_watch_folders returns {'ok': False, 'reason': 'no BPG settings directory'} when os.path.isdir(dirname(target)) is False (bpg.py:381-388); _ensure_watch_folders maps that to False (:540-549) and maybe_launch returns "watch folders could not be set" WITHOUT setting _last_launch_at (:626-630, and test_bpg.py:397-408 asserts no cooldown is burnt) — so it retries and refuses indefinitely. The behaviour is deliberate and tested (test_bpg.py:351-357, "a machine with no bpg profile collects no stray config"), but the premise it rests on is wrong: on this box %APPDATA%\Blackmagic Design\Blackmagic Proxy Generator\{Preferences,Support} were all created 27/04/2026 13:00:43, the same second as Support\logs\rollinglog.txt and gpudetect.bin — i.e. by BPG's first RUN — while Resolve's own appdata dir dates to 13/04/2026 and Resolve.exe to 22/06/2026. So the directory is not laid down by the installer, and find_bpg_command's Resolve.exe check (:189-202) is already the real "is there a proxy generator here" test.

### COMP-MEDIA-9 (major, upgrade) — Every fusionscript call runs in-process behind one global RLock — a single hung call takes the whole companion's Resolve half down with no recovery
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_bridge.py:45`

Goal: talk to Resolve reliably from a long-lived tray app. Current approach: lazy-connect and call the C extension in-process, serialized by `_API_LOCK`, with each call holding the GIL for its full native duration (the file's own comments at :35-45 and :434-450 document both). The cost is that there is no timeout anywhere: a call that blocks — ImportMedia against a P: share that has gone away, a media-pool walk on a stalled SMB mount, the stale-fusionscript wedge of R12 — holds `_API_LOCK` and the GIL indefinitely. Everything queues behind it: the timeline watcher stops polling (so the popup fixer and path classification go blind), tray "Scan whole project"/"Copy media in" hang, the b-roll and music loopback servers hang their HTTP handlers, and the win32 tray message pump starves so the menu will not open. The only exit is killing the companion, and R11 shows what an editor left with no companion costs. The repo already has the better pattern in-house: `music_worker` drives Resolve from a killable CHILD PROCESS (noted at resolve_bridge.py:1216-1220), which is also the pattern that survives fusionscript faulting 0xc0000005 instead of taking the windowed exe down with zero log output.

**Failure scenario:** An editor's P: mapping drops while the YouTube importer is mid-batch. `media_pool.ImportMedia([...])` blocks inside fusionscript on the dead share; `_API_LOCK` is held. Within one poll interval the watcher thread, the media-tree thread, the tray refresh and any /insert request are all parked on the same lock, the tray icon stops responding to right-clicks, and the dashboard stops getting a project name — with nothing in the log to say why, because the blocked call never returns to log anything.

**Suggested fix:** Move the long/hang-prone entry points (ImportMedia, AppendToTimeline, the media-pool walk, ReplaceClip/LinkProxyMedia batches) behind a small child-process worker — music_worker's existing spawn is the template — with a per-call timeout and kill, keeping the cheap enumerations in-process. At minimum, run each call on a watchdog thread with a deadline so a wedged call is reported (and the bridge marked dead via note_connection) instead of silently freezing every other thread.

**Verifier:** The factual core holds: _API_LOCK is a single module-global RLock serializing every fusionscript call (resolve_bridge.py:35-45) and grep for timeout/watchdog in that file returns nothing but ui_state.wait_while_menu_open() calls — there is no deadline, no kill path, and no note_connection on a wedge, so a blocked native call holds the lock and the GIL indefinitely. The in-house precedent is real and documented for exactly this reason: music_worker.py:17-21 says it runs as a CHILD PROCESS "never in the tray app" because "scriptapp() returns None (or blocks for minutes) ... and a hung Resolve must not take the companion with it", with music_server killing it on a timeout. Two corrections to the failure scenario: the MUSIC loopback handler does NOT hang on _API_LOCK (it goes through the child worker, music_server.py:195), and the repo does document a deliberate in-process choice for the YouTube importer/b-roll insert (resolve_bridge.py:1216-1220 — "a child would have to pay for a full pool walk PER FILE"), which constrains moving ImportMedia wholesale. The minimum form of the fix (per-call deadline/watchdog thread reporting the wedge) conflicts with nothing documented.

### COMP-MEDIA-7 (minor, logic) — cancel_run() outside a drain latches _cancel forever, so the next eligible drain is aborted on its first clip and the whole scanned queue is dropped
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/proxy_gen.py:779`

`_cancel` is only ever cleared inside `_drain_queue` (proxy_gen.py:1583) and by `request_run` (:765). `cancel_run` can be called when no drain is running: the tray builds its menu from a snapshot, and the "Stop making proxies" item is shown whenever `gap()['encoding']` was true at snapshot time (tray.py:1948) — a state that ends the instant the user touches the keyboard, which is exactly when they open the tray.

**Failure scenario:** An editor comes back to their desk; every unforced encode is interrupted within ~2 s and the drain ends. They open the tray, see the stale "Stop making proxies" item and click it. No drain is running, so `_cancel` stays set. That night the gate finally says RUNNING: the first worker pops the newest clip, pays an ffprobe and an ffmpeg spawn, `_interrupt_reason` immediately answers "you asked it to stop", the encode is killed, `_drain_over` is set, and `_drain_queue` then empties the entire freshly-scanned queue. Nothing is encoded until the next scan interval (up to 900 s) rebuilds it.

**Suggested fix:** Only honour `_cancel` while a drain is in flight: have `cancel_run` set it and immediately clear it if `self._active == 0 and not self._encoding` (under `_lock`), or clear `_cancel` at the top of `_drain_queue` before the workers start rather than only at the end.

**Verifier:** Verified. _cancel is set by cancel_run (:779) with no guard on whether a drain is in flight, and cleared in only two places (grep: :765 request_run, :1583 the tail of _drain_queue). _interrupt_reason checks it second, above the idle check (:1129-1130, "you asked it to stop"), so the next drain's first encode is killed and _drain_over is set; _drain_queue then empties the whole freshly scanned queue and clears the flag (:1576-1585), leaving nothing until the next scan (default proxy_scan_interval 900 s). The stale-menu precondition is real: tray.py:1946-1948 shows "Stop making proxies" whenever the SNAPSHOT's proxy_encoding was true, and rule 1 kills unforced encodes within ~2 s of the user touching the keyboard — the moment they reach for the tray.

### COMP-MEDIA-8 (minor, bug) — _find_or_create_bin does not check AddSubFolder's return, so a refused bin makes the b-roll insert report 'Resolve didn't answer'
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_bridge.py:1049`

`AddSubFolder` is documented in this very file (`_ensure_bin_path`, :1281-1308) as returning None/False on a locked project rather than raising, and that helper handles it. `_find_or_create_bin` — the b-roll insert's path — returns the raw value, and `_perform_insert_locked` (:1169-1170) feeds it straight back in as the next `root_folder`, so the second segment of BROLL_BIN_PATH dereferences None.

**Failure scenario:** An editor in a collaboration/locked project clicks "Send to Resolve" in the b-roll web UI. `AddSubFolder(root, 'B-Roll')` returns None; the loop's next iteration calls `None.GetSubFolderList()`, raising AttributeError, which the outer `except` turns into the generic `_SCRIPTING_ERROR_MESSAGE` — the toast tells them to "make sure a project is open, then try again" when a project IS open and the real problem is that Resolve will not create a bin. Repeating the action can never work.

**Suggested fix:** Have `_find_or_create_bin` return None when AddSubFolder is falsy (or reuse `_ensure_bin_path`, which already implements the whole walk-or-create contract correctly), and have `_perform_insert_locked` return {"ok": False, "message": "Resolve would not create the B-Roll/Archive bin — is the project locked?"}.

**Verifier:** Verified. _find_or_create_bin (resolve_bridge.py:1044-1049) returns media_pool.AddSubFolder(...) raw, and BROLL_BIN_PATH is a TWO-segment path ("B-Roll", "Archive", :983) walked in a loop that feeds the previous result back in as root_folder (:1168-1170), so a None/False first segment makes the next iteration call None.GetSubFolderList(). The outer except at :1206-1211 converts that into _SCRIPTING_ERROR_MESSAGE = "Resolve didn't answer. Make sure a project is open, then try again." (:704), which is the wrong instruction. That AddSubFolder returns None/False rather than raising on a locked project is documented in this same file by _ensure_bin_path (:1279-1308), which handles it correctly and returns None — so the correct contract already exists two hundred lines below. Minor severity is right: it needs a locked/refusing project.

## COMP-BROLL — Companion loopback servers (b-roll / music / ytdl)

### COMP-BROLL-1 (critical, bug) — `--write-info-json` drops a 40-200 KB file into the Syncthing sendreceive project tree, then deletes it -- the exact `ignoreDelete` immortality bug R15 fix 3 just closed for `.part`/`.ytdl`
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:1159`

`build_argv` passes `--write-info-json`, so yt-dlp writes `<Uploader - Title [id]>.info.json` into `P:\Projects\<label>\Youtube\<term>\` at the START of the download (right after extraction, before the media bytes). `write_sidecar` (:754-757) unlinks it after the clip lands. That folder is a Syncthing *sendreceive* root -- server/common.py:149-166 says so in as many words -- and `build_stignore_lines()` (server/common.py:188-205) ignores only video extensions, `*.partial`, `*.part`, `*.part-Frag*`, `*.ytdl` and `Proxy`. `.json` is deliberately NOT ignored (plan §7: "Sidecars/manifest.json travel Syncthing"). Since the 2026-08-11 delete-protection retrofit set `ignoreDelete=True` on editor folders too, the later delete never propagates. The NAS worker never had this exposure: `vendor/downloader.py build_opts` sets no `writeinfojson` at all -- it reads the info dict in-process. This is a companion-only regression introduced by the 0.8.0 executor.

**Failure scenario:** An editor claims a 40-clip 1080p job. For each clip yt-dlp writes `Some Channel - Some Title [abc123].info.json` (~120 KB) into `<project>/Youtube/<term>/` and the clip then downloads for 2-6 minutes -- far longer than Syncthing's 10 s fsWatcher delay -- so every one of the 40 files is indexed and fanned out to the NAS and to every other editor with that project ticked. The executor then deletes each one locally; `ignoreDelete=true` means the deletion is never replicated. Result: ~5 MB of permanent, undeletable `.info.json` litter per job on the NAS and on every editor's disk, growing with every job, removable only by hand -- byte for byte the failure server/common.py:149-158 documents for `.part` files (27 orphans, 1.6 GB on ruskin's machine).

**Suggested fix:** Stop writing the info json into the canonical tree. Either (a) replace `--write-info-json` with `--print-to-file` / `--print '%(.{channel,channel_url,uploader,webpage_url,title,id,upload_date,duration})j'` writing to a path OUTSIDE the synced tree (e.g. `%TEMP%`/`ytdlp_manager.tools_dir()`) and build the sidecar from that, or (b) keep `--write-info-json` but add `--paths infojson:<scratch dir>` so it never lands in `Youtube/`. If neither, add `*.info.json` to `YTDL_IGNORE_LINES` in all three copies (server/common.py, dashboard provision.build_stignore_lines, companion syncthing_admin.STIGNORE_LINES) plus the lane B `- *.info.json` rule -- the same three-way lockstep the 2026-08-14 fix used.

**Verifier:** Verified end to end. ytdl_executor.py:1159 passes --write-info-json and ytdl_common.outtmpl() (:71-78) points yt-dlp at outdir itself, so `<stem>.info.json` is written into <project>/Youtube/<term>/ at extraction time and lives there for the whole media download; write_sidecar (:754-757) unlinks it afterwards. server/common.py:180-205 YTDL_IGNORE_LINES covers only *.part/*.part-Frag*/*.ytdl (identical copies in companion sync/syncthing_admin.py:86-90 and dashboard provision), and build_stignore_lines adds only video extensions + Proxy — .json is not ignored anywhere. syncthing_admin.py:427-439 creates project folders as type=sendreceive, fsWatcherEnabled=True, ignoreDelete=True, and docs/delete-protection-ignoredelete.md states the semantics explicitly (a device does not APPLY received deletes but does propagate its own), so the later unlink leaves the copy on the NAS and on every other ticked editor permanently — structurally identical to R15 fix 3 (KNOWN_BUGS.md:271-280). The hunter is also right that the NAS worker never had this exposure: vendor/downloader.py build_opts (:126-200) sets no writeinfojson at all (grep for writeinfojson across ytdl/ returns nothing), so write_sidecar's docstring claim that 'the server's downloader deletes it for exactly the same reason' is itself inaccurate. Caveat on scale/severity: this is litter (tens of KB per clip, a few MB per job), not data loss, so 'critical' is inflated — but the mechanism and the ignoreDelete immortality are real and undocumented anywhere in the plan or KNOWN_BUGS.

### COMP-BROLL-2 (major, bug) — The local executor embeds none of the credits metadata the NAS worker embeds -- §5's "embedded tags" half of the naming contract is simply absent from the argv
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:1154`

docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §5 lists what the two executors must produce identically: "outtmpl ..., the `.credits.json` sidecar, **embedded tags**, `ensure_edit_ready`'s h264 policy, and the quality-fallback rung semantics." The vendored server downloader implements the embedded half with two postprocessors (`vendor/downloader.py:137-155`): a `MetadataParser` at `pre_process` writing `meta_channel` / `meta_video_url` / `meta_channel_url` / `meta_uploader` plus `artist` and `meta_comment`, then `FFmpegMetadata` with `add_metadata: True`. Its module header calls these "three redundant metadata channels" that "the downstream DaVinci Resolve credits script reads". `build_argv` emits `-f`, `--merge-output-format`, `-o`, `--write-info-json`, `--no-playlist`, `--no-progress` and optionally `--ffmpeg-location` -- no `--parse-metadata`, no `--embed-metadata`. `ytdl_common` pins the filename and the sidecar between the trees, and `tools/release.ps1`'s byte-compare gate only covers `ytdl_common`, so nothing catches this. `test_the_argv_is_the_shared_naming_contract` (companion/tests/test_ytdl_executor.py:389) asserts only `-f`, `-o`, `--merge-output-format`, `--write-info-json`, `--no-playlist`.

**Failure scenario:** Two clips land in the same `Energy Transition/Youtube/lng terminal/` folder -- one fetched by the NAS worker before an editor upgraded, one fetched by the editor's own 0.8.0 companion. `ffprobe` on the first shows `artist=Cruz Yanez`, `comment=https://youtube.com/watch?v=...`, `meta_channel`, `meta_channel_url`, `meta_uploader`, `meta_video_url`; the same fields on the second are empty. The Resolve credits script that reads the embedded tags (the primary of the three channels) produces a credits timeline missing every locally-downloaded clip, and nothing in the fleet reports a difference because the FILENAMES match.

**Suggested fix:** Add the CLI equivalents to `build_argv`: `--parse-metadata` for each of the four `_credits_action` mappings plus the two `INTERPRET` actions (`%(channel,uploader)s:%(meta_channel)s` etc.), and `--embed-metadata`. Better: move the tag list into `ytdl_common` beside `OUTTMPL` so the parity gate and `tests/test_ytdl_common.py` pin it against `downloader._credits_action` the way `QUALITY_HEIGHTS` is pinned, and bump `TEMPLATE_VERSION` (or add a `TAGS_VERSION`) so the mixed-artifact window is caught by the claim handshake.

**Verifier:** build_argv (ytdl_executor.py:1154-1167) emits only -f / --merge-output-format / -o / --write-info-json / --no-playlist / --no-progress (+ optional --ffmpeg-location). vendor/downloader.py:131-155 does embed the credits metadata on the NAS side: a MetadataParser pre_process pass writing meta_channel / meta_video_url / meta_channel_url / meta_uploader plus artist and meta_comment, then {'key':'FFmpegMetadata','add_metadata':True}; the module header (:8-23) calls these the channels the Resolve credits script reads. docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §5 names 'embedded tags' as part of the must-be-identical set, and the doc's 'As-built deviations' list documents four other deviations but not this one — so it is not a documented decision. The parity gate genuinely does not cover it: server/tests/test_cross_component.py:350-473 compares only ytdl_common (bytes, OUTTMPL, SIDECAR_FIELDS, versions), and companion/tests/test_ytdl_executor.py:389-404 asserts only -f/-o/--merge-output-format/--write-info-json/--no-playlist. Mitigating: the .credits.json sidecar IS still written locally and downloader.py:473-474 says the sidecar alone is sufficient for the credits script, so 'no credits information anywhere' overstates it — the real defect is a fleet with two artifact shapes for one clip, which §5 forbids.

### COMP-BROLL-3 (major, bug) — `is_sweepable` knows only `.part`/`.ytdl`/`.temp`, so a COMPLETED per-format intermediate (`... [id].f137.mp4`) survives every cleanup path and is uploaded fleet-wide by lane A
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:91`

`SWEEPABLE_SUFFIXES` + the `.part-Frag` prefix are worker parity, and they cover files yt-dlp is still writing. They do not cover the file yt-dlp has FINISHED writing: with `-f bestvideo+bestaudio`, yt-dlp renames `... [id].f137.mp4.part` to `... [id].f137.mp4` the moment the video stream completes, and keeps it (deliberately, for resume) if anything afterwards fails. `clear_partials` (:667) and `_cleanup_current` (:1169) therefore leave it, `landed_file` (:651) correctly ignores it (its stem ends in `.f137`, not `[id]`), and nothing else on the machine ever deletes it. It matches lane A's `+ *.mp4` include and no exclude (`IN_PROGRESS_EXCLUDE_RULES` is `*.tmp/*.lock/*.partial`, rclone_lane.py:87), and it matches no `.stignore` line, so it replicates to the NAS and onward. The plan's own failure table asserts the opposite: "A clip the editor half-downloaded leaves only `.part` litter" (§3).

**Failure scenario:** An editor starts a 20-clip 1080p job and closes the laptop lid at clip 7, whose f137 video stream (1.4 GB) had just finished and whose f140 audio was in flight. `stop_all()` -> `job.stop()` kills yt-dlp; `_cleanup_current` removes `... [id].f140.m4a.part` and `... [id].ytdl` and leaves `Cruz Yanez - Aerial View Rio Grande LNG [SAQBbd1Rxmo].f137.mp4`, 1.4 GB, video-only, forever. The server reclaims the lease and re-downloads the clip properly, so the folder now holds both the real clip and the orphan; lane A uploads the orphan to the NAS and lane B fans it back out to every editor with the project ticked.

**Suggested fix:** Extend the sweep to yt-dlp's completed per-format intermediates: in `is_sweepable`, also return True for a stem matching `\.f\d+$` (i.e. `Path(name).stem` ends in `.f<digits>`), which is id-scoped already because `clear_partials`/`id_bearing_files` only ever look at `[video_id]`-bearing names. Mirror it in the server worker's `_sweepable` so the two executors stay in step, and add a regression test that a `[id].f137.mp4` left behind by a killed run is removed by `_cleanup_current`.

**Verifier:** is_sweepable (:632-635) matches only the .part/.ytdl/.temp suffixes and the .part-Frag prefix, so a COMPLETED per-format file (`... [id].f137.mp4`) is untouched by clear_partials (:667) and _cleanup_current (:1169); landed_file (:651-664) correctly ignores it (stem ends .f137, not [id]), so nothing on the machine removes it. Stronger than the hunter states: the NAS worker HAS a guard for exactly this file and the companion does not — worker.py:731-748 _disown_output renames every non-sweepable leftover the failed attempt created to `<name>.failed` (YTDL-3), and it is called from _record_failure (:817-826) on the ordinary failure path; the companion's failure path calls only clear_partials (:1057, :1069). Sync exposure checks out: rclone_lane IN_PROGRESS_EXCLUDE_RULES is *.tmp/*.lock/*.partial (:87) and lane A's includes are by video extension, so `[id].f137.mp4` uploads; `.failed` would not have. Note the same orphan also fails the merge-less path in COMP-BROLL-5, and the clip is reported failed either way, so the harm is the fleet-wide orphan, not a mis-reported success.

### COMP-BROLL-4 (major, bug) — `VIDEO_EXTS` admits yt-dlp's `[id].fNNN.mp4` intermediates, so the auto-importer files a video-only fragment into the shared media pool and yt-dlp later deletes it out from under Resolve
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/youtube_import.py:89`

`_is_clip_name` (:501-516) whitelists by extension only: dotfiles, `.partial`/`.tmp`/`.lock` and non-video extensions are excluded, but `Cruz Yanez - Aerial View [SAQBbd1Rxmo].f137.mp4` has extension `.mp4` and passes. Before 0.8.0 that was harmless on editor rigs because no yt-dlp ever ran there -- lane B delivered only finished files. The 0.8.0 executor runs yt-dlp directly inside `<project>/Youtube/<term>/`, which is exactly the directory `_collect` walks, and `_import_batch` then canonicalises the path to `P:\...` (`_canonical_spelling`, :708) before storing it, so the bad path is the fleet-portable one. The settle rules do not save it: the intermediate's mtime stops advancing the instant the video stream completes, so it becomes "settled" (same size+mtime across two scans, `min_age_seconds` 120 elapsed) while the audio stream and the ffmpeg merge are still running, or permanently after the COMP-BROLL-3 orphan case.

**Failure scenario:** A remote editor downloads a 2-hour 1080p LNG clip locally. Video (f137) finishes at T; audio and the merge run until T+4 min. The importer's 60 s scan sees `... [SAQBbd1Rxmo].f137.mp4` unchanged at T+60 and again at T+120, mtime older than 120 s -> it is imported into `Master/Youtube/lng terminal` and stored as `P:\Projects\2026\FF5\Energy Transition\Youtube\lng terminal\... [SAQBbd1Rxmo].f137.mp4`. At T+4 min yt-dlp merges and DELETES it. The shared project now holds a Media Offline clip pointing at a path that exists on no machine in the fleet, plus the real clip imported a minute later -- and per this module's own decision 2 (no database) nothing ever removes the ghost.

**Suggested fix:** Reject yt-dlp intermediates by name in `_is_clip_name`: skip anything whose stem matches `\.f\d+$` (and, belt and braces, anything whose stem ends in `.temp`, which is the merge target's transient name). Same regex as the COMP-BROLL-3 fix so the two agree, with a test using a real `Title [id].f137.mp4` fixture.

**Verifier:** _is_clip_name (youtube_import.py:501-516) filters on dotfiles, SKIP_SUFFIXES (.partial/.tmp/.lock) and VIDEO_EXTS (:89) only, so `Title [id].f137.mp4` passes; _collect walks exactly <project>/Youtube/ and its term folders (:520-582), which is the directory the 0.8.0 executor now writes into. The settle rule is (now-mtime) >= min_age_seconds (default 120, :170) AND an unchanged (size,mtime) fingerprint across two scans at scan_interval (default 60, :169) — a finished f137 fragment's mtime stops advancing immediately, so it qualifies in ~2-3 minutes while the audio stream and merge are still running, and permanently in the COMP-BROLL-3 orphan case. _import_batch then stores the canonical P: spelling (_canonical_spelling, :708-724), and decision 2 in the module docstring (no database) means the ghost pool entry is never reaped after yt-dlp deletes the fragment. No guard, comment or test anywhere excludes `.fNNN` names.

### COMP-BROLL-5 (major, logic) — `capabilities()` reports ok:true without checking ffmpeg, yet every in-scope quality is a merge format -- a machine with no ffmpeg claims the job and fails 100% of its clips
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:506`

`capabilities()` gates on `local_downloads_enabled`, dashboard URL+token, a verified editor and the yt-dlp sidecar's cached status. It never asks whether ffmpeg resolves. But `SCOPE_QUALITIES` is 480p/720p/1080p, and `ytdl_common.format_selector(q, prefer_avc=True)` for all three is a `bestvideo...+bestaudio...` selector -- yt-dlp refuses those outright when no merger is available (`report_error('You have requested merging of multiple formats but ffmpeg is not installed')`). `build_argv` only adds `--ffmpeg-location` when `_ffmpeg_location(cfg)` resolves (:1163-1165), i.e. it silently omits the flag on exactly the machines where the download cannot work. ffmpeg is explicitly optional on this fleet -- `ffmpeg_tools.ffmpeg_available`'s docstring says "ffmpeg is an OPTIONAL" dependency and `proxy_generation_enabled` is tri-state for that reason. The design promise this breaks is plan §6's "Editors never see a broken local downloader -- they see the old behaviour."

**Failure scenario:** A Mac editor (or any Windows editor who never installed ffmpeg / has proxy generation off) has yt-dlp installed and current, so `capabilities()` answers ok:true. The SPA dispatches a 30-clip job; the companion claims it, flips `download_mode=local`, downloads nothing successfully, posts 30 `failed` rows, and the server's second-chance sweep then re-downloads all 30 on the NAS. Net effect: the editor's IP is burned on 30 metadata calls, the job takes twice as long, the history shows 30 red rows attributed to that editor, and the feature is silently useless on that machine forever (the sidecar manager only ever fixes yt-dlp, never ffmpeg).

**Suggested fix:** Add an ffmpeg check to `capabilities()` -- `ffmpeg_tools._resolve_binary(cfg.get('ffmpeg_path', 'ffmpeg'))` is already a cheap, cached, zero-subprocess call that fits the 1 s probe budget -- and answer `ok:false, reason='ffmpeg is not installed on this machine, so downloaded video and audio cannot be merged'`. Carry the resolved directory into `build_argv` so the flag is always present when the capability said yes.

**Verifier:** capabilities() (:473-507) gates on local_downloads_enabled, dashboard_url+token, editor, and the cached yt-dlp status, then sets ok:true — no ffmpeg check. All three SCOPE_QUALITIES resolve through ytdl_common.format_selector(q, prefer_avc=True) (:149-172) to `bestvideo[height<=N][vcodec^=avc1]+bestaudio[acodec^=mp4a]/...` merge selectors, and yt-dlp takes the first satisfiable alternative, which for any normal YouTube video is a video+audio pair. Without a merger yt-dlp either errors out (non-zero exit -> clip failed) or, in the warning branch, leaves `[id].fNNN.mp4` + `[id].fNNN.m4a` with no merged file, which landed_file (:651) rejects -> 'yt-dlp reported success but left no output file' (:1070-1072). Either branch = 100% clip failure. ffmpeg really is optional on this fleet (ffmpeg_tools.ffmpeg_available docstring :152-158; nothing in installer/ or onboarding/ provisions it), and build_argv only adds --ffmpeg-location when _ffmpeg_location resolves (:1163-1165), i.e. it is silent precisely on the broken machines. The suggested probe is cheap and already used by _ffmpeg_location (ffmpeg_tools._resolve_binary, no subprocess), so it fits the documented 1 s budget.

### COMP-BROLL-6 (major, logic) — The `sidecar_version` half of the §5 version handshake is checked nowhere -- neither the claim nor the manifest guard compares it
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:923`

`ytdl_common` defines TEMPLATE_VERSION and SIDECAR_VERSION as two separate numbers precisely because "they fail differently: a template skew duplicates clips, a sidecar skew feeds the Resolve credits script a shape it does not read" (ytdl_common.py:57-60), and both are advertised by the server (`routes_fleet.download_manifest` returns `sidecar_version`, `client_config` returns it too). But the claim body the companion sends is `{editor, ytdlp_version, template_version, free_bytes}` (:562-567) -- no sidecar_version -- and `routes_fleet.ClaimIn` has no such field and `claim` compares only `body.template_version`. `_download_all`'s belt-and-braces manifest guard likewise compares only `manifest.get('template_version')`. So the entire sidecar contract is unversioned in practice, and the plan's stated protection ("a companion whose vendored copy is older than the server's declines the claim in the capability handshake") does not exist for it.

**Failure scenario:** A ninth field is added to `SIDECAR_FIELDS` on the server and the dashboard is redeployed (SIDECAR_VERSION 1 -> 2; TEMPLATE_VERSION stays 1, because the filename did not change). Editors still on the 0.8.0 build claim jobs successfully -- their template_version 1 matches -- and write 8-field `.credits.json` files into the canonical tree beside the server's 9-field ones. The Resolve credits script silently drops the new field for every clip an editor fetched, and nothing anywhere reports a version skew, which is precisely the divergence §5 exists to make impossible.

**Suggested fix:** Send `sidecar_version: ytdl_common.SIDECAR_VERSION` in the claim body, add the field to `ClaimIn` and 410 on a mismatch in `routes_fleet.claim` (same branch and same reason string as the template check), and add the equivalent belt-and-braces comparison to `_download_all` alongside the template one.

**Verifier:** Factually exact. The claim body is {editor, ytdlp_version, template_version, free_bytes} (:562-567); routes_fleet.ClaimIn (:144-156) has no sidecar_version field and claim() compares only body.template_version (:213-221) — it merely echoes sidecar_version in the 410 payload. _download_all's belt-and-braces guard compares only manifest['template_version'] (:922-927). Grep confirms SIDECAR_VERSION is referenced nowhere in companion/src except its definition in ytdl_common (:60), and only once in tests as a manifest fixture field. So the sidecar half of the §5 handshake is advertised (client_config :136, download_manifest :317) and enforced nowhere. Caveat that keeps this from being a live bug: today both sides are SIDECAR_VERSION 1, the release parity gate byte-compares ytdl_common, and test_cross_component pins SIDECAR_FIELDS — the failure needs a future field addition that bumps SIDECAR_VERSION without TEMPLATE_VERSION, which is exactly the case the two separate numbers exist for (ytdl_common.py:56-60). Real latent gap, not a present divergence.

### COMP-BROLL-8 (minor, bug) — `write_sidecar` unlinks the info json even when the sidecar write failed, destroying the only source the credits could be rebuilt from
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:754`

The `try: info_path.unlink()` at :754-757 is outside every failure branch above it. If `json.loads` on the info json fails, or `Path(target).write_text(...)` raises OSError (a full disk, a share that dropped, or -- likely here -- a Windows path over 260 characters, since the outtmpl alone is `%(uploader).60B - %(title).140B [%(id)s]` and `.credits.json` is 5 characters longer than `.mp4`), `target` is set back to None, a warning is logged, and the info json is deleted anyway. The clip is then reported `done` and the ledger row is written. Nothing regenerates a sidecar for a clip already marked `done`, and the id-scoped cleanup does not touch `.credits.json` either. The NAS worker cannot hit this: `downloader._write_sidecar` builds from the in-memory info dict, so there is no artifact to lose.

**Failure scenario:** A clip with a long CJK title lands as `<205-char name>.mp4` inside `P:\Projects\2026\FF5\Energy Transition\Youtube\<term>\`. `write_text` on `<205-char stem>.credits.json` raises OSError (path > MAX_PATH); the warning is logged, `info_path.unlink()` runs, and `clip_status(done)` is posted. The clip is in the project with no `.credits.json` and no embedded tags (see COMP-BROLL-2) -- i.e. no credits information anywhere -- and the only way back is re-downloading it by hand.

**Suggested fix:** Only unlink the info json once a sidecar was actually written: move the unlink inside the `if target:` success path, or `return None` before it when `target is None`. A leftover `.info.json` is recoverable; a deleted one is not. (Once COMP-BROLL-1 moves the info json out of the synced tree, leaving it behind on failure costs nothing at all.)

**Verifier:** Verified at ytdl_executor.py:723-758: the info_path.unlink() try-block sits at function scope, after both failure branches (json.loads failure sets info=None so target stays None; Path(target).write_text OSError logs a warning and resets target=None). In both cases the info json is deleted and _download_one (:1078-1080) then posts clip_status 'done' regardless of write_sidecar's return value, and nothing re-derives a sidecar for a done clip (the id-scoped cleanup only touches sweepable suffixes). No test covers this — test_ytdl_executor.py:1031-1072 covers only the success path and the info-json-absent path. The MAX_PATH rationale is narrow (the sidecar suffix is only 3 chars longer than yt-dlp's own .info.json, so the band where one writes and the other does not is small), but any OSError — full disk, dropped share, a read-only tree — hits the same line, and the fix (move the unlink inside `if target:`) is trivially correct.

### COMP-BROLL-9 (minor, logic) — The companion ranks yt-dlp versions as int tuples while the server ranks the same strings lexicographically, so one non-zero-padded floor 403s the whole fleet forever with no self-heal
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdlp_manager.py:265`

`version_is_older` goes through `upgrade.parse_version`, which splits on `.` and compares tuples of ints. `routes_fleet._version_at_least` compares the raw strings (`reported >= minimum`), documented as exact "for yt-dlp's own YYYY.MM.DD version scheme: the fields are fixed-width and zero-padded". That assumption holds for yt-dlp's own output but not for the operator-supplied `YTDL_MIN_YTDLP_VERSION` env var (ytdl/web/ytdlweb/config.py:158), which is free text. The two rankings disagree on any unpadded value, and they disagree in the worst direction: the server refuses the claim while the companion concludes it has nothing to update.

**Failure scenario:** An admin raises the floor to `2026.8.5` (no leading zero) after a YouTube break. Companion side: `parse_version('2026.08.11') = (2026,8,11)` vs `(2026,8,5)` -> not older -> `ensure()` publishes `ok:true, action=none, 'yt-dlp 2026.08.11 is current'`, so the sidecar never runs `-U` and `capabilities()` keeps answering ok:true. Server side: `'2026.08.11' >= '2026.8.5'` is False ('0' < '8' at index 5) -> every claim from every machine in the fleet 403s. Local downloads are silently dead fleet-wide, every job falls back to the NAS IP the feature exists to avoid, and the only log line anywhere says the yt-dlp is old while the companion insists it is current.

**Suggested fix:** Make one of them authoritative. Cheapest: normalise the floor on the server (`'.'.join(f'{int(p):02d}' ...)` where every part is numeric, else keep as-is) before comparing, or simply replace `_version_at_least` with the same tuple comparison `upgrade.parse_version` does and pin the pair in `server/tests/test_cross_component.py` alongside the existing ytdl_common parity assertions. Either way, reject a `MIN_YTDLP_VERSION` that does not parse at import time rather than letting it silently mean "refuse everyone".

**Verifier:** The divergence is real: ytdlp_manager.version_is_older (:264-276) ranks via upgrade.parse_version (upgrade.py:313-336), which splits on '.' and compares int tuples, while routes_fleet._version_at_least (:159-169) does a raw string >= comparison, documented as exact only because 'the fields are fixed-width and zero-padded' — true of yt-dlp's own output, not of the operator-supplied YTDL_MIN_YTDLP_VERSION (ytdl/web/ytdlweb/config.py:158, free-text env var with no validation anywhere). With an unpadded floor like 2026.8.5 the server 403s every claim while every companion concludes it is current, and there is no import-time rejection of an unparseable floor. Two caveats that cap the severity at the minor the hunter gave it: it requires operator misconfiguration, and the consequence is that every job falls back to the NAS worker — the documented, safe degradation (§10 rollback story), not data corruption; the 'no self-heal' angle is also weak, since in the example the companion's yt-dlp is genuinely newer and `-U` would change nothing.

### COMP-BROLL-10 (minor, upgrade) — The executor claims first and learns what the job is second, so every out-of-scope job costs a full 180 s lease before the server can start it
`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:930`

Goal: dispatch a job to whichever executor can actually run it. Current approach: the companion takes the lease (`claim`, :892) and only then fetches the manifest and discovers the quality (:929), the project label (:942) and whether the destination resolves (:946). Every one of those early-outs ends by stopping the heartbeat and letting the lease expire -- documented, and the "no release endpoint" reasoning behind it is sound and not what I am questioning. The avoidable part is that the SERVER already knows all three facts at claim time and grants a lease it will have to reclaim anyway. Concrete cost: 180 s of dead time per affected job plus a reclaim log line, and the SPA has already shown the editor the "downloading on your machine" badge (§9) which then flips back -- exactly the "silent executor swap" §9 says editors read as broken.

**Failure scenario:** An editor runs a 2160p job (the UI offers 1440p/2160p/best; only 480p/720p/1080p are in SCOPE_QUALITIES). The claim succeeds, `download_mode` flips to local, the badge says "downloading on your machine", the manifest arrives, the quality is out of scope, the companion returns having posted nothing -- and the job sits completely still for up to 180 s before `claim_next_job` can see it again. Same for a job whose project the machine does not sync, and for a machine whose `local_root`/`canonical_prefix` cannot resolve the destination.

**Suggested fix:** Declare the executor's scope in the claim instead of discovering it after: send `scope_qualities: list(SCOPE_QUALITIES)` and the machine's synced project labels (or just `is_base_rig`) in the claim body, and have `routes_fleet.claim` 410 with `reason='out_of_scope'` when `job['quality']` is not in the offered list or `job['project_label']` is not one the caller syncs. That reuses the existing 410 path, needs no new endpoint, and turns "three minutes of a job sitting still" into an immediate server start -- while leaving the lease-expiry reclaim as the single recovery path it is today for genuine failures.

**Verifier:** Upgrade is viable. The sequencing is as described — claim at :892, then manifest (:914), template check (:922), quality/SCOPE_QUALITIES (:929-940), label (:942), destination (:946) — and every early-out just stops the heartbeat and lets the <=180 s lease expire (:901-911, doc 'no release endpoint'). The server does hold quality and project_label at claim time, ClaimIn is a pydantic model with defaulted fields so adding scope_qualities is backward-compatible in both directions, and a 410 reason='out_of_scope' reuses the existing branch — no new endpoint, no conflict with §8 (that rule constrains the BROWSER, not the token-authed companion) or with the single-8899-listener rule. The dead time is not hypothetical: ytdl/web/static/app.js:1219-1239 dispatches on job_id alone after a capability probe that carries no scope, so every 1440p/2160p/best/audio job pays the full lease. Two weaknesses in the write-up: the §9 argument is backwards (§9 objects to a SILENT swap; the badge flipping back is the designed remedy, and renderMode is built to do exactly that), and only the quality half is cleanly server-checkable — the label check would need the companion to ship its synced labels and the destination check cannot move server-side at all.

## YTDL-WEB — YTDL web service

### YTDL-WEB-1 (major, logic) — CANCEL is a silent no-op for the entire duration of a local (leased) download
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_api.py:746`

`cancel` can only take two actions: `db.cancel_now()` (which requires phase IN ('queued','ready_for_review')) or `db.request_cancel()` + `worker.nudge()`. A job being downloaded by an editor's companion is at phase `downloading`, so only the flag is set — and the flag is honoured in exactly one place, `worker.run_job` (worker.py:234), which the worker reaches only via `_tick` → `db.claim_next_job`. `claim_next_job` (db.py:551-556) deliberately hides any job with `download_mode='local'` and an unexpired lease. Nothing on the fleet side reads `cancel_requested`: neither `claim`, `heartbeat` (routes_fleet.py:256), `download_manifest`, nor `clip_status`. The companion heartbeats every 30 s and `heartbeat_download` (db.py:647) extends the lease unconditionally, so the lease never expires while the executor is alive and the worker never gets the job back. This is the YTDL-1 shape recurring on the new code path, and no test in test_local_download.py posts a cancel (grep: zero hits for `cancel` outside a phase parametrize).

**Failure scenario:** Editor submits a 41-clip job, presses DOWNLOAD, their 0.8.0 companion claims the lease and starts fetching. Two clips in they realise they picked the wrong project and press CANCEL. `POST /api/jobs/{id}/cancel` returns {ok:true,phase:'downloading'}, the SPA toasts "cancelling — it stops after the video in flight" (app.js:1161), and the cancel button hides only on terminal phases (app.js:749). Nothing stops: all 41 clips are downloaded onto the editor's disk, uploaded by lane A into the wrong project's canonical tree, and ledgered fleet-wide as `done`. Only when the last clip's status post triggers `_hand_back_to_the_server` (routes_fleet.py:432) does the lease end, the worker pick the job up, and `run_job` finally flip it to `cancelled` — after every byte the editor cancelled has landed.

**Suggested fix:** Make the lease respect the stop signal: have `db.heartbeat_download` add `AND cancel_requested=0 AND (mode_lock IS NULL OR mode_lock<>'server')` to its WHERE clause so the heartbeat 410s and the companion stops, and have `clip_status`/`download_manifest` return 410 when `cancel_requested` is set. The lease then expires within LEASE_SECONDS and the worker's existing `run_job` cancel branch finishes the job. Add `cancel_requested=0` to `claim_download`'s WHERE as well so a job with a pending cancel is never handed out.

**Verifier:** Verified end to end. routes_api.py:730-748 cancel() -> db.cancel_now (db.py:508, WHERE phase IN ('queued','ready_for_review') only) else db.request_cancel + nudge. cancel_requested is read in exactly two places: worker.run_job (worker.py:233) and worker._cancelled inside the phases; db.claim_next_job (db.py:551-556) hides any download_mode='local' job with an unexpired lease, and run_job returns early on db.lease_active (worker.py:245) at the other door. grep for 'cancel' across routes_fleet.py returns ZERO hits, so claim (routes_fleet.py:172), heartbeat (256), download_manifest (280) and clip_status (352) never look at it; db.heartbeat_download (db.py:645-655) extends on (download_mode='local' AND claimed_by=? AND lease_expires_at>?) with no cancel/mode_lock predicate, so a live companion renews forever. Companion side confirms there is no other stop signal: ytdl_executor.py:518-520/547 stops only on HTTP 410, and default_request (ytdl_executor.py:154) has no polling of job state. app.js:749 hides #cancel only on job.terminal and app.js:1161 toasts 'cancelling - it stops after the video in flight'. Not documented anywhere as deliberate: docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md contains no occurrence of 'cancel', and docs/youtube_dlp_bugs.md YTDL-1 is the same shape fixed only for the review/queued phases. Endgame is as described: the cancel lands only after _hand_back_to_the_server (routes_fleet.py:432) -> end_lease -> nudge -> run_job's cancel branch.

### YTDL-WEB-2 (major, logic) — "Download on the server instead" does nothing while the download it is offered for is running, and a test pins that
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_api.py:714`

`mode_lock` is consulted in exactly one place, `db.claim_download`'s WHERE clause (db.py:639). `db.lock_mode` (db.py:707) deliberately does not touch a running lease, and `db.heartbeat_download` does not consult `mode_lock` either — so a companion that is already heartbeating keeps renewing its lease forever and the lock never takes effect. The route's own docstring (routes_api.py:701-703) claims "the lock takes effect at the next claim (or the next reclaim), which is at most YTDL_LEASE_SECONDS away"; that is only true if the holder stops heartbeating, i.e. only in the case the escape hatch is not for. Worse, the SPA shows the button ONLY in the state where it is inert: `renderMode` (app.js:1262) does `btn.classList.toggle('hidden', !live || mode !== 'local')`, and `download_mode` is `'local'` only after a claim. `test_locking_a_running_download_lets_it_finish` (tests/test_local_download.py:587) pins this: it asserts the heartbeat still returns 200 after the lock, then calls `_expire_lease()` by hand to make the 410 appear — an expiry that cannot happen in production while the executor lives.

**Failure scenario:** Editor is on hotel wifi with a 9 GB, 86-clip job running locally. They click "download on the server instead". `POST /api/jobs/{id}/mode-lock` returns 200 and the SPA toasts "handing this job back to the server — it picks up whatever your machine has not finished" (app.js:1277). Their companion's next heartbeat 200s, the lease is extended to now+180 s, and all 86 clips continue over the hotel connection to completion; the badge keeps reading "downloading on your machine" the whole time. The only thing the click achieved is that the job can never be claimed again after it finishes.

**Suggested fix:** Add `AND (mode_lock IS NULL OR mode_lock<>'server')` to `db.heartbeat_download`'s WHERE clause (and to `download_manifest`/`clip_status`'s leaseholder check) so the lock ends the lease at the next heartbeat: the companion gets its documented 410-and-stop, the lease expires within one interval, and `_reclaim_local_job` picks up only what is missing — which is exactly what the toast promises. Then rewrite test_local_download.py:587 to assert the heartbeat 410s after the lock instead of forcing the expiry by hand.

**Verifier:** Every mechanical claim verified. mode_lock is read only at db.py:639 (claim_download) and routes_fleet.py:207 (claim's pre-check); grep shows no other reader. db.lock_mode (db.py:707-716) is a bare UPDATE of the column; db.heartbeat_download (db.py:645) has no mode_lock predicate, so the running lease renews indefinitely. app.js:1262 `btn.classList.toggle('hidden', !live || mode !== 'local')` shows the button only when job.download_mode==='local', which only claim_download sets - i.e. only after the claim the docstring says the lock is meant to precede. tests/test_local_download.py:587 test_locking_a_running_download_lets_it_finish does assert heartbeat 200 after the lock and then calls _expire_lease() by hand. One correction to the hunter's framing: the 'a running lease stands until it expires' behaviour IS a documented deliberate decision (routes_api.py:701-703 + that test), and docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §9 says the link 'sets mode_lock=server BEFORE claim'. So the suggested fix (410 the heartbeat) contradicts the documented decision; the real, confirmed defect is that the UI surfaces the button only in the state where the lock is inert, and the toast at app.js:1277 promises an immediate hand-back that cannot happen.

### YTDL-WEB-3 (major, bug) — download-manifest skips the pre-download dedupe re-check, so the local executor re-downloads clips the fleet already has
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:319`

The handler's docstring says the clip list is "THE SAME selection query the worker's download phase reads, so the two executors cannot disagree about what is still owed". That is only half of what the worker does. `worker._phase_download` reads `db.pending_videos` AND then, per clip and immediately before spending bandwidth, re-checks both halves of the dedupe (worker.py:960-981): `db.ledger_get(c, vid)` and `ytsearch.existing_id_locations(outdir)`, marking a hit `skipped/duplicate` instead of fetching it. `download_manifest` does neither — it returns `db.pending_videos` raw — and `mark_pending` (db.py:882) has no dedupe predicate either, so nothing between the review grid and the local executor applies REQ 6. `clip_status`'s `_record_done` then calls `db.ledger_add` unconditionally (routes_fleet.py:418), and `ledger_add` UPSERTs on video_id, so the ledger row is *moved* to the new project.

**Failure scenario:** Editor A's manifest sits at `ready_for_review` for three days (a normal wait — `active_job` deliberately counts that as active). On day two editor B's job downloads clip `SAQBbd1Rxmo` into `2026/FF5/Energy Transition`. On day three A presses DOWNLOAD for `2026/FF5/Creator Profiles`; their companion claims and gets a manifest still containing `SAQBbd1Rxmo`, fetches it again from YouTube, and writes a second copy into Creator Profiles. `_record_done` upserts the ledger row so it now says the clip is in Creator Profiles; B's copy in Energy Transition is orphaned and every later ALREADY-IN badge points at the wrong project. Had A's job run on the server (the same job, same rows) the worker would have marked it `skipped, duplicate_of='2026/FF5/Energy Transition'` and spent no bandwidth at all.

**Suggested fix:** Run the same two-source re-check inside `download_manifest` before building the clip list: for each pending row, `db.ledger_get` then the `existing_id_locations` scan of the job's own term folder; write the `skipped/duplicate/duplicate_of` row exactly as worker.py:979 does and omit the clip from `clips`. Doing it there also keeps the two executors' row writes identical, which is the stated contract of the endpoint.

**Verifier:** download_manifest (routes_fleet.py:280-320) builds clips straight from db.pending_videos (db.py:904 - a raw dl_state='pending' SELECT) with no ledger or disk re-check, while worker._phase_download does both immediately before spending bandwidth: db.ledger_get at worker.py:955 and ytsearch.existing_id_locations(outdir) at worker.py:975, writing dl_state='skipped', duplicate=1, duplicate_of=... at 979. db.mark_pending (db.py:896) filters on selected/duplicate/meta_error/dl_state only - no dedupe predicate - and mark_duplicates runs back at the filter phase, before the review wait. The companion has no dedupe of its own (grep for existing_id/landed_file before download in ytdl_executor.py: landed_file is only called AFTER yt-dlp runs, ytdl_executor.py:1063). db.ledger_add (db.py:1069-1078) is an ON CONFLICT(video_id) DO UPDATE, so the row is moved to the new project as claimed. The stale-manifest window is real: routes_api.py:635-660 explicitly contemplates a manifest sitting at review 'for a week'. Only nuance: for a search job the two copies land in different term folders, so yt-dlp's own 'already downloaded' skip cannot save it - the bandwidth is genuinely spent.

### YTDL-WEB-4 (major, logic) — TOCTOU between the worker's per-clip lease check and its dl_state write lets both executors fetch the same clip into the same folder
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/worker.py:949`

The worker checks `db.lease_active` at the TOP of each iteration (worker.py:949) but does not write `dl_state='downloading'` until line 987, after the ledger lookup and — for any clip not in the ledger — a full `ytsearch.existing_id_locations(outdir)` rglob (worker.py:975). A claim landing inside that window is invisible to the worker for the rest of the clip, and `download_manifest` (which the companion GETs immediately after claiming) selects on `dl_state='pending'`, so the clip is still in the manifest. Both executors then download it. `db.claim_download` does nothing to exclude a job with a row already in flight, and `test_a_claim_landing_mid_phase_makes_the_server_stand_down` (tests/test_local_download.py:470) only exercises the safe ordering — it fires the claim from inside the fake `download()`, i.e. after the row is already `downloading`.

**Failure scenario:** On the BASE RIG — which docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §10 designates the phase-2 pilot, and where P:\Projects and /projects are the same NAS directory — an editor presses DOWNLOAD on a pasted-links job. `start_download` nudges the worker, which enters `_phase_download` and begins clip 1's dedupe rglob; for a paste `outdir` is the project's entire `Youtube` tree (worker.py:914 with an empty term_dir), so that rglob over an NFS mount with thousands of files takes ~1 s. Meanwhile the SPA's 1 s loopback probe returns and the companion claims and pulls the manifest, which still lists clip 1. Two yt-dlp processes now write `Uploader - Title [id].f137.mp4.part` and the merged output into the identical directory: the fragments interleave, one of the two ends in "Giving up after 10 retries" or a truncated merge, and `_record_failure`/`_clear_partials` from one side deletes the other's resume state.

**Suggested fix:** Close the window at the row level rather than the job level: have the worker claim each clip with a compare-and-set (`UPDATE job_videos SET dl_state='downloading' WHERE ... AND dl_state='pending'`, skipping the clip when rowcount is 0) as the FIRST thing in the iteration, before the dedupe scan, and re-check `lease_active` after the scan. Alternatively have `claim_download` refuse (409) while the job has any row in `dl_state='downloading'`, so the companion retries a moment later once the worker has stood down.

**Verifier:** Window verified: worker.py:949 `if db.lease_active(db.get_job(c, job_id))` at the top of the iteration, then ledger_get (955) and, for a ledger miss, the full existing_id_locations rglob (975), and only at worker.py:987 is dl_state set to 'downloading'. Nothing else guards the row - db.claim_download (db.py:631-643) tests only phase/mode_lock/lease, never whether a row is in flight, and routes_fleet.claim (172-249) adds only phase/mode_lock/template/version checks. db.pending_videos selects dl_state='pending', so a manifest fetched inside that window still lists the clip. Concurrency is real, not theoretical: db.con() (db.py:297-313) is one SQLite connection per thread precisely because 'FastAPI dispatches sync endpoints across a threadpool and the pipeline worker is a thread of its own', and worker.ensure_started() runs it in the same uvicorn process (main.py lifespan). tests/test_local_download.py:470 does fire the claim from inside the fake download(), i.e. after the row is already 'downloading', so it pins only the safe ordering. Timing is plausible for clip 1: start_download nudges the worker and the SPA's PROBE_MS is 1000 ms (app.js:1190) before dispatchLocal, which lands the claim right around the rglob.

### YTDL-WEB-5 (major, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — The enrich phase re-fetches per-video metadata the flat search already returned, paying the exact call volume that bot-checked the fleet
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/worker.py:443`

GOAL: give the review grid a title, channel, duration, view count, thumbnail and upload date per candidate, and give the filter phase a duration.

CURRENT APPROACH: `_phase_search` keeps only `id`, `url` and `title` off each flat search entry (`db.add_video`, worker.py:403) and throws the rest away. `_phase_enrich` then selects every row with `duration is None` — which, because of that discard, is every row — and makes one full `yt_dlp.extract_info` call per candidate through `ytsearch.enrich`. At the default cap that is 100 player-API calls per job, deliberately throttled to one per 0.75 s (config.py:104), so a default job spends ~75 s of pure sleeping in this phase plus the request latency. Config.py:91-102 records that this phase, at 112 calls, is the one measured event that got the NAS's IP cut off, and it is the reason `CANDIDATE_CAPS` and `ENRICH_PAUSE` exist at all.

BETTER APPROACH: yt-dlp's `extract_flat: 'in_playlist'` YouTube search entries already carry `duration`, `view_count`, `channel`/`uploader` and `thumbnails` — the vestigial `parse_duration`/`fmt_duration` helpers in ytsearch.py:89-106 are left over from batch_dl.py consuming exactly those fields. Carry them into `add_video`, and `_phase_enrich`'s `todo` collapses to the handful of entries YouTube gave no duration for. The one field flat search genuinely lacks is `upload_date`, and it is used by nothing but a line in the review card — fetch it for the rows the editor actually SELECTS (typically 10-40 of 100, and after review, off the critical path) instead of for every candidate up front.

This is the same shape as the local-download change itself: stop making the bot-checkable IP do work whose answer is already in hand.

**Failure scenario:** Today, every 100-candidate search costs ~100 extra YouTube player-API calls and ~75 s of mandated sleeping to recover duration/channel/views/thumbnail values that arrived in the search response minutes earlier and were discarded — on the precise endpoint and at the precise volume documented (config.py:91, DEPLOY.md "Volume is still the trigger") as having cut the fleet off for hours on 2026-08-11. Reusing the flat fields would take a default job's metadata calls from ~100 to near zero pre-review and ~20-40 post-review, cutting both the wall-clock cost and the block risk without changing anything the editor sees.

**Suggested fix:** In `_phase_search`, pass the flat entry's `duration`, `view_count`, `channel`/`uploader` and best `thumbnails[-1]['url']` through `db.add_video` into the job_videos row. Leave `_phase_enrich`'s `todo` predicate as-is (`duration is None`) so it becomes a small top-up pass for the entries YouTube shorted, and move the `upload_date`-only fetch behind `mark_pending` so it runs over the selected subset. Keep ENRICH_PAUSE unchanged — the point is fewer calls, not faster ones.

**Verifier:** The premises check out: _phase_search keeps only id/url/title (worker.py:391-403 -> db.add_video, db.py:788-797 inserts video_id,url,title only); _phase_enrich's todo is `duration is None and not meta_error` (worker.py:442-444), which after that discard is every row; ytsearch.enrich does one full extract_info per entry (ytsearch.py:216) gated at ENRICH_PAUSE=0.75 s (config.py:104), and config.py:89-102 does record this phase at 112 calls as the measured block. parse_duration/fmt_duration (ytsearch.py:89-106) are indeed dead in-tree. What I could NOT establish, and what decides the finding: (a) no offline evidence that this deployment's flat entries actually carry duration/view_count/channel/thumbnails - ytsearch.search's own contract says 'candidate entries (id/url/title only)' (ytsearch.py:139) and no test fixture models a real flat entry, so the claim rests on yt-dlp behaviour I cannot exercise here; (b) the suggested fix under-states what enrich does. It is also the availability gate - worker.py:477-483 turns r['error'] into meta_error/relevance_note='unavailable' so the grid shows what the editor cannot have, and worker.py:472 raises BotCheckError off an enrich error, which is the pipeline's pre-review bot-check tripwire. Collapsing todo to 'duration is None' silently drops both for ~all candidates. (c) upload_date is not review-card-only: db.py:1183 writes it into the on-disk manifest.json for every done/skipped video, and skipped duplicates are excluded by mark_pending, so a post-selection fetch would leave holes there. Directionally a real saving, but not the drop-in win as written.

### YTDL-WEB-7 (minor, logic) — An orderly hand-back pins the job to the server forever, so every later DOWNLOAD retry silently runs on the NAS
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/db.py:681`

`end_lease` sets `mode_lock='server'` on the ordinary, successful completion path — the same one-way pin `reclaim_download` uses for an abandoned laptop. `mode_lock` is never cleared by anything: `start_download` (routes_api.py:635) revives a `done` job into `downloading` and re-queues its failed rows but leaves `mode_lock` alone, and `claim_download` refuses any job carrying it (db.py:639, 410 'mode_lock'). So the YTDL-16 retry path — the one the plan §2 step 7 explicitly expects to be used for clips that failed on an editor's IP — is permanently server-only for any job that ever ran locally, and the SPA's dispatch fails silently (a 410 from the claim is deliberately invisible, app.js:1235).

**Failure scenario:** An editor's 41-clip job runs locally; 3 clips fail on their wifi. The second-chance sweep retries them on the NAS and 2 more fail (the NAS IP is the bot-checkable one). The editor presses DOWNLOAD again on the `done` job to retry those 2. The SPA probes the companion, dispatches, the claim 410s on `mode_lock`, nothing is said, and the retry runs from the same NAS IP that just failed them — the exact IP the whole feature exists to avoid, with no way for the editor to get their own machine to try.

**Suggested fix:** Distinguish "the server is closing this job out" from "this job may never go local again". Either use a separate transient column for the close-out (or simply rely on `phase` — the worker's close-out only runs while phase is `downloading` and `claim_download` already requires that), or clear `mode_lock` in `start_download`, which is a fresh human request and the natural place to reset a per-run pin. Keep `reclaim_download`'s pin as-is; that one is genuinely one-way.

**Verifier:** Verified. db.end_lease (db.py:679-681) sets mode_lock='server' on the ordinary successful close-out, identically to db.reclaim_download (db.py:699-701). grep for mode_lock across ytdl/web (excluding tests) returns only db.py:639/681/701/715 and routes_fleet.py:207 - nothing clears it, and start_download (routes_api.py:632-679) clears cancel_requested (db.clear_cancel) but never mode_lock while reviving a 'done' job to 'downloading'. routes_fleet.claim:207 then 410s with reason 'mode_lock', and app.js:1235 dispatchLocal swallows every non-202, so the YTDL-16 retry path documented at routes_api.py:635-643 is permanently server-only for any job that ever ran locally. end_lease's own docstring justifies the pin only transiently ('so nothing can re-claim a job whose close-out the worker is now performing'), and docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md:121-123 scopes the one-way rule to reclaim, not to an orderly hand-back. One softening: it is not wholly silent - renderMode (app.js:1256) still shows 'downloading on the server', so the editor sees where it runs, just not that their machine was refused.

### YTDL-WEB-8 (minor, bug) — A locally-downloaded clip is ledgered with whatever channel the row already had, so pasted-link jobs get a NULL channel
`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:418`

`_record_done` passes `row['channel']` to `db.ledger_add`, and `ClipStatusIn` has no `channel` field at all — the companion can report `title` and `thumbnail` but not the uploader. The server path takes it straight from yt-dlp: `res.get('channel') or v['channel']` (worker.py:1063). For a `kind='urls'` job the rows are written by `create_url_job` from a pasted link with no metadata fetch behind them (db.py:396-403 writes only video_id/url/title), so `row['channel']` is NULL — worker.py:1050-1053 documents this precisely, that yt-dlp is the first thing to learn the title and channel for those rows. The `.credits.json` sidecar on disk is unaffected (the companion writes it from its own info dict via `ytdl_common.credits_sidecar`), so this is a divergence in the fleet ledger only.

**Failure scenario:** An editor pastes 12 YouTube links, presses GET LINKS, and their companion executes the download. Each `clip_status` done post carries a title but no channel, so `_record_done` ledgers all 12 with `channel=NULL` and the fleet download-history panel shows a blank uploader column for them — while the identical paste executed on the NAS fills it in. Anyone auditing "who made this clip" from the ledger has to open the sidecar instead.

**Suggested fix:** Add `channel: str | None = None` to `ClipStatusIn` (it is already in the info dict the companion writes the sidecar from) and use `body.channel or row['channel']` in `_record_done`, mirroring worker.py:1062-1063. Pin it in the cross-component parity test alongside the sidecar fields.

**Verifier:** ClipStatusIn (routes_fleet.py:322-346) has fields state/error/note/title/thumbnail/filepath_rel and no channel; _record_done (routes_fleet.py:418) passes row['channel'] into db.ledger_add, while the server path uses res.get('channel') or v['channel'] (worker.py:1062-1063). db.create_url_job (db.py:397-403) inserts only job_id,video_id,url,title,selected,duplicate,duplicate_of,dl_state - no channel - and routes_api.create_url_job (403-470) does no metadata fetch at all (it only runs the ledger half of the dedupe), so a pasted-link row's channel is NULL. Confirmed divergence in the fleet ledger only; the sidecar is written companion-side from its own info dict. Two corrections worth carrying into the fix: the shipped companion sends only filepath_rel and note on a done post (ytdl_executor.py:1077-1078) - it never sends title or thumbnail either - so a pasted-link local job also ledgers a NULL/absent title, which worker.py:1050-1053 calls out as the case where yt-dlp is the first thing to learn the title.

## DASH — Fleet dashboard

### DASH-1 (major, logic) — adopt_folder's "no nested projects" guard is blinded by the very marker it is adopting
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1353`

`adopt_folder` (and `create_tree_project` at api.py:1282) checks for marked DESCENDANTS with `provision.scan_project_dirs(projects_dir)`, which prunes its os.walk at every marker it finds. When the folder being adopted already carries a marker, the scan yields that folder and stops — so any real project underneath it is invisible and `child_rel.startswith(rel + "/")` never fires. This is exactly the hole `provision.marked_descendants()` was written for; the collector's `_creatable()` (collector.py:570) correctly uses it, and provision.py:250-257 documents the failure mode verbatim. The two dashboard call sites are the only ones that did not get the fix. (`_marked_ancestor(projects_dir, rel)` also passes here because it returns `rel` itself, and `marked != rel` is False.)

**Failure scenario:** Someone hand-drops `.ccsync-project` on `Projects/2026/CCT/` (a plain JSON file on a share every editor can write), which already contains three real project folders each with their own marker. An admin opens /project-setup, browses to `2026/CCT` and clicks [ USE THIS FOLDER ]. `_marked_ancestor` returns "2026/CCT" == rel → allowed. `scan_project_dirs` returns `[("2026/CCT", <container slug>)]` and prunes, so the descendant loop sees nothing → adopt succeeds, a projects row is written for the container, and the next provision cycle asks Syncthing for a folder whose path CONTAINS three other folders' paths (nested folders, or a 400). The three real projects simultaneously vanish from discovery because scan_project_dirs prunes at the container's marker.

**Suggested fix:** Replace both `for child_rel, _ in provision.scan_project_dirs(projects_dir): if child_rel.startswith(rel + "/")` loops (api.py:1282 and api.py:1353) with `below = provision.marked_descendants(target)` — the scoped look-below the collector already uses — and raise on a non-empty result. It is also strictly cheaper: it walks only the candidate directory instead of the whole depth-8 Projects tree on every link/create POST.

**Verifier:** Mechanism verified. api.py:1353 `for child_rel, child_slug in provision.scan_project_dirs(projects_dir): if child_rel != rel and child_rel.startswith(rel + '/')` — scan_project_dirs (provision.py:294, os.walk with `dirnames[:] = []` at every marker) prunes AT the container's own marker, so when the adopted folder already carries a marker the scan yields exactly ('2026/CCT', slug) and nothing below it; the loop can never fire. `_marked_ancestor` (api.py:1188 → provision.marked_ancestor include_self=True) returns rel itself, and `marked != rel` is False, so that guard passes too. Same hole at api.py:1282 in create_tree_project when the container's existing marker slug happens to equal slugify(rel) (the 'partial-create convergence' branch at api.py:1291-1295). provision.marked_descendants (provision.py:250-257) documents this failure mode verbatim and collector._creatable (collector.py:570) uses it; these two are indeed the only call sites that did not get it. Tests confirm the gap: test_project_setup.py:328 only covers the UNMARKED container (which scan_project_dirs does catch), test_provision.py:434 covers the collector side, and no test links/creates over a MARKED container. Two corrections to the reported blast radius: (a) the 'next provision cycle asks Syncthing for a nested folder or 400s' half does NOT happen — collector._creatable calls marked_descendants and refuses, loudly, every cycle (test_provision.py:434 pins it); (b) the three real projects vanish from discovery because of the hand-dropped marker itself, not because of the adopt. Residual real harm: adopt/create writes a projects row + resolve-project mapping for a container that the collector will then refuse to provision forever, i.e. a project that silently never syncs. Suggested fix (marked_descendants(target)) is the right one and is also cheaper.

### DASH-2 (major, logic) — The container healthcheck only asserts HTTP 200, so a dead collector never marks the dashboard unhealthy
`dashboard/deploy/compose.yaml:287`

The healthcheck is `sys.exit(0 if urllib.request.urlopen('.../api/v1/health').status == 200 else 1)` — it never reads the response body. `api_health` (api.py:597-618) returns HTTP 200 unconditionally; the liveness signal is the `ok` field, which is `syncthing_reachable or not syncthing_url`, and `db.fetch_collector_status` (db.py:1863-1871) deliberately clears `syncthing_reachable` once the newest non-prune poll run is older than COLLECTOR_STALE_SECONDS. So the one thing the check exists to catch is the one thing it cannot see. Both sides document the opposite: compose.yaml:282-285 says it exists because "a collector thread that died before entering its guarded loop … leaves a container that answers nothing useful forever", and api_health's docstring says "Docker's healthcheck (see deploy/compose.yaml) uses syncthing_reachable". It does not.

**Failure scenario:** The collector thread dies (an unguarded exception before its loop, or a wedged Syncthing client). poll_runs stops advancing; after COLLECTOR_STALE_SECONDS `/api/v1/health` starts answering `{"ok": false, "version": …}` — with status 200. The healthcheck keeps exiting 0, the container stays `healthy`, `restart: unless-stopped` never fires, and the fleet page silently serves hours-old completion percentages with no banner (the topbar's SYNCTHING UNREACHABLE banner needs a `view`, which /partials/* renders do not always carry). Nobody is paged.

**Suggested fix:** Parse the body in the healthcheck: `python -c "import json,urllib.request,sys; sys.exit(0 if json.load(urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8480/api/v1/health', timeout=5)).get('ok') else 1)"`. The unauthenticated branch of api_health already returns `ok`, so no credentials are needed. Alternatively make api_health answer 503 when `ok` is false and keep the status-only probe — but then say so in both comments.

**Verifier:** compose.yaml:287 is `sys.exit(0 if urllib.request.urlopen('http://127.0.0.1:8480/api/v1/health', timeout=5).status == 200 else 1)` — body is never read. api_health (api.py:597-633) returns a plain dict on every path, so FastAPI answers 200 whether `ok` is True or False; the unauthenticated branch (api.py:617-620) returns ok = syncthing_reachable or not syncthing_url, and db.fetch_collector_status (db.py:1862-1870, COLLECTOR_STALE_SECONDS=180 at db.py:311) sets reachable=False once the newest non-prune poll run is older than that. So the dead-collector signal exists in the body and is invisible to the probe. Both comments do claim otherwise: compose.yaml:282-285 ('a collector thread that died before entering its guarded loop … answers nothing useful forever') and api.py:625-627 ('Docker's healthcheck (see deploy/compose.yaml) uses syncthing_reachable, which this clears'). Searched CLAUDE.md, KNOWN_BUGS.md, docs/GOTCHAS.md (incl. the /api/v1/health section at :230), docs/SERVER.md:321-323 and docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md — the only documented decision is that the UNAUTHENTICATED body is trimmed to {ok,version}; nothing documents a deliberate status-only probe. One overstatement: the topbar SYNCTHING UNREACHABLE banner (partials/topbar.html:41, `{% if view and not view.syncthing_reachable %}`) DOES appear on any full page load, since _sidebar_context supplies `view`; it is only the open tab's /partials/* polls that never re-render the topbar.

### DASH-3 (major, bug) — Package publish stages every upload under one fixed `.part` name and unlinks it on any exception
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1829`

`api_publish_package` stages the body at `dest_dir / (filename + ".part")`, a name derived only from (kind, platform, version) — identical for every attempt at the same version — and its `except Exception: part.unlink(missing_ok=True); raise` (api.py:1845-1847) deletes that path unconditionally, including on the `ClientDisconnect` that `request.stream()` raises when an upload is abandoned.
