Nothing serialises two in-flight PUTs for the same version: the duplicate check (`db.get_package`) runs long before the DB row is written, and every `await run_in_threadpool(fh.write, chunk)` is a yield point, so two requests interleave into the same file handle while each computes sha256 over only its own bytes. The sha256 gate therefore passes for a file on disk that matches neither upload.

**Failure scenario:** An admin runs `build_editor_package.ps1 -Publish` for companion 0.7.8 over Tailscale; the ~40 MB PUT stalls and they Ctrl-C and re-run it while uvicorn is still draining the first request. Request B opens and starts writing `ccsync-companion-0.7.8.exe.part`; request A's stream then raises ClientDisconnect and its handler unlinks that exact path. B finishes, its digest matches (it hashed its own chunks), and `os.replace(part, dest)` fails with FileNotFoundError → 500, no package published, and `ship.cmd`'s "already published" gate now has to be worked around (the R13 recovery path). In the interleaved-success variant both requests complete: the file on disk is a mix of both streams, the first INSERT wins the UNIQUE (kind, platform, version) row with a sha256 that does not describe it, and if `make_current=1` every companion in the fleet downloads a build that fails its own sha256 verification.

**Suggested fix:** Stage to a per-request unique path — `tempfile.mkstemp(dir=dest_dir, prefix=filename + ".", suffix=".part")` or `f"{filename}.{uuid4().hex}.part"` — so concurrent or retried publishes can never share a handle or delete each other's staging file, and keep the existing `os.replace` for the atomic swap. Optionally take an asyncio.Lock keyed on (kind, platform, version) around the get_package→insert window so the duplicate check is not a TOCTOU.

**Verifier:** api.py:1829 `part = dest_dir / (filename + '.part')` where filename = _package_filename(kind, platform, version) — no request-unique component — and api.py:1844-1846 `except Exception: part.unlink(missing_ok=True); raise` catches ClientDisconnect from `async for chunk in request.stream()` along with everything else, deleting that fixed path. Nothing serialises publishes: the only threading.Lock in the package is collector.py:107 (_nudge_lock), the duplicate gate `db.get_package(...)` runs at api.py:1813 long before insert_companion_package at api.py:1870, and every `await run_in_threadpool(fh.write, chunk)` is an await point, so two concurrent PUTs for the same (kind, platform, version) each open the same path 'wb' and interleave while each hashes only its own bytes — the sha256 gate at api.py:1857 then passes for a file that matches neither stream. tests/test_packages.py has no concurrency or staging-collision coverage (only :363-372, that no .part survives an over-size body). Admin-only and requires two overlapping uploads of the same version, so it is narrow, but the retry-after-Ctrl-C path described is a realistic trigger and the file-corruption variant would ship a build whose recorded sha256 does not describe it. Per-request staging name is the correct fix.

### DASH-4 (major, bug) — An expired session makes every htmx poller swap the full login page into a page fragment
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/app.py:307`

`login_gate` answers `/api/`, `/broll/api/`, `/broll/media/`, `/music/api/` and `/ytdl/api/` with a 401 JSON body precisely because those callers cannot follow a 303 to an HTML login page — but `/partials/*` is not in that list and falls through to `RedirectResponse("/login?next=…", 303)`. htmx uses XHR, which follows redirects transparently: the poller receives 200 with the whole `<!doctype html>` login document and swaps it into its target. Every self-refreshing fragment on every page does this (`/partials/sidebar` 30 s, `/partials/transfers` 2 s, `/partials/project/{slug}` 10 s, `/partials/project/{slug}/bins` 5 s, `/partials/fleet` 15 s, `/partials/admin/*` 30 s), and the swap targets are `<aside class="sidebar">`, `<main class="main">`, `#project-detail` and `#media-presence`.

**Failure scenario:** An editor leaves /transfers open overnight and the 7-day session cookie expires (or an admin redeploys with a rotated DASH_SESSION_SECRET, which auth.py warns logs everyone out). Two seconds later the `/partials/transfers` poll gets the 303, XHR follows it, and htmx swaps a complete login page — nested `<html>`, `<head>`, a second copy of the topbar — inside `<main class="main">`. The page keeps polling every 2 s, the stale topbar still shows the old username, and nothing ever navigates the browser to /login; the user sees a garbled page reporting nothing about their transfers and has to work out to reload by hand.

**Suggested fix:** Give htmx requests a fragment-safe answer. Detect the `HX-Request: true` header in login_gate and reply `204` (or `401`) with `HX-Redirect: /login?next=<target>`, which htmx turns into a real browser navigation; keep the 303 for plain document GETs so the companion's /project-setup deep link still works. Adding `/partials/` to the JSON-401 prefix tuple alone is not enough — htmx would swap nothing and the page would just freeze silently.

**Verifier:** app.py:299-309: the JSON-401 prefix tuple is exactly ('/api/', '/broll/api/', '/broll/media/', '/music/api/', '/ytdl/api/'); '/partials/' is absent, so an expired session on any partial falls to `RedirectResponse(f'/login?next={...}', status_code=303)` at app.py:309. '/login' is in _OPEN_EXACT (app.py:29) and page_login (ui.py:161-166) renders the full base.html document, so the XHR — which follows 3xx transparently — gets 200 + a complete <!doctype html> page and htmx swaps it into the fragment target. The pollers are real and match the report: transfers.html:8 (every 2s into <main class="main">), project.html:12/:17 (10s / 5s), fleet.html:7/:13/:17/:21, admin_users.html:4/:8/:11, all hx-swap=innerHTML. Nothing intercepts it: the only htmx listeners in the codebase are base.html:22 and :31 (details open/closed persistence), and there is no HX-Request / HX-Redirect handling anywhere in src/ or templates/ (grepped). No test covers /partials/* under an expired session (test_auth.py only pins the 303 for document GETs at :81-83). The HX-Redirect fix is the standard one and does not disturb the /project-setup deep-link behaviour, which is a document GET.

### DASH-5 (minor, bug) — One unreadable subfolder truncates the project-setup folder browser and hides [ USE THIS FOLDER ]
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ui.py:391`

`_browse_context` calls `child.iterdir()` for every child inside the shared `try:` block, purely to compute the cosmetic `has_children` flag (used only to pick the link text in project_setup_panel.html). An OSError from any single child — PermissionError on a directory the container's uid 3000/gid 3001 cannot read, or ESTALE/EIO on the NFS-backed `/projects` mount — aborts the whole loop and is caught by `except OSError` at ui.py:404. The already-collected `entries` are returned, so every alphabetically LATER sibling silently disappears from the picker, and because `can_link_current` requires `error is None` (ui.py:415), the [ USE THIS FOLDER ] button vanishes too.

**Failure scenario:** `Projects/2026/` contains `Aurora/`, `Bulkhead/` (created 0700 by a different uid during a hand-copy) and `CCT/`. An editor follows the companion's /project-setup deep link and browses to `2026`. `Bulkhead.iterdir()` raises PermissionError, so the panel shows only `Aurora`, a "could not list the folder" banner, and no way to pick the current folder — `CCT`, the project they came to link, is simply not on screen. Nothing in the message says a sibling was skipped or that the list is partial.

**Suggested fix:** Wrap only the per-child probe: `try: has_children = any(...) except OSError: has_children = False`, and keep listing. Better still, drop the probe — it costs one directory listing per child over the NAS mount on every browse AND on every link/create POST (`_render_setup_panel` rebuilds the panel), on top of one `read_marker()` open+read per child, so a Projects level with 80 folders is ~160 NFS round trips for a flag that only changes a link's label.

**Verifier:** ui.py:386-401: the `for child in sorted(target.iterdir(), ...)` loop is inside the same try whose handlers are at ui.py:402-405, and ui.py:391-393 calls `child.iterdir()` per child purely for the cosmetic has_children flag. PermissionError/ESTALE from one child is an OSError, so it exits the loop, sets `error`, and the already-appended (alphabetically earlier) entries are returned with no indication the list is partial. can_link_current at ui.py:415 is `bool(norm_rel) and error is None and (...)`, so [ USE THIS FOLDER ] disappears as well — both halves of the claim check out. has_children is only consumed to choose link text in project_setup_panel.html, and it is skipped for marked children (`if slug is None`), so the probe cost is per unmarked child only. Nothing in CLAUDE.md / KNOWN_BUGS.md / docs/GOTCHAS.md / docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md discusses this loop, and no test exercises an unreadable child. Minor severity is right — it needs a genuinely unreadable sibling — but the truncation-plus-vanishing-button behaviour is real, and the per-child try is a two-line fix.

### DASH-6 (minor, inefficiency) — The fleet page builds the entire fleet snapshot twice per render
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/ui.py:140`

`page_fleet` spreads `_sidebar_context(request, conn, None)` (which calls `build_projects_view`) and then calls `build_queue_view(conn, queue_editor)`, whose first act is `projects = {p["slug"]: p for p in build_projects_view(conn, now)["projects"]}` (api.py:689). So `build_projects_view` — `db.fetch_collector_status` plus `_lanes_by_editor` plus `db.fetch_projects`, which is itself N+1 (one `fetch_project_editors` query per project, db.py:1643-1644) — runs twice for one page, against two different `now` values. `partial_toggle`'s default branch pays the same cost again on every checkbox click, and `page_admin_users` stacks `build_packages_view` → `build_editors_view` on top of a third `build_projects_view`.

**Failure scenario:** With 12 active projects the fleet page issues ~2×(1 collector-status query + 1 lanes query + 1 projects query + 12 editor queries) = ~30 SQLite queries and two full Python view builds where one would do, on a uvicorn running `workers=1` that also has to answer every companion's `/api/v1/report`. The two snapshots are taken microseconds apart with independent `now` timestamps, so the sidebar's status dots and the queue panel's percentages are computed from two different reads of the same tables.

**Suggested fix:** Let `build_queue_view` take an optional prebuilt projects view (`build_queue_view(conn, editor, now=None, projects_view=None)`) and have `page_fleet` build it once and pass it to both `_sidebar_context` and `build_queue_view` — which also makes the two panels consistent by construction. Separately, collapse `db.fetch_projects`' N+1 into a single `completion_current JOIN devices JOIN projects` query grouped in Python.

**Verifier:** Core claim verified: ui.py:140 spreads _sidebar_context(request, conn, None), which at ui.py:675 calls build_projects_view(conn); ui.py:142 then calls build_queue_view(conn, queue_editor), whose first statement (api.py:689) is `projects = {p['slug']: p for p in build_projects_view(conn, now)['projects']}`. queue_editor is non-None for any logged-in viewer (_queue_editor, ui.py:666-676) and the page is login-gated, so the fleet page always builds the snapshot twice, with two independently-taken `now` values (build_projects_view defaults now=db.utcnow_iso()). build_projects_view is db.fetch_collector_status + _lanes_by_editor + db.fetch_projects, and fetch_projects is N+1 (db.py:1638-1645, one fetch_project_editors per project) — so the query-count argument holds. Two supporting details are WRONG and should not be carried forward: (1) page_admin_users (ui.py:806-812) builds build_projects_view exactly ONCE — build_packages_view (api.py:1723) does not call build_editors_view, and build_editors_view (api.py:512) does not call build_projects_view; (2) partial_toggle's default branch (ui.py:640-642) pays one build_projects_view via build_queue_view, not two. The proposed projects_view= parameter is viable and conflicts with nothing documented.

### DASH-7 (minor, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — The admin Users panel re-queries TrueNAS and Syncthing over REST every 30 s per open tab
`dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1441`

Goal: show which editor accounts exist and which Syncthing devices still need approving. Current approach: `build_admin_users_view` performs a live `truenas.list_editors()` (an authenticated HTTPS call carrying TRUENAS_PW) plus `syncthing.config()`, `syncthing.system_status()` and `syncthing.pending_devices()` on EVERY call — and admin_users.html:8 polls `/partials/admin/users` every 30 s, with `page_admin_users` doing one more on load. Concrete cost: four blocking backend round trips every 30 s per open admin tab, each occupying an anyio threadpool worker on the single-worker container, and each one re-authenticating to the NAS with the admin password; a tab left open all day makes ~2,880 TrueNAS calls and ~8,600 Syncthing calls for data that changes when someone plugs in a new machine. A TrueNAS blip turns the whole panel into an error banner with no last-known state to fall back on. Better approach: the collector ALREADY pulls the full Syncthing config every `interval_config` (120 s) and writes devices into the `devices` table, so the pending/unmapped half can be answered from SQLite with zero network I/O; and the TrueNAS editor roster can be refreshed on the same collector cadence into a small table (or an in-process TTL cache) so the page renders instantly, survives a NAS blip with a "as of HH:MM" stamp, and the write actions (create/approve/set-password) stay live calls that invalidate the cache.

**Failure scenario:** Today: an admin leaves /admin/users open while an editor's onboarding runs. Every 30 s the container makes four synchronous NAS calls; when the NAS is busy (a scrub, or the SMB probe pool saturated by /login) each can take seconds, and while they run a threadpool worker is unavailable for `/api/v1/report`. If TrueNAS answers slowly enough to raise, `build_admin_users_view` returns `{"error": "truenas: …"}` and the entire panel — including the Syncthing pending-device list that has nothing to do with TrueNAS — is replaced by a banner, losing the roster the admin was working from mid-task.

**Suggested fix:** Read the pending/unmapped device list from the `devices` table the collector already maintains instead of calling Syncthing per request, and add a collector kind (or a 60 s in-process TTL cache keyed on nothing, since workers=1) for `truenas.list_editors()`. Render the cached roster with a `generated_at` stamp on failure rather than blanking the panel, and invalidate on the create/approve/password POSTs so an admin's own action still shows immediately.

**Verifier:** The cost description is accurate: build_admin_users_view (api.py:1439-1501) constructs a fresh TrueNASClient and calls list_editors() on every invocation, then syncthing.config(), system_status() and pending_devices(); there is no cache anywhere; partial_admin_users (ui.py:815-820) and page_admin_users (ui.py:806) both call it, and admin_users.html:8 polls every 30s. The blanking claim is also right: a TrueNASError returns editors=[] AND pending_devices=[] (api.py:1459-1461), so a NAS blip wipes the Syncthing half of the panel too. But the central premise of the proposed fix does not hold. The devices table (schema.sql:15-26: device_id, name, editor_username, is_server, connected, address, timestamps) is populated by _run_config (collector.py:673+) from Syncthing's CONFIG only — a device from /rest/cluster/pending/devices is by definition not in the config, is never written to any table (pending_devices appears nowhere outside api.py and syncthing_client.py:139), and so cannot be served from SQLite without a new collector pass plus a schema column. Only the 'configured but unmapped' half is answerable from the DB today. Worse for the goal: the primary use of this panel is approving a machine someone just plugged in, and a collector-cadence cache (interval_config=120s, settings.py:108) makes exactly that case slower to appear. The TrueNAS-roster TTL cache + 'as of HH:MM' fallback half of the suggestion is viable and would fix the blanking; the zero-network-I/O pending-device half is not viable as written. Marking uncertain rather than confirmed because the upgrade as specified rests on a false claim about what the collector stores.

### DASH-8 (minor, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — Every live view is a fixed-interval full-fragment poll rather than a push from the collector that already knows when data changed
`dashboard/templates/transfers.html:8`

Goal: show the fleet's sync state as it changes. Current approach: each page hangs 3-5 independent htmx pollers off fixed intervals — `/partials/transfers` every 2 s, `/partials/project/{slug}/bins` every 5 s, `/partials/queue` and `/partials/project/{slug}` every 10 s, `/partials/fleet` every 15 s, `/partials/sidebar` + `/partials/project-roots` + both admin panels every 30 s. Every tick is a full HTTP request that opens a brand-new SQLite connection (`get_conn`, which also runs `PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL` per connection, db.py:355), rebuilds the whole view from scratch and re-renders the entire fragment, whether or not anything changed — against a `workers=1` uvicorn that is also the endpoint every companion in the fleet POSTs its report to. Concrete cost: one open /transfers tab is 30 req/min on its own; the base rig plus three editors with a project page open is ~150-200 req/min of pure re-render, and the data behind it only changes when the collector finishes a cycle (`interval_connections` 15 s, `interval_completion` 60 s) or a companion report lands. Better approach: one SSE endpoint per session (htmx's `sse` extension, or a tiny `EventSource` + `hx-trigger="sse:fleet"`), fed by the collector publishing a cheap event after each successful cycle and by `api_report` after each commit; clients then re-fetch only the fragments whose data actually moved. That is strictly less work than today and removes the current worst case entirely — a fragment that is 2 s stale by construction between two collector cycles that are 15-60 s apart.

**Failure scenario:** Today: five editors leave the dashboard open (the intended usage — "the dashboard is what tells everyone whether their footage is syncing"). The container is fielding ~10 fragment renders per second at peak, each opening and closing a SQLite connection and re-running `fetch_collector_status` + `build_projects_view`, while `/api/v1/report` from every companion queues behind them on the single worker. Meanwhile the /transfers view still cannot be fresher than the 15 s connections cycle, so the 2 s poll buys nothing but load — and the same load is what makes a slow `build_admin_users_view` or a 40 MB package upload visibly stall the fleet page.

**Suggested fix:** Add a `/events` SSE route that yields a named event when the collector completes a cycle (it already has the seam: `Collector.nudge()`/poll_runs) and when `api_report` commits, then change the templates' `hx-trigger="every Ns"` to `hx-trigger="sse:fleet"` / `sse:transfers` with a long (60 s) interval as a fallback. Keep the fragment routes exactly as they are — this changes only what decides to fetch them.

**Verifier:** The factual substrate checks out: every poller cited exists at the stated intervals (transfers.html:8 2s, project.html:17 5s, project.html:12 & fleet.html:13 10s, fleet.html:17 15s, the 30s sidebar/roots/admin ones), get_conn (api.py:91-96) opens and closes a fresh sqlite3 connection per request and db.connect (db.py:351-359) runs PRAGMA journal_mode=WAL + busy_timeout + foreign_keys on each one, and app.py's run() pins uvicorn workers=1. Nothing in CLAUDE.md, dashboard/README.md (:30 just describes 'vendored htmx polling') or the bug-hunt docs defends fixed-interval polling as a deliberate constraint, and no reverse proxy is configured in deploy/compose.yaml or docs/SERVER.md that would buffer SSE — so the approach is not blocked. What I cannot establish is that it is a net win, and two of the stated justifications are wrong: (a) '/transfers cannot be fresher than the 15s connections cycle' is false — its per-file rows come from active_transfers, written by companion /api/v1/report, and the companion reports every dashboard_report_interval_active = 5s (companion/src/ccsync_companion/config.py:185); only the lane-C rows follow the 60s completion cycle; (b) the '~10 fragment renders per second at peak' figure is inflated — a /transfers tab is 32 req/min and a project tab ~20 req/min, so five open tabs is ~2-3 req/s, matching their own '150-200 req/min' and not 10/s. The offsetting costs (N long-lived event-loop connections on a single worker, a thread-to-loop bridge from the collector thread, plus keeping the interval fallback) are not weighed. Direction is defensible; 'strictly less work' is not demonstrated.

## SERVER — NAS-side install/setup scripts

### SERVER-1 (critical, bug) — The SFTP half of every post-swap tree push is unguarded, so a dropped transport still escapes main() as a traceback (the exact OPS-3/OPS-2 hole run_ssh_guarded was added to close)
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:1710`

OPS-3's fix wrapped the *remote shell* steps in `run_ssh_guarded` (`:1626-1641`) so "a socket.timeout out of the swap step used to escape main() as an unhandled exception, so the container was never restarted". But the transfer itself was left bare. `upload_tree` opens a client at `:1710` and loops `sftp.put` at `:1726` inside a `try/finally` that only closes the client — there is no `except`. Same shape in `install_music_db` (`ssh_client` at `:938`, `sftp.put` at `:941`) and `install_binary_over_lan` (`:1494-1497`) and `install_ffmpeg_over_lan` (`:1199-1204`, whose follow-up `wc -c` at `:1206` is also a plain `run_ssh`, not `run_ssh_guarded`, despite `provision_ffmpeg`'s docstring promising "NON-FATAL throughout ... Every failure below prints a NOTE and returns"). The SFTP is by far the longest-lived operation in the whole script — 906 MB of proxies and 482 MB of encoder — i.e. the single most likely place for paramiko to raise, and it is the only major step with no error path at all.

**Failure scenario:** A `--music-data all` (or first-install) deploy: steps 2/2b/2c succeed, so `app` has already been renamed to `app.old.<T>` and the container is serving that inode. Step 2d reaches `install_tree(root, 'music-proxies', ...)`, `upload_tree` starts putting 906 MB, and eight minutes in the NAS's sshd drops the channel (ClientAlive, a NIC blip, a Tailscale re-handshake). `sftp.put` raises `paramiko.SSHException`/`socket.timeout`; nothing catches it in `upload_tree`, `install_tree`, or `main()`. The process dies with a traceback, `fail_after_app_swap()` never runs, the container is never restarted onto the freshly-installed `app`, and the operator gets a Python stack trace instead of the documented "the live tree is untouched, staging left at ... for inspection". ~906 MB of staging is also stranded under `<host-root>/staging` (see SERVER-5).

**Suggested fix:** Wrap the SFTP body of `upload_tree`, `install_music_db`, `install_ffmpeg_over_lan` and `install_binary_over_lan` in the same `except Exception` translation `run_ssh_guarded` uses — return a failure result (`-1`/`False`/`(False, msg)`) so the caller's existing `return fail_after_app_swap(args.dry_run)` path runs. Also route `install_ffmpeg_over_lan`'s `wc -c` (`:1206`) and `install_binary_over_lan`'s (`:1501`) through `run_ssh_guarded` so provision_ffmpeg's stated non-fatal contract is actually true.

**Verifier:** Verified. `upload_tree` (install_dashboard_app.py:1709-1731) opens `ssh_client` at :1710 and loops `sftp.put` at :1726 inside a bare `try/finally` whose only clause is `client.close()`; same shape in `install_music_db` (:938-944), `install_ffmpeg_over_lan` (:1199-1205) and `install_binary_over_lan` (:1494-1500). There is no module-level guard: the file ends `if __name__ == "__main__": sys.exit(main())` (:2542-2543) with no try/except, and `run_ssh` (common.py:521-555) has no internal exception translation — `run_ssh_guarded` (:1626-1641) is the only place that translates, and it wraps only the shell steps. So a paramiko/socket exception out of the SFTP loop escapes `upload_tree` -> `install_tree` -> `main()` as a traceback. Post-swap exposure is real: the `app` swap is step 2 (:2387) and `install_tree`/`install_music_db` for broll-web/music-web/music-data/ytdl-web run at :2395-2468, each guarded only by `return fail_after_app_swap(...)` on a False RETURN, which an exception bypasses. Two corrections to the finding's scope: (a) `provision_ffmpeg` (:2358) and `provision_ytdl_binaries` (:2364) run BEFORE the swap, so the ffmpeg/deno/claude SFTP paths abort the deploy with a traceback but do not leave the container on app.old.<ts>; (b) that half is still a real contract break — provision_ffmpeg's docstring at :1227-1230 says "NON-FATAL throughout ... Every failure below prints a NOTE and returns", and the unguarded `sftp.put` plus the plain `run_ssh` at :1206 / :1501 can make it fatal.

### SERVER-2 (major, bug) — POST /app create is never waited on, so the deploy prints "installed custom app" for a create job that fails asynchronously
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:2525`

`POST /api/v2.0/app` returns a JOB ID, not a finished install — this repo knows that and says so twice: `install_syncthing_app.py:224-239` waits on it citing AUDIT INST-24 ("A 2xx here only means the create job was ACCEPTED"), and this same file's `--recreate` DELETE path (`:2481-2490`) polls `wait_for_job`. The create at `:2525` does neither: `if not ok(resp): ...` then straight to `print(f"installed custom app: {APP_NAME} on port {args.port}")` and `return 0`. The failure modes this hides are named in this script's own docstring (`:136-139`): a stale `DASH_BIND_LAN`/`DASH_BIND_TAILNET` makes "Docker refuse to start the app with 'cannot assign requested address'" — which is an async job failure, not a rejected POST. Image pull failure of `python:3.12.7-slim` or `brainicism/bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider:1.3.1-deno` is the same shape.

**Failure scenario:** First install (or a `--recreate` after the NAS's DHCP lease moved): `DASH_BIND_LAN=192.168.0.102` no longer exists on the NAS. The middleware accepts the POST with 200 and an int job id; the compose up then fails with "cannot assign requested address". This script prints "installed custom app: ccsync-dashboard on port 8480" and exits 0, so `tools\ship.cmd` step 1 passes and proceeds to build and publish the companion fleet-wide. Nobody learns the fleet dashboard is not running until an editor reports it — the same exit-code-lies class as OPS-4.

**Suggested fix:** Copy `install_syncthing_app.py:227-243` verbatim: `job_id = resp.json()`; if `isinstance(job_id, int)`, `wait_for_job(job_id, timeout=900, poll=5)` and `return 1` on any state other than SUCCESS, printing the job error and the manual-fallback advice; otherwise print the same "could not be waited on — confirm it is running" NOTE.

**Verifier:** The code defect is real: install_dashboard_app.py:2525 `truenas_api("POST", "/app", ...)` is followed only by `if not ok(resp)` (:2529) and then the unconditional success print at :2536 and `return 0` at :2539 — no `resp.json()`/`wait_for_job`. The in-repo precedent is verbatim as cited: install_syncthing_app.py:224-243 waits on the job id with the comment "A 2xx here only means the create job was ACCEPTED ... (AUDIT INST-24)", and this same file's --recreate DELETE path polls `wait_for_job` at :2481-2490. `wait_for_job` is already imported at :192. I found no doc (KNOWN_BUGS.md, bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md, README) recording this as a deliberate exception. CORRECTION to the failure scenario: the ship.cmd blast radius is wrong. tools/ship.ps1:275-299 polls /api/v1/health after step 1 for up to 90 s and `Write-Fail`+`exit 1` unless the live version equals the repo's, so ship would NOT proceed to publish the companion fleet-wide. Also, on any host where the app already exists main() returns at :2504 via the restart path, so :2525 is first-install/--recreate only. The real exposure is an operator running the script directly on a fresh host and getting exit 0 for an async-failed create.

### SERVER-3 (major, bug) — check_editor_accounts compares the UNIX gid against /user's `groups` (which holds DATABASE ids), so check 6 always FAILs and check_health.py never exits 0 on a healthy NAS
`server/check_health.py:357`

`gid = groups[0]["gid"]` at `:351` is the **unix** gid. It is then tested with `gid in u.get("groups", []) or u.get("group") == gid` at `:357-358`. Both arms are wrong, and this repo documents why in two other places. `setup_editor_account.ensure_group` (`:126-133`) carries the explicit note: "the /user endpoints' `group`/`groups` fields take the group's database id, NOT its unix gid — passing the gid fails validation with 'This group does not exist' (learned against the live 25.10 API)", and it returns `(existing["id"], existing["gid"])` as two separate values precisely so they cannot be confused. The dashboard's canonical membership test agrees: `dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/truenas_client.py:165` is `gid in (user.get("groups") or []) or (user.get("group") or {}).get("id") == gid`, with `gid` bound from `existing["id"]` (`:135`) — note it also unwraps `group` as a **dict**, which `check_health.py`'s bare `== gid` never can.

**Failure scenario:** On the live NAS, `editors` has unix gid 3001 and some different database id (say 41). Every editor's `/user` row carries `groups: [41, ...]` and `group: {"id": 41, "bsdgrp_gid": 3001, ...}`. `41 in [...]` is never tested (3001 is), and `{"id": 41, ...} == 3001` is False, so `members` is empty and check 6 reports `FAIL: group 'editors' exists but has no members yet` even with six editors provisioned. Since `main()` returns `len(failed)`, `check_health.py` exits non-zero on a completely healthy server — the one command whose whole contract is "exit code = number of failed checks (0 = all good)".

**Suggested fix:** Take both ids from the group row and use the DB id for membership: `grp = groups[0]; db_id, unix_gid = grp["id"], grp["gid"]`, then `members = [u["username"] for u in resp.json() if db_id in (u.get("groups") or []) or (u.get("group") or {}).get("id") == db_id]` — identical to `truenas_client._in_editors_group`. Keep `unix_gid` only if it is printed.

**Verifier:** check_health.py:351 takes `gid = groups[0]["gid"]` (the UNIX gid from the /group row) and :357-358 tests `gid in u.get("groups", []) or u.get("group") == gid`. Both in-repo authorities say that is the wrong id: setup_editor_account.ensure_group:126-140 carries the note "the /user endpoints' `group`/`groups` fields take the group's database id, NOT its unix gid — passing the gid fails validation with 'This group does not exist' (learned against the live 25.10 API)" and returns `existing["id"], existing["gid"]` as two values; dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/truenas_client.py:161 and :165 use `gid in (u.get("groups") or []) or (u.get("group") or {}).get("id") == gid` with gid bound from `existing["id"]` (:135), and unwrap `group` as a dict — which `== gid` against an int can never match. check 6 therefore reports "exists but has no members yet", check_editor_accounts runs unconditionally from main() (:469), and main() returns `len(failed)` (:483) against a docstring promising "exit code = number of failed checks (0 = all good)" (:3). Confirmed.

### SERVER-4 (major, logic) — read_marker_slug's remote shell reports a FAILED sudo as MARKER-ABSENT, collapsing the very distinction the module refuses to fall back on
`server/setup_syncthing_folder.py:244`

The module docstring is explicit: "A marker that exists but cannot be read or parsed is a REFUSAL, not a fallback", and `read_marker_slug`'s own docstring lists `MARKER_UNAVAILABLE  could not read it at all (no TRUENAS_PW, host down, permission denied). NOT the same as absent." But the command it sends (`:244-246`) is `if echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" test -e <marker>; then echo MARKER-PRESENT; ...; else echo MARKER-ABSENT; fi`. An `if` condition swallows the exit status, so *any* sudo failure — not in sudoers, sudo lockout after failed attempts, requiretty, a sudo policy change — takes the else branch and the whole remote command still exits 0. `run_ssh` returns `(0, "MARKER-ABSENT\n", <sudo's stderr>)`, `rc != 0` at `:251` is False, and the function returns `MARKER_ABSENT`. `choose_folder_id` then silently takes branch 3, `slugify(rel)`. The test that claims to pin this (`server/tests/test_safety.py:634 test_ssh_failure_is_unavailable_not_absent`) fakes `run_ssh` returning `rc=1`, which the real remote shell cannot produce for this failure — so it pins the intent, not the behaviour. `write_marker.read_existing_marker` (`server/write_marker.py:72-84`) has the identical construct, though there the follow-up `sudo test -d` fails too, so it lands safe.

**Failure scenario:** The SSH account can log in (so `ssh_client` succeeds and nothing raises) but its sudo has been revoked or is temporarily locked out. An operator runs `setup_syncthing_folder.py --project-rel-path "2026/CCT/Creator Profiles/Season 1"` for a project that was moved on the NAS and whose marker slug is `2026-cct-season-1`. The marker read returns MARKER_ABSENT, the script prints the reassuring "note: no .ccsync-project at ... -- using the id derived from --project-rel-path", and proceeds against Syncthing (which needs no sudo) with id `2026-cct-creator-profiles-season-1`. If the collector's real folder is still pointing at the project's OLD path, the `find_folder_by_path` twin guard sees no conflict and a SECOND Syncthing folder is created over the directory — shared with nobody, failing the collector every cycle, cleaned up by nothing: exactly the failure WHICH FOLDER ID exists to prevent.

**Suggested fix:** Make the shell distinguish the three states instead of two, e.g. `out=$(echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" sh -c 'if [ -e <marker> ]; then echo MARKER-PRESENT; cat <marker>; else echo MARKER-ABSENT; fi') || { echo MARKER-UNREADABLE >&2; exit 9; }` and treat a missing MARKER-PRESENT/MARKER-ABSENT sentinel (or the non-zero rc) as MARKER_UNAVAILABLE. Then change the test's fake to return `(0, "MARKER-ABSENT\n", "sudo: a password is required")` so it actually exercises the shell's real answer. Apply the same shape to `write_marker.read_existing_marker`.

**Verifier:** setup_syncthing_folder.py:244-246 is `if echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" test -e <marker>; then echo MARKER-PRESENT; ... else echo MARKER-ABSENT; fi`. An `if` condition is exempt from the exit status, so a sudo-level failure (not in sudoers, lockout, policy) takes the else branch and the whole remote command exits 0; the `rc != 0` guard at :251 never fires and :255-256 returns MARKER_ABSENT. That collapses the distinction the docstring at :230-231 explicitly refuses to collapse ("MARKER_UNAVAILABLE ... permission denied. NOT the same as absent") and feeds choose_folder_id's branch 3 (:285-287, slugify(rel)). The pinning test is indeed inert: tests/test_safety.py:633-638 fakes `run_ssh` returning rc=1, an rc the real shell cannot emit for this failure. Scope check: this is the ONLY sudo in the module (grep: lines 244-245 only), so no earlier privileged step would fail loudly first. The scenario is narrow — a wrong password fails SSH itself (run_ssh reuses TRUENAS_PW for both login and sudo, common.py:536/545), so it needs a genuine sudo-only revocation/lockout — but the logic hole is real as described.

### SERVER-5 (major, logic) — prune_orphaned_staging only sweeps /tmp, but the 1.4 GB artefacts it was written for stage in <host-root>/staging — the OPS-8 debris case is still unfixed
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:1678`

OPS-8 produced two changes that do not meet. `make_staging_dir` gained a `parent` argument and `main()` (`:2251-2264`) points the music pushes at `<host-root>/staging` precisely because "/tmp on the NAS may be RAM-backed and holds the whole component until the swap `cp -a`s it into place". `prune_orphaned_staging` (`:1678-1679`) hardcodes `find /tmp -maxdepth 1 -name ccsync-*-upload.* -mmin +120`. So the reclaim runs against the location the big pushes no longer use, and never against the one they do. `prune_orphaned_staging`'s own docstring states the motivating case as "at ~1.4 GB a go for the music artefacts". Nothing else deletes `<host-root>/staging/*`: the swap scripts' trailing `rm -rf <staging>` (`:897`, `:1781`) only runs on the SUCCESS path, and both `install_tree` (`:1871`) and `install_music_db` (`:951`) deliberately leave staging behind on failure. The prune is also called at `:2241`, before `<host-root>/staging` is even created at `:2253`.

**Failure scenario:** A deterministic failure in step 2d — a truncated proxies transfer, or the unguarded SFTP drop of SERVER-1 — leaves `<host-root>/staging/ccsync-musicproxies-upload.AbC123` holding ~906 MB. The operator retries; the retry's `prune_orphaned_staging` looks only in /tmp, finds nothing, and adds a second 906 MB staging dir. Three retries later the pool is carrying ~2.7 GB of dead staging beside the live music-proxies tree, on the same dataset as the projects, and no script in this repo will ever remove it.

**Suggested fix:** Give `prune_orphaned_staging` the parent(s) to sweep — at minimum both `/tmp` and `<host-root>/staging` — and call it after the staging root is prepared (or call it twice). The dirs are mode 700 owned by the SSH user in both locations, so still no sudo is needed. Update `test_deploy_resilience.py:240` to assert the host-root parent is swept too.

**Verifier:** Verified mechanically. prune_orphaned_staging (:1670-1689) hardcodes `find /tmp -maxdepth 1 -name ccsync-*-upload.* -mmin +120` (:1678-1679, STAGING_GLOB/STAGING_ORPHAN_MINUTES at :212-213), while main() points every music push at `<host-root>/staging` (:2251-2264) and passes it as `staging_parent` to install_music_db (:2448) and install_tree (:2455). Nothing sweeps that parent: the trailing `rm -rf <staging>` exists only on the success path of both swap scripts (:897, :1781), and both install paths deliberately leave staging behind on failure (:1871-1876, :949-953). The ordering claim also holds — the prune runs at :2241, twelve lines before the staging root is created at :2253. One correction to the framing: OPS-8's stated motivation (docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md:814-819) was ENOSPC on a possibly RAM-backed /tmp, and that half IS fixed by the relocation; what remains is unbounded ~900 MB-per-retry accumulation on the pool that no code path removes. Real, but narrower than "the OPS-8 debris case is still unfixed".

### SERVER-6 (major, inefficiency) — The tree verification cat-reads every shipped byte twice, and the swap cp -a rewrites them, on a 906 MB tree written for a 1 MB one
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:1741`

`count_and_size_cmd` (`:1734-1741`) is `find <path> -type f -exec cat {} + | wc -c`, with the docstring's justification "`cat | wc -c` rather than `find -printf '%s'` so the check does not depend on GNU find; the tree is well under a megabyte." That premise died when steps 2c/2d started routing the music artefacts through the same `install_tree`. It is now executed twice per tree — once on staging (`:1862`) and once on the candidate, inside `build_swap_script` (`:1772`) — and between them `build_swap_script:1768` does `cp -a <staging>/. <new>/`, a full second write of the same bytes onto the same dataset even though staging is now deliberately on that dataset (OPS-8). The `tree_ssh_timeout` helper (`:1607-1623`) exists only to stop this from tripping paramiko's inactivity timeout: it is a ceiling raised around avoidable work. TrueNAS SCALE is Debian, so GNU `find -printf` is present and the portability caveat no longer applies.

**Failure scenario:** `--music-data all` (how a re-index is published) pushes proxies (906 MB) + encoder (482 MB). Per tree the NAS reads the staged copy end to end, writes a full duplicate via `cp -a`, then reads that duplicate end to end — roughly 2.8 GB read and 1.4 GB written on top of the 1.4 GB the SFTP already wrote, for a check whose entire purpose is to compare two integers. On the measured ~818 MB/s pool ceiling this is minutes of pure overhead per publish, all of it silent (nothing prints until it finishes), which is itself what made OPS-3 fire.

**Suggested fix:** Replace the byte count with metadata only: `find <path> -type f -printf '%s\n' | awk '{s+=$1} END {print s+0}'` (and keep `| wc -l` for the count) — same two numbers, no file data read. And when the staging parent is on the same dataset as the target (the music case), `mv <staging> <new>` instead of `cp -a <staging>/. <new>/`: it is a rename, the verify still runs on `<new>` before anything live is touched, and the failure message already points the operator at `<new_dir_raw>` for inspection.

**Verifier:** All the mechanics check out. count_and_size_cmd:1741 is `find <p> -type f -exec cat {} + | wc -c` with the docstring justification at :1736-1738 ("so the check does not depend on GNU find; the tree is well under a megabyte"); it is executed on staging at :1862 and again on the candidate inside build_swap_script at :1772, with a full `cp -a {staged}/. {new}/` between them at :1768. Music artefacts route through the same install_tree (:2452-2455, MUSIC_DATA_TREES at :265), so the 1 MB premise is dead, and tree_ssh_timeout (:1607-1623) exists solely to raise the ceiling around that work. The suggested `find -printf '%s'` is viable: the remote is TrueNAS SCALE (Debian, GNU findutils), and this script SSHes nowhere else, so the portability caveat no longer binds. Two caveats on the finding's numbers, neither fatal: the "minutes of pure overhead" figure is unverified (2.8 GB read + 1.4 GB write at the documented ~818 MB/s pool ceiling is seconds in the streaming case; small-file proxies would be slower, but nobody has measured it here), and the `mv` half of the fix trades away the "staging left at <staging> for inspection" affordance that :1873/:1892 advertise.

### SERVER-7 (minor, bug) — build_db_swap_script's rollback restores music.db but leaves its -wal/-shm moved aside, discarding every commit since the last checkpoint
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:895`

The script moves the live db aside, then moves its WAL sidecars aside (`:893-894`), then attempts the swap-in. The rollback arm (`:895-896`) is `mv {new} {db} || { if [ -e {old} ]; then mv {old} {db}; fi; echo "swap failed, previous index restored" >&2; exit 9; }` — it restores only the `.db`. The sidecars stay at `music.db.old.<ts>-wal` / `-shm`. That inverts the function's own stated rule ("Its -wal and -shm siblings belong to the database file they sit beside ... They are moved aside with it, never deleted"): after the rollback the restored db no longer sits beside its WAL, and the message printed to the operator claims the previous index was restored intact.

**Failure scenario:** The container has `/music-data/music.db` open in WAL mode with queued-ingest `pending` rows committed into `music.db-wal` and not yet checkpointed. A `--music-data db` push reaches the final `mv new db` and it fails (an ACL/immutable-flag refusal on the dataset, or ENOSPC on the metadata write). The script rolls the old db back, prints "swap failed, previous index restored", and exits 9. SQLite next opens a `music.db` with no WAL beside it: every ingest committed since the last checkpoint is silently gone — the exact rows MUSIC-3 is about — and the operator has been told nothing was lost.

**Suggested fix:** Emit the inverse sidecar moves inside the rollback braces: for each of `-wal`/`-shm`, `if [ -e {old}{s} ]; then mv {old}{s} {target}{s}; fi;` before the echo/exit 9.

**Verifier:** build_db_swap_script moves the live db aside at :893, emits the sidecar moves (`mv {target}{-wal,-shm} {old}{-wal,-shm}`, built at :876-880) at :894, and the rollback arm at :895-896 is `mv {new} {db} || {{ if [ -e {old} ]; then mv {old} {db}; fi; echo "swap failed, previous index restored" >&2; exit 9; }}` — only the .db comes back. The sidecars stay at `music.db.old.<ts>-wal/-shm`, which contradicts this function's own docstring at :864-868 ("Its -wal and -shm siblings belong to the database file they sit beside ... They are moved aside with it, never deleted") and makes the operator-facing "previous index restored" message untrue for any uncheckpointed commit. Nothing downstream restores them. Minor as rated: it needs the final rename to fail after the preceding ones succeeded (ACL refusal / ENOSPC on metadata), which is uncommon but is exactly the case this rollback arm exists for.

### SERVER-8 (minor, logic) — Two sys.exit(1) paths run after the app swap and bypass fail_after_app_swap's container restart
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:1662`

`fail_after_app_swap` (`:1921-1947`) documents the invariant: "Every step after the swap ... used to `return 1` straight out of main(), leaving [the container serving app.old.<ts>]", and every `install_tree`/`install_music_db` call site now honours it. Two exits do not. `make_staging_dir` calls `sys.exit(1)` at `:1662` when mktemp fails or returns a path outside the requested parent — and it is called from steps 2b, 2c, 2d and 2f, all after the `app` swap. `app_installed` calls `sys.exit(1)` at `:1960` when the `GET /app` query fails, which is step 3, also after the swap. Both leave the container on the previous code directory with no restart and no NOTE. The mountinfo guard (R4-verified) stops the *next* deploy from rm -rf'ing that directory, so this is the braces failing, not the belt — but the exposure the restart exists to end is still left open.

**Failure scenario:** A `--music-data all` deploy fills the pool part-way through step 2d. The next `make_staging_dir` for the encoder gets a failed `mktemp` and calls `sys.exit(1)` at `:1662`. `app` was swapped in step 2, so the container is still serving `app.old.<T>` — it never picks up the freshly installed dashboard code, the operator sees only "FAILED to create staging dir", and nothing in the output says the container is running the old build.

**Suggested fix:** Give `make_staging_dir` a failure return (`""`) instead of `sys.exit`, and have `install_tree`/`install_music_db`/`install_*_over_lan` treat it as `return False` so the existing `return fail_after_app_swap(...)` call sites fire. Same for `app_installed`: return a tri-state (or raise a typed error) and let `main()` route step-3 failures through `fail_after_app_swap`.

**Verifier:** Both exits verified. make_staging_dir:1660-1662 prints "FAILED to create staging dir" and `sys.exit(1)`; it is called from install_tree:1847 and install_music_db:930, i.e. from steps 2b (:2395), 2c (:2406), 2d (:2447/:2452) and 2f (:2465) — all after the `app` swap at :2387. app_installed:1957-1960 `sys.exit(1)` on a failed GET /app and is called at :2471/:2492, also after the swap. Neither routes through fail_after_app_swap (:1918-1947), whose docstring states the invariant the other call sites honour. (Additional exposure in the same function: the `run_ssh` at :1658 is unguarded, so a transport drop there escapes as a traceback too.) Correctly rated minor — build_prune_script's mountinfo guard (:1793-1812) stops the next deploy from rm -rf'ing the live directory — but the restart the OPS-2 fix exists to guarantee is genuinely skipped on these two paths.

### SERVER-9 (minor, logic) — setup_tree's idempotency probe runs unprivileged on a dataset TRUENAS_USER cannot traverse, so every re-run reports "created:" for folders that already exist
`server/setup_tree.py:144`

The template loop emits `if [ -d '<full>' ]; then echo 'exists: <rel>'; else echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" mkdir -p '<full>' && echo 'created: <rel>'; fi` (`:143-147`). The `[ -d ]` runs as TRUENAS_USER, not under sudo, and this repo states twice that TRUENAS_USER has no traverse rights on that tree: `check_health.check_tree` (`server/check_health.py:326-327`, "TRUENAS_USER itself has no traverse rights on the 770 dataset, so an unprivileged test -d false-negatives even when the tree exists") and `setup_syncthing_folder.read_marker_slug`'s docstring ("the dataset is 770 and TRUENAS_USER has no traverse rights"). Every other privileged action in this same generated script is correctly wrapped in `sudo -S -p ""`. The module docstring's contract — "Idempotent: every mkdir is preceded by a `test -d` check so re-running only reports 'already exists' for what's there and creates what's missing" — therefore does not hold on the real NAS.

**Failure scenario:** An operator re-runs `setup_tree.py --project-rel-path "2026/CCT/Creator Profiles/Season 1"` against a project set up last month to re-apply ownership. The `[ -d ]` test false-negatives on all eight template folders, so the output is `created: AE`, `created: Audio/Music`, ... `created: Youtube` — indistinguishable from having just built a brand-new tree at a mistyped path. The operator cannot tell from the output whether they created eight folders in the wrong place; the only correct signal (the marker's "already present with the same identity" line) is buried among eight false "created" lines.

**Suggested fix:** Run the probe with the same privilege as the mkdir: `if echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" test -d '<full>'; then echo 'exists: <rel>'; else ... fi` — the shape `build_marker_write_cmd(only_if_absent=True)` and the ancestor/descendant refusals in this same file already use.

**Verifier:** setup_tree.py:143-147 emits `if [ -d '<full>' ]; then echo 'exists: <rel>'; else echo "$SUDO_PW" | sudo -S -p "" mkdir -p '<full>' && echo 'created: <rel>'; fi` — the probe alone runs unprivileged, while every other privileged action in the same generated script (the ancestor-marker refusal :119, the descendant scan :131-132, the mkdir :138, chown :158, chmod :166) is wrapped in `sudo -S -p ""`. The repo states twice that the unprivileged user cannot traverse there: check_health.py:326-327 ("TRUENAS_USER itself has no traverse rights on the 770 dataset, so an unprivileged test -d false-negatives even when the tree exists") and setup_syncthing_folder.read_marker_slug:233-234. TRUENAS_USER defaults to `truenas_admin` (common.py:39), not root, and the script's own final steps set the tree to <owner>:editors 2770 (:156-169), so after the first run the probe reliably false-negatives. This contradicts the module docstring's idempotency claim at :27. Impact is reporting-only (mkdir -p is idempotent), matching the minor rating.

### SERVER-10 (minor, upgrade) — The 1.4 GB music artefacts ride inside the dashboard deploy over a non-resumable SFTP + hand-rolled stage-verify-swap, when the fleet already owns a resumable checksummed transport for exactly this
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:2411`

GOAL: get music.db, the 482 MB text encoder and 906 MB of preview proxies from the base rig onto the NAS, whenever a re-index is published. CURRENT APPROACH: steps 2c/2d of `install_dashboard_app.py` — a per-file `sftp.put` loop (`upload_tree:1701-1731`), a full cat-based re-read to verify, a root `cp -a` into `<target>.new`, another full cat-based re-read, then renames, all inside the same script and the same run that deploys the dashboard code, gated by a presence probe (`music_components_present`) and a bytes-derived channel timeout (`tree_ssh_timeout`) that exists only to keep the channel alive through the silence. CONCRETE COST: the transfer is not resumable — a drop at 90% (SERVER-1) restarts from byte 0 and strands ~900 MB of staging that nothing prunes (SERVER-5); it is not checksummed per file, only count-and-total-bytes, so two compensating errors pass; it blocks `ship.cmd` step 1 for the whole push; it duplicates every byte on the pool via `cp -a` and reads them twice more to verify (SERVER-6); and `--music-data auto`'s presence probe is a binary "is it there" — a changed encoder or a re-cut proxy set can only be published as a full 1.4 GB re-push of the whole component. BETTER APPROACH: this fleet already runs rclone over SFTP against this NAS for lanes A/B, with the editor accounts and `rclone.conf` stanzas this very package generates. Move the three artefacts to an `rclone sync` (or a dedicated one-shot lane) against `<host-root>/music-*`: resumable, per-file hash-verified, parallel, `--backup-dir` for the previous copy instead of a full `.old.<ts>` duplicate, and incremental so re-publishing an index that changed 20 files moves 20 files. `install_dashboard_app.py` then ships CODE only (its original job, and the thing the 1 MB assumptions in `count_and_size_cmd` and `MIN_TREE_SSH_TIMEOUT` were written for) and the data push becomes its own command with its own cadence — which is what the `--music-data` flag is already trying to express from inside the wrong script.

**Failure scenario:** Publishing a re-index today: `ship.cmd` runs `install_dashboard_app.py --music-data all`. 1.4 GB goes over a single non-resumable SFTP stream; a transport blip at minute nine (routine on the Tailscale/HiNet path) throws away the entire transfer, kills the deploy with a traceback, leaves ~900 MB of staging debris on the pool that no code path removes, and the next attempt starts from zero. With rclone the same blip costs the current file.

**Suggested fix:** Split step 2d out into a `push_music_data.py` (or an rclone invocation from within it) that syncs `music/web/data/{music.db,text_encoder,proxies}` to `<host-root>/music-{data,encoder,proxies}` with `--checksum --backup-dir`, keeping the root-owned chown/chmod pass and the db's WAL-sidecar handling as a small post-step. Leave `--music-data none` as the dashboard deploy's default so a routine ship never touches data at all.

**Verifier:** The characterisation of the current approach is accurate (serial `sftp.put` loop at :1726 with no per-file hashing, count+total-bytes verify at :1741, `cp -a` at :1768, presence-only probe `music_components_present` feeding `--music-data auto` at :828-830), and the transport it proposes genuinely exists in this fleet: lanes A/B are rclone-over-SFTP against this NAS (SPEC.md:30-34, :61) and the companion — which bundles rclone — runs on the base rig too (CLAUDE.md:13), so the tooling is already on the pushing machine. Two corrections the fix should carry, neither of which kills it: (1) SPEC.md:46 documents that rclone has NO mid-file resume over SFTP ("--partial-suffix names the temp file, it does not checkpoint it"), so the win is per-FILE skip-on-retry, not true resume — the finding's own scenario states this correctly, but its "resumable" summary language oversells it, and for the 482 MB encoder (few large files) the benefit is small; (2) the targets `<host-root>/music-*` are root-owned, so rclone would still have to land in the SSH user's staging and keep a root chown/mv step — it removes the sftp loop, not the stage-and-swap.

### SERVER-11 (minor, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — Four host binaries are hand-provisioned by generated root shell because there is no image build — an image is the thing that solves this whole class
`server/install_dashboard_app.py:974`

GOAL: have ffmpeg, ffprobe, the Claude CLI and deno available to the dashboard container. CURRENT APPROACH, stated as a constraint in the code itself (`:280-285`, `:348-351`): "THERE IS NO IMAGE BUILD ANYWHERE IN THIS DEPLOYMENT -- compose runs a pinned, stock python:3.12.7-slim ... and the container runs as 3000:3001, so it cannot apt-get anything either. The remaining provisioning surface is this script." That surface is now ~350 lines: `build_ffmpeg_install_script`, `build_ffmpeg_unpack_script`, `build_binary_install_script`, `fetch_ffmpeg_tarball`, `fetch_pinned_file`, two URL-keyed local caches, `ffmpeg_present`/`binary_present` probes, `install_*_over_lan`, four extra `<host-root>` dirs, four extra read-only compose mounts, and a PATH prepend in run.sh — plus a venv that run.sh rebuilds inside the container on boot. CONCRETE COST, all documented in this file: the first deploy of the app was stranded because the NAS pulls johnvansickle at ~28 kB/s and 42 MB outlived run_ssh's 600 s (`:305-312`); the Claude CLI and deno have no pinnable URL this repo can name (`:353-361`), so on any fresh host `/ytdl` degrades to "claude CLI missing" until an operator hand-configures four env vars; an unverified ffmpeg nearly shipped once because a shell pipeline reported the filter's exit status (`:1045-1049`); adding a fifth binary means another YTDL_BINARIES tuple, another host dir, another mount, another chown/chmod clause in step 1. BETTER APPROACH: TrueNAS SCALE 25.10 runs Docker and can run any image. Build one image FROM python:3.12.7-slim with the four binaries and the pip deps baked in, and either push it to a registry or ship it as a `docker save`/`docker load` tarball over the same SSH this script already has. Steps 2e and 2f, four host dirs, four mounts, both fetch/cache helpers and run.sh's venv bootstrap all disappear; the versions become reproducible and reviewable in a Dockerfile instead of eight environment variables; and the "container is unprivileged so it cannot install anything" constraint stops being a problem to work around.

**Failure scenario:** Provisioning a second NAS (or rebuilding this one) today: `install_dashboard_app.py` runs to completion and reports success, but `YTDL_CLAUDE_URL`/`YTDL_DENO_URL` are unset by design, so both binaries are skipped with a NOTE and every `/ytdl` job on the new host fails immediately with the 'claude CLI missing' banner until someone finds a download URL, a sha256, and re-runs. With an image, the new host is one `docker load` + app create away from identical behaviour.

**Suggested fix:** Add `dashboard/deploy/Dockerfile` pinning the base image plus ffmpeg/ffprobe/deno/claude and `requirements.txt`; publish it (registry, or `docker save | ssh 'docker load'` inside this script) and point `compose_config`'s `image:` at the tag. Retire steps 2e/2f, the `<host-root>/{ffmpeg,claude-bin,deno}` dirs and their mounts. Keep `/claude-home` (a live credential, correctly a volume) and the data mounts as they are.

**Verifier:** The problem statement is accurate and the costs are real and in-code: the no-image-build constraint is asserted at :89, :280-285, :348-351, :598, :617, :977 and in dashboard/deploy/compose.yaml:57/:67/:274/:306; the 28 kB/s stranded-first-deploy history is at :305-312; the operator-supplied-URL degradation for claude/deno is at :353-361; and there is no Dockerfile anywhere in the repo. What I could not establish is that the proposed replacement is viable HERE. The base rig is a Windows machine and nothing in the repo evidences a Linux Docker build host on it; there is no registry in this infrastructure (grep found none); and I could not verify that a TrueNAS 25.10 custom app will run a compose `image:` tag that was side-loaded via `docker load` rather than pulled (the one non-stock image in use, brainicism/bgutil at compose.yaml:319, is pulled from a public registry, which proves pulling works, not loading). The npm-installable Claude CLI would plausibly solve the unpinnable-URL half, but the distribution path — the load-bearing part of the proposal — is unestablished. Sound direction, unproven viability; I would not call it confirmed on the evidence in the repo.

## BROLL-WEB — B-roll web platform

### BROLL-WEB-1 (critical, bug) — mode="semantic" returns zero results for every CJK query
`broll/web/app/search.py:1148`

The CJK semantic-leak gate is `if semantic_hits and not keyword_video_ids and is_cjk_query(q): semantic_hits = []`. Its comment says it "Only applies when there is NO keyword corroboration; a CJK query that did match still gets semantic hits fused normally", and the module docstring (lines 63-66) states "mode='semantic' is unaffected by any of the above except the floor ... nothing here caps or reorders it". But in mode="semantic" the keyword branch at line 1097 never runs, so `keyword_video_ids` is `set()` **by construction** — the gate therefore fires for every predominantly-CJK query. `semantic_hits` becomes `[]`, `rank_lists` stays empty, and line 1171 returns `([], 0)`. The gate was written for hybrid's "keyword found nothing" case and is placed one branch too high. Nothing in the test suite pins semantic mode against a CJK query (grep for `is_cjk_query` across the repo hits only search.py), which is why it survived.

**Failure scenario:** Editor clicks the "Semantic" mode button and types 防彈衣 (or 塞車, or any query that is ≥50% CJK). `is_cjk_query` is true, `keyword_video_ids` is empty because mode="semantic" skipped the keyword pass, so every semantic hit — including the exact cross-lingual matches migrations/004's header measured 9/9 on — is thrown away and /api/search answers `{"results": [], "total": 0}`. The archive's entire Chinese-language corpus is unreachable in the one mode that exists to search it by meaning; the same query in hybrid mode returns results, so it reads as "semantic mode is broken" rather than "no such footage".

**Suggested fix:** Scope the gate to hybrid only — move the three lines inside the `else:` branch at line 1159 (or guard with `mode == "hybrid" and ...`). Add a test: mode="semantic" + a CJK query with a stub encoder must still return the seeded hit.

**Verifier:** Verified at search.py:1093-1172. `keyword_video_ids: set[int] = set()` (line 1095) is only ever reassigned inside `if mode in ("hybrid", "keyword")` (line 1128), so under mode="semantic" it is empty by construction. The gate at line 1148 (`if semantic_hits and not keyword_video_ids and is_cjk_query(q): semantic_hits = []`) therefore fires unconditionally for any query that is >=50% CJK (is_cjk_query, line 160-166), leaving rank_lists empty -> `return [], 0` at line 1171. This directly contradicts the module docstring lines 63-66 ("mode='semantic' is unaffected by any of the above except the floor") and the gate's own comment ("Only applies when there is NO keyword corroboration" - vacuously always true here). Searched for a documented exemption: no mention of the CJK semantic gate in CLAUDE.md, KNOWN_BUGS.md, docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md or docs/youtube_dlp_bugs.md; grep for is_cjk_query hits only search.py (no test anywhere), and the CJK tests that exist (test_v2_search.py:30-62, test_ingest.py:157) all use default hybrid mode. test_semantic_booster.py's only mode="semantic" test (line 267-284) uses the ASCII query "body armor", so nothing pins this. Cited file:line is correct (search.py:1148).

### BROLL-WEB-2 (major, logic) — The keyword/semantic search path never applies BROWSE_PREDICATE, so confirmed duplicates are searchable
`broll/web/app/search.py:1091`

`BROWSE_PREDICATE` (`status != 'skipped' AND status != 'excluded' AND duplicate_of IS NULL`) is applied only by `_browse` (line 968) and by /api/tree's counts. Once `raw_terms` is non-empty, execution goes to the FTS/semantic path, where the only WHERE fragments are `cat_clause` (category/collection/shoot/path) and `flags_clause` — nothing filters status or `duplicate_of`. `_browse`'s docstring justifies the asymmetry with "Search never surfaced any of these anyway: with no segments and no transcript they cannot match", which is false for duplicates: `broll/indexer/broll_index/duplicates.py:pick_canonical` ranks group members by status and then by *segment count*, i.e. it is explicitly built for the case where the losing copies are already `indexed` and carry segments/transcripts/embeddings. Marking a row `duplicate_of` writes only that column (`duplicates.py:302`); its segments, transcript cues, embeddings and FTS entries all stay. migrations/005_duplicates.sql states duplicates are "excluded from indexing, from search results, and from the copy manifest" — two of the three hold. (The live E:\broll-queue\broll.db currently has 246 duplicate rows, 0 of them with segments, so this is latent today; the 3 skipped/excluded rows that do carry transcript cues are the already-known BROLL-14 half.)

**Failure scenario:** Two copies of the same clip are both indexed (the ordinary shape — the archive scanner sweeps several shares), then `broll-index duplicates --apply` runs and marks the lower-ranked copy `duplicate_of` the other. An editor searches "nuclear reactor": both rows come back as separate cards with identical thumbnails. The duplicate row was skipped by `build_archive.py:320` (`AND duplicate_of IS NULL`), so it has no `archive_path`: `/media/proxy/{id}.mp4` falls through `_proxy_path` to the non-existent flat `proxies/{id}.mp4` and 404s (black player), and `_insert_target` falls back to the ingest identity, so "Send to Resolve" sends `share=ff3` and the companion answers "no mount configured for share 'ff3'" — the exact 2026-08-12 failure R12 exists to prevent. The sidebar count for that folder also disagrees with the click, which is the rule get_tree's docstring sets.

**Suggested fix:** Fold `BROWSE_PREDICATE` into `cat_clause` for the search path too (it is already a `v.`-qualified fragment and every FTS builder joins `videos v`), and into `_allowed_video_ids` so semantic hits are filtered identically. Then delete the now-wrong sentence in `_browse`'s docstring.

**Verifier:** Verified. BROWSE_PREDICATE (search.py:939-941) is used only by _browse (line 968) and routes_api.py:184; the search path (search.py:1093+) appends only cat_clause/coll/shoot/path + flags_clause to every FTS builder (_search_segments_en:557, _search_segments_cjk:583, _search_transcript_en:610, _search_transcript_cjk:633) and _allowed_video_ids, so status/duplicate_of are never filtered on the search path. The hunter's premise checks out: duplicates.py's do_duplicates (line 300-306) writes only duplicate_of + full_hash, leaving segments/cues/embeddings/FTS intact, and _rank (line 56-62) tie-breaks by segment count, i.e. the design explicitly expects losing copies to be already-indexed. duplicates.py's own module docstring says duplicates are excluded "per SPEC.md, on the web/ side -- from search results" - the stated contract is not met. Downstream claims also check out: build_archive.py:320 filters `duplicate_of IS NULL` (so no archive_path), routes_media.py:16-24 _proxy_path falls back to the non-existent flat proxies/{id}.mp4, and routes_api.py:_insert_target (line 45-46) returns the ingest share when archive_path is empty - the exact 2026-08-12 'no mount configured for share ff3' failure named in its docstring. No test covers it: test_search.py:153 and test_tree_counts_match_clicks.py only pin BROWSE mode. Latent today (I confirmed on E:\broll-queue\broll.db read-only: 246 duplicate rows, 0 with segments), so severity 'major' is arguably generous, but the logic gap and the wrong docstring sentence in _browse (line 965-967) are real.

### BROLL-WEB-3 (major, inefficiency) — No LIMIT on any FTS source query and no cap on hits-per-video: ~1 MB of JSON for one 24-card page
`broll/web/app/search.py:1213`

`_search_segments_en/_cjk` and `_search_transcript_en/_cjk` (lines 549-647) run their MATCH with no LIMIT and `fetchall()` everything, including the full `description`/`text` column and a `snippet()` per row. Every one of those rows then becomes a `meta` entry, an RRF rank-list entry, and — for the 24 videos that survive paging — a full entry in that video's `hits` array at lines 1214-1228, each carrying `description` + `snippet`. Measured against the live E:\broll-queue\broll.db (15,103 videos / 53,226 segments / 29,783 transcript cues): q="taiwan" pulls 1,771 transcript rows + 4,899 segment rows and yields 263 KB of hit text for the top-24 videos; q="the" pulls 9,663 + 3,375 rows, up to 193 hits on a single video, and 1.01 MB of hit text for the top-24 page. The frontend uses `hits[0]` for the card thumbnail/seek target and `state.detailHits` for the seekbar bands of the one clip that gets opened — everything past the first few hits per video is transferred and parsed for nothing, on a 250 ms-debounced search box over a Tailscale link.

**Failure scenario:** An editor types "the taiwan strait" in the search box. Each debounced keystroke burst issues /api/search, which fetches ~13,000 FTS rows with their text and snippets into Python, fuses them, and serializes ~1 MB of `hits` for 24 cards — of which the UI reads 24 `t_start` values. Over a remote link this is a multi-second search that looks like the archive is slow.

**Suggested fix:** Add `ORDER BY rank LIMIT ?` (a few hundred) to each per-table query — RRF only reads rank position, and a rank past ~500 contributes 1/560, i.e. noise — and cap the serialized `hits` per video (e.g. the top 20 by fused score) in the loop at line 1212, keeping the full list only for /api/videos/{id} where the seekbar actually needs it.

**Verifier:** Verified structurally and numerically. None of the four per-table queries (search.py:549-647) has a LIMIT or ORDER BY; each ends in `.fetchall()` and selects the full description/text plus a per-row snippet(). Every returned row becomes a meta entry + rank-list entry, and lines 1212-1228 serialize EVERY fused hit for each of the 24 paged videos into `hits`. I re-measured the hunter's figures on E:\broll-queue\broll.db read-only and they match exactly: 15,103 videos / 53,226 segments / 29,783 cues; q="taiwan" -> 4,899 segments_fts + 1,771 transcript_fts rows; q="the" -> 3,375 + 9,663 rows. Frontend use confirmed: buildCard (app.js:642-707) reads only hits[0].t_start (2026-08-10 decision removed hit chips entirely), and the rest is used only as state.detailHits for the opened clip's seekbar bands. Caveat the fix should account for: adding a per-source LIMIT would also change `total` (line 1188 counts distinct videos over all fused hits), so the suggested cap is not purely cosmetic - it trades an exact result count for the payload win.

### BROLL-WEB-4 (minor, logic) — openDetail's one-shot loadedmetadata seek listener leaks onto the next clip
`broll/web/static/app.js:1023`

`openDetail` registers `player.addEventListener("loadedmetadata", () => { player.currentTime = seekToSeconds }, {once: true})` on the single, persistent `#player` element. `{once: true}` only removes it once it has *fired*. If that clip's metadata never loads — the proxy 404s (`_proxy_path` falls back to a flat `proxies/{id}.mp4` that does not exist for un-archived / duplicate / skipped rows), or the user navigates away first — the listener stays attached. `closeDetail` does `removeAttribute("src")` + `load()`, which fires `emptied`, not `loadedmetadata`, so it does not clear it either. The next `openDetail` sets `player.src` on the same element and the stale listener fires against the new clip.

**Failure scenario:** Search "harbour", click a result whose best hit is at 02:00 — `openDetail(id, 120)` arms the listener. Before its metadata arrives (or because that clip's proxy 404s), press Back, clear the search box so the grid is in browse mode, then click any card. Browse rows have `hits: []`, so `seekTo` is null and the new `openDetail` arms no listener of its own — but the leftover one fires on the new clip's `loadedmetadata` and jumps the playhead to 120 s. The editor sets in/out points against a clip that silently opened two minutes in.

**Suggested fix:** Keep the handler in a module-level variable and `player.removeEventListener("loadedmetadata", pendingSeekHandler)` at the top of `openDetail` (and in `closeDetail`) before arming a new one — or drop the listener entirely and do the seek from the existing `loadedmetadata` handler by reading `state.detailSeekTo`, which is already reset per clip.

**Verifier:** Verified at app.js:1021-1030 (openDetail) and 1033-1046 (closeDetail). The listener is added to the single persistent #player with {once:true}, which only detaches after it fires; closeDetail does pause + removeAttribute("src") + load(), which cannot fire loadedmetadata, and nothing calls removeEventListener anywhere (grep: no removeEventListener for loadedmetadata in app.js). openDetail arms a new listener only when seekToSeconds != null, so a subsequent browse-mode click (buildCard:706 sets seekTo=null when hits is empty) leaves the stale one to fire against the new src. The 404 half of the scenario is also real: routes_media.py:16-24 _proxy_path falls back to a flat proxies/{id}.mp4 that does not exist for un-archived rows, so metadata never loads and the listener persists. Back does route through closeDetail (applyHistoryState, app.js:286-288), which as noted does not clear it. Minor severity is right - it needs a slow/failed load or a fast navigate-away - but the leak is genuine.

### BROLL-WEB-5 (minor, logic) — prefix_query returns a query identical to the exact pass when nothing expands, double-weighting the porter tables in RRF
`broll/web/app/fuzzy.py:248`

`prefix_query`'s docstring promises it "Returns None if no term survives sanitization ... **or if no term is long enough to expand**". The code does not do the second half: a term shorter than `_MIN_TERM_LEN_FOR_PREFIX` (5) is appended as a plain quoted term (line 249) and `None` is returned only when `parts` is empty. So for a query whose terms are all short, `prefix_query` hands back a string byte-identical to `_terms_to_fts_query(raw_terms)`, and `_run_fuzzy_pass` (search.py:793-808) re-runs `_run_fts_pass` with it. That is two redundant FTS queries per fuzzy-triggered search, and — because the prefix pass is called with `include_cjk=False` — it registers `fuzzy_prefix_segments_fts` / `fuzzy_prefix_transcript_fts` as extra RRF sources holding exactly the same ranking as the originals, while the two trigram sources are not duplicated. The porter tables' contribution to every key is doubled relative to the trigram tables. tests/test_fuzzy.py:85 pins the string behaviour (`prefix_query(["mars"]) == '"mars"'`), so the unit contract is fine; the defect is that `_run_fuzzy_pass` does not notice the query is a duplicate.

**Failure scenario:** q = "槍戰現場" (4 CJK chars, one token). Exact pass: segments_fts + segments_cjk_fts + the transcript pair. Fewer than FUZZY_MIN_VIDEO_RESULTS=5 videos come back, so the fuzzy pass runs: the term is 4 chars < 5, so `prefix_query` returns `"槍戰現場"` — the same query — and segments_fts/transcript_fts are queried a second time and added again as `fuzzy_prefix_*` sources. A segment found only by the trigram table now scores 1/(60+r) against 2/(60+r) for one found only by the unicode61 table, so the substring-matched CJK hit is pushed down purely by the redundant pass. Same shape for any all-short-term English query ("surf pier").

**Suggested fix:** In `_run_fuzzy_pass`, skip the prefix pass when `prefix_q == _terms_to_fts_query(raw_terms)`; or make `prefix_query` honour its docstring and return None when no term reached `_MIN_TERM_LEN_FOR_PREFIX` (updating test_fuzzy.py:85/88 to say why).

**Verifier:** Verified. prefix_query (fuzzy.py:243-252) appends a plain quoted term for anything under _MIN_TERM_LEN_FOR_PREFIX=5 and returns None only when `parts` is empty, so its docstring's "or if no term is long enough to expand" (line 233) is not implemented. For an all-short-term query the returned string is byte-identical to _terms_to_fts_query (search.py:248-250) - both produce '"term" AND "term"' with the same quoting - and _run_fuzzy_pass (search.py:793-808) re-runs _run_fts_pass with it under source_prefix="fuzzy_prefix_", registering fuzzy_prefix_segments_fts / fuzzy_prefix_transcript_fts as separate rank_lists keys (confirmed at search.py:692,712 etc. where keys are f"{source_prefix}..."). RRF (line 527-532) sums per source key, so those two porter sources contribute twice for identical rankings while the trigram sources (include_cjk=False on the prefix pass, line 806) contribute once - the documented CJK substring hit is genuinely demoted. tests/test_fuzzy.py:78-87 pins the string output but nothing pins the pass-level behaviour. Note the redundancy only bites when the terms contain no punctuation (_TERM_CLEAN_RE strips it), which is the common case.

### BROLL-WEB-6 (minor, inefficiency) — correct_terms rebuilds a 315k-entry lowercase map and a 167k-entry candidate list on every fuzzy query
`broll/web/app/fuzzy.py:190`

`VocabularyCache` caches only the sorted `list[str]`. `correct_terms` then rebuilds, per request, the `vocab_lower` dict comprehension over the whole vocabulary (line 190) and, per query term, the `candidates` list filtered by length delta (line 210). None of that depends on the query except the length window, so it is pure per-request re-derivation of a cacheable structure. Measured on the live E:\broll-queue\broll.db: the vocabulary is 434,034 tokens; `vocab_lower` is 315,562 entries and takes ~80 ms to build; the candidate list for a 9-character term is 167,093 entries and takes ~19 ms, then rapidfuzz scores all of them. That is ~100 ms+ per term added to every search thin enough to trigger the fuzzy fallback — which is exactly the searches that are already the slowest.

**Failure scenario:** Editor searches "ambluance harbour" (a typo, so the exact pass returns <5 videos and the fuzzy fallback fires). The request builds the 315k-entry lowercase map once and a ~167k-entry candidate list twice — ~120 ms of pure Python dict/list construction before rapidfuzz has scored anything — and throws all of it away when the response is sent. The next typo query rebuilds the identical structures.

**Suggested fix:** Make `VocabularyCache` store the derived artefacts alongside the list: the lowercase map, and a `dict[int, list[str]]` bucketed by token length so `candidates` is a slice-and-concat of 7 buckets instead of a 315k-element scan. Same key, same invalidation, built once per generation instead of once per request.

**Verifier:** Verified at fuzzy.py:90-155 (VocabularyCache caches only the sorted list[str]) and 190-213 (vocab_lower dict comprehension per call; candidates list per term). I reproduced the hunter's measurements against E:\broll-queue\broll.db read-only using the module's own columns and regex: vocabulary 434,034 tokens (1.22 s to build), vocab_lower 315,562 entries (0.083 s), candidate list for a 9-char term 167,093 entries (0.027 s) - i.e. ~110 ms of pure re-derivation per fuzzy query, on exactly the queries that already under-delivered. Nothing in CLAUDE.md/KNOWN_BUGS.md/the bug-hunt docs documents this as deliberate; the class docstring only justifies the cache KEY (BROLL-17 / R2), not the choice of what is cached. The proposed length-bucketed dict is compatible with the existing key/invalidation, so the fix is viable. Genuinely inefficiency-class, not correctness.

### BROLL-WEB-7 (minor, upgrade) — search_generation is global, so one clip's write evicts the whole 127 MB matrix and the 434k-word vocabulary
`broll/web/app/semantic.py:176`

GOAL: search caches must never serve vectors or vocabulary from rows that have been replaced. CURRENT APPROACH (migration 010 / R2): a single process-wide counter folded into both cache keys, bumped by every write that touches `embeddings`, `segments` or `transcript_segments`. It is correct, but its granularity is the entire corpus — one clip's `POST /api/ingest/index` (or one `SqliteBackend.upsert_embedding`) invalidates everything. The rebuild then re-reads and re-parses the full table: measured on the live DB, `SELECT ...
