FROM embeddings WHERE model = ?` over 82,889 rows is 0.21 s of fetch + 0.08 s of `np.frombuffer` into a 127.3 MB float32 matrix, and the fuzzy vocabulary is ~1.4 s (53,226 segments × 6 columns + 29,783 cues × 2 columns, regex-split in Python into 434k tokens). BETTER APPROACH: track dirtiness per video rather than per corpus — a `meta`-keyed dirty set, or a `search_generation` column/row per `video_id` — and apply it incrementally: for the matrix, delete the affected video's rows from the cached matrix and append the new ones (`index.rows` already carries `video_id`), rebuilding wholesale only when the dirty set exceeds some fraction; for the vocabulary, re-scan only the dirty videos' rows. The counter stays exactly as the correctness signal — this only changes what gets thrown away when it moves.

**Failure scenario:** The indexer HTTP-ingests a batch of clips (one `POST /api/ingest/index` each, each bumping the counter in-transaction). Any editor searching during that run pays a full rebuild on every single query: ~0.3 s to re-read and re-parse the whole 127 MB embedding matrix in `_ensure_index`, plus ~1.4 s to rebuild the 434k-token vocabulary in `VocabularyCache._build` when the query is thin enough to trigger fuzzy — so a 2-second-plus search, per keystroke burst, for the entire duration of the index run, with 127 MB of allocation churn per request in the dashboard process.

**Suggested fix:** Keep meta.search_generation as the staleness signal but make the caches diff against it instead of dropping: record which video_ids changed (a `meta` JSON dirty list, or a `videos.search_generation` column bumped alongside), and have `_ensure_index` / `VocabularyCache._build` patch only those rows. Fall back to a full rebuild when the recorded generation gap is larger than the retained dirty history.

**Verifier:** Upgrade, and viable here. Verified the mechanism: semantic.py:154-197 keys the matrix on (db path, read_search_generation, count, high-water) and rebuilds wholesale on any change; fuzzy.py:119-138 does the same for the vocabulary; the counter is bumped per write by routes_ingest.py:172 and the indexer's sqlite_backend.py:133,192,209 - i.e. genuinely corpus-global granularity. I re-measured on the live DB read-only and the hunter's numbers are accurate: 82,889 embedding rows, dim 384, 0.21 s fetch + 0.06 s np.frombuffer, 127.3 MB matrix; vocabulary rebuild 1.22 s. Both processes write the same DB the dashboard serves, so the concurrent-ingest scenario is reachable, and the fuzzy path fires on thin queries (partially-typed words), so the 1.4 s is not hypothetical. I looked for a documented constraint that would block per-video dirtiness (KNOWN_BUGS R2, semantic.py:161-172, fuzzy.py:98-107) and found only the opposite: the docs argue for keeping count/high-water as belt-and-braces, which the proposal preserves. Caveat worth flagging to whoever takes it: the dirty set would have to be maintained by two independent codebases (web ingest + indexer backend), reproducing exactly the 'only as good as those write paths' discipline' risk the docs already call out - so the full-rebuild fallback is load-bearing, not optional.

### BROLL-WEB-8 (minor, bug) — selectFolder assigns a shoot slug to the category <select>, leaving it blank with selectedIndex -1
`broll/web/static/app.js:556`

`selectFolder` does `select.value = category` unconditionally. For a Creators_Club node the slug is a shoot path (`ff4::Whisky / Interviews`), which is never an `<option>` — `loadCategories` populates the select from /api/categories only. Assigning a `<select>` a value that no option carries silently sets `selectedIndex = -1` and renders the control empty (the exact trap R15 fix 1 documents for the ytdl project picker). The two other places that write this control both guard against it — `applyHistoryState` (line 277) and `jumpToSearch` (line 1074) each use `state.collection === "creators_club" ? "" : state.category` — so `selectFolder` is the odd one out and the UI disagrees with itself depending on how the same folder was reached.

**Failure scenario:** Editor clicks `ff4 > Whisky > Interviews` in the folder rail. `selectFolder` sets `select.value = "ff4::Whisky / Interviews"`, no option matches, and the category dropdown goes visually blank instead of reading "All categories". Reaching the same folder via Back/Forward (`applyHistoryState`) or via a detail-panel crumb (`jumpToSearch`) leaves it reading "All categories" — same state, two different renderings, and the blank box reads as a filter the editor cannot see or identify.

**Suggested fix:** Apply the same guard the other two call sites use: `select.value = collection === "creators_club" ? "" : category;` — or better, hoist the three copies into one `syncCategorySelect()` helper so the next control that touches it cannot diverge again.

**Verifier:** Verified. selectFolder (app.js:555-556) does `select.value = category` with no collection guard, while the two other writers of the same control guard it identically: applyHistoryState (line 277) and jumpToSearch (line 1074) both use `state.collection === "creators_club" ? "" : state.category`. The select's only static option is value="" ("All categories", index.html:33) and loadCategories (app.js:401-414) populates it purely from /api/categories, which is `SELECT * FROM categories` (routes_api.py:148-151) - subject slugs only, never the `share::A / B` shoot slugs the Creators_Club tree emits (runSearch itself distinguishes them at line 580-585 by sending `shoot=` rather than `category=`). Assigning a non-matching value to a <select> sets selectedIndex=-1 and renders it blank, so the same folder renders two different ways depending on route. Minor/cosmetic but real, and the cited line is correct.

## BROLL-IDX — B-roll indexer pipeline

### BROLL-IDX-1 (critical, bug) — The bare "429" rate-limit marker is matched against EVERY stage's error text, so a filename or path containing 429 aborts and permanently wedges the run
`broll/indexer/broll_index/pipeline.py:618`

`_process_video`'s catch-all applies `is_fatal_api_error(str(e))` to exceptions from probe/proxy/frames as well as claude. `is_fatal_api_error` delegates to `is_rate_limited`, whose marker list contains the bare substring `"429"` (broll_index/claude_client.py:203). Any local-stage failure whose message merely CONTAINS those three digits is therefore reclassified as an account-wide rate limit: `FatalRunError` is raised, `run_pipeline` logs "rate limit reached", the whole run stops, and — critically — the clip's status is deliberately left untouched so it is retried. It fails identically on every subsequent run, so the queue never advances past it. The triggers are not hypothetical: on the live DB at E:/broll-queue/broll.db, 35 video ids and 8 rel_paths contain "429", and the error strings all embed them — `UnreadableMediaError(f"ffprobe failed on {Path(path).name}: ...")` carries the source filename (C0429.MP4, IMG_4290.MP4 are ordinary camera names), the proxy encoder's RuntimeError carries ffmpeg's stderr including the output path `.../proxies/4290.mp4`, and `stage_frames`' `subprocess.CalledProcessError` stringifies the whole argv including `.../sheets/4291/frames/frame_0003.jpg`. The claude stage has its own guaranteed trigger: `parse_claude_response` raises `ValueError(f"claude response is not valid JSON: {e}")` and a `json.JSONDecodeError` renders as "... (char 429)". tests/test_api_failure_handling.py only ever feeds real API strings, so nothing catches this.

**Failure scenario:** Video id 4291 in the live queue has a truncated download. `broll-index run` (or run_queue's serial fallback) reaches it; `stage_frames` fails and raises CalledProcessError whose message contains the path `E:\broll-queue\sheets\4291\frames\frame_0003.jpg`. `is_rate_limited` sees "429", `FatalRunError` is raised, the run aborts after N videos printing "rate limit reached ... The queue is untouched — re-run to pick up where it stopped". The clip is never marked 'error', so every re-run stops at exactly the same clip. Overnight indexing makes zero progress and the log blames a rate limit that never happened.

**Suggested fix:** Only consult `is_fatal_api_error` for stages that actually call the API (`stage == "claude"`), and tighten the marker: replace the bare `"429"` in `_RATE_LIMIT_MARKERS` with an anchored form such as a regex for `\b(api )?error 429\b` / `status(?: code)? 429`, or drop it entirely since `rate_limit_error`, `rate limit`, `session limit` and `too many requests` already cover the real envelopes. Add a regression test asserting `is_fatal_api_error("ffprobe failed on C0429.MP4: moov atom not found")` is False.

**Verifier:** Mechanism verified. pipeline.py:602-624 wraps probe/proxy/frames/transcribe/claude in ONE try and the classifier arm is `elif is_fatal_api_error(str(e)): raise FatalRunError(...)` — no stage guard. claude_client.py:203 does contain a bare "429" in _RATE_LIMIT_MARKERS, and is_fatal_api_error delegates to is_rate_limited (claude_client.py:209-224). run_pipeline (pipeline.py:672-682) catches FatalRunError, logs "rate limit reached", and leaves status untouched, so the same clip stops the run every time. Error text really does carry paths: extract_frames uses `subprocess.run(cmd, check=True)` (ffmpeg_tools.py:520) so CalledProcessError stringifies the whole argv including E:/broll-queue/sheets/<id>/frames/frame_NNNN.jpg (sheets dir is keyed by video id, pipeline.py:208), and UnreadableMediaError embeds Path(path).name (ffmpeg_tools.py:52). Live DB confirms 35 ids and 8 rel_paths containing "429". No doc (CLAUDE.md, KNOWN_BUGS.md, docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md) records this as deliberate; tests only feed real API envelopes. TWO CORRECTIONS to the hunter's scenario: (a) id 4291 is currently status 'indexed' and none of the 35/8 429-rows is currently in a runnable status (discovered/error/probed/proxied/organised is empty for them), so the named live instance does not exist today — the trigger needs a newly scanned/re-run 429 clip; (b) in the DEFAULT overnight path the local stages run through parallel_local._process_one (parallel_local.py:100-106), which catches Exception and calls set_error with NO fatal check, so probe/proxy/frames misclassification only bites the serial `broll-index run` path. The claude-stage trigger is live on both paths: parse_claude_response raises ValueError("claude response is not valid JSON: ...") (claude_client.py:541) and a JSONDecodeError renders "(char 429)".

### BROLL-IDX-2 (critical, bug) — The overnight watchdog restarts the PRE-FOLD standalone checkout, silently re-introducing R9 (10-bit previews), R10 (no proxy timecode) and BROLL-1/2 (unrecorded sprite geometry)
`broll/indexer/watchdog.ps1:22`

`$indexer = 'E:\Projects\broll-platform\indexer'` still points at the standalone repo the b-roll platform was folded out of on 2026-08-10 (CLAUDE.md: "folded in from the standalone broll-platform repo 2026-08-10 (pre-fold git history stays there)"). That directory still exists on this machine and is stale: `E:/Projects/broll-platform/indexer/broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py` is dated 2026-07-21 and has zero occurrences of `-pix_fmt yuv420p`, no `-timecode`/`read_timecode`, and no sprite-geometry return from `build_sprite`. The watchdog is described in its own header as "what the scheduled task runs", it launches `run_queue.py` from that working directory, and `config.queue.yaml` there points at the same live DB (E:\broll-queue\broll.db) and the same DATA_ROOT — so it writes real artifacts into the real archive using code that predates the entire 2026-08-11/12 fix pass. Every clip a watchdog restart indexes gets a 10-bit H.264 preview (black player, R9), a timecode-less proxy Resolve will refuse to link (R10), and NULL sprite_cell_* (the exact rows regen_sprites.py exists to sweep). The repo copy of watchdog.ps1 has never been updated — `git log` shows its only commit is the fold commit e0b47cd.

**Failure scenario:** The scheduled task fires at 03:00, sees no live run_queue (the session ended), and Start-Process launches `python -u run_queue.py --config config.queue.yaml --model haiku --api-workers 12` with -WorkingDirectory E:\Projects\broll-platform\indexer. Overnight it proxies and sprites 400 new clips with the 2026-07-21 encoder: all of them come out yuv420p10le (black in the browser), none carries source timecode (Resolve refuses LinkProxyMedia), and all have sprite_cell_h NULL. Nothing in the log says which tree ran. If the stale checkout is ever deleted instead, `Push-Location $indexer` throws under `$ErrorActionPreference='Stop'`, the catch writes "watchdog error" and exits 1 — the run is never restarted at all, which is the exact 14-hour silent death the watchdog was written for.

**Suggested fix:** Point `$indexer` at the in-repo path (`E:\Projects\resolve-remote-sync\broll\indexer`), or better, derive it from the script's own location (`$PSScriptRoot`) so it can never drift again; do the same for `$db`/`$python` if they are repo-relative. Then re-register the scheduled task against the new script path, and archive or delete `E:\Projects\broll-platform\indexer` so nothing can accidentally run it.

**Verifier:** broll/indexer/watchdog.ps1:22 does read $indexer = 'E:\Projects\broll-platform\indexer' and both the `Push-Location $indexer` remaining-count call and the `Start-Process -WorkingDirectory $indexer` launch use it. That tree exists, is NOT a junction to the repo, and is genuinely pre-fold: broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py there is dated 2026-07-21, has zero `-pix_fmt yuv420p` (only the yuvj420p JPEG ones), no read_timecode / `-timecode`, build_sprite returns None with no geometry, and `sprite_cell` appears nowhere in its broll_index/*.py; its pipeline.py is 2026-08-07 vs the repo's 2026-08-11. Its config.queue.yaml points at the same data_root E:/broll-queue and same db E:/broll-queue/broll.db, so it would write real artifacts. CLAUDE.md:19 confirms the 2026-08-10 fold and git log shows watchdog.ps1's only commit is the fold commit e0b47cd. CORRECTIONS: the registered task `broll-indexer-watchdog` is currently State=Disabled (LastRunTime 23/07/2026, 1055 missed runs), so the "fires at 03:00" scenario cannot happen until it is re-enabled; and its action is `-File "E:\Projects\broll-platform\indexer\watchdog.ps1"` with WorkingDirectory in the stale tree — i.e. the task runs the STALE COPY of the script, so fixing only the in-repo watchdog.ps1 would not change what the task does. broll/OVERNIGHT.md:9 documents the same stale working directory, so the drift is repo-wide, not a lone typo.

### BROLL-IDX-3 (major, logic) — run_queue classifies a stage failure as a rate limit by grepping the whole tee'd child output for "429", which its own progress lines contain — a permanent error becomes an infinite 15-minute retry loop
`broll/indexer/run_queue.py:198`

`run_cmd` returns the child stage's ENTIRE stdout, and the retry decision is `any(k in lowered for k in ("session limit", "rate limit", "429", "usage limit"))` over that whole blob. parallel_claude.py prints `[{i}/{len(ids)}] ... last={vid}:{status}` every fifth video and on every success, so a run of more than ~429 videos, or one that touches any of the 35 live video ids containing "429", puts the literal string "429" in the output regardless of why the stage exited. Anything that then makes parallel_claude exit 1 — an `authentication_error`, "credit balance is too low", a bad model name — is misread as a transient limit. Because the same output also carries no parseable reset hint in that case, `seconds_until_reset` returns None, the loop sleeps `RETRY_WAIT_S` (15 min) and `continue`s, forever. The `if after < before` progress check and the `log.error(...); return 1` stop path are both below the rate-limit branch and are never reached. This is the opposite of the intent stated at parallel_claude.py:250-255 ("run_queue.py matches the message to decide between 'wait out the session limit' and 'stop, something is actually broken'").

**Failure scenario:** An overnight `run_queue.py` reaches video 800; the subscription login expires. parallel_claude's first worker gets `API error: authentication_error: invalid x-api-key`, reports fatal, cancels the queue and exits 1. Its output already contains `[429/2024] ...` from earlier in the same invocation. run_queue matches "429", logs "rate limited — no usable reset time, waiting 15 min, queue untouched", sleeps, relaunches, and repeats indefinitely — burning a full set of concurrent auth-failing calls every 15 minutes and never surfacing the real cause, while the watchdog sees a live run_queue and stays quiet.

**Suggested fix:** Do not scan the whole transcript. parallel_claude already prints a single machine-readable last line (`claude stage aborted: <fatal>`); match only that line (or have it emit a structured marker / distinct exit code for rate-limit vs. permanent), and reuse `claude_client.is_rate_limited` on that one string once the bare "429" marker is tightened per BROLL-IDX-1. Also cap the number of consecutive rate-limit sleeps so an unparseable case eventually stops instead of looping forever.

**Verifier:** Verified: run_cmd (run_queue.py:89-108) tees and returns the child's ENTIRE stdout, and run_queue.py:198 tests `any(k in lowered for k in ("session limit","rate limit","429","usage limit"))` over that whole blob; the `after < before` retry and the `log.error(...); return 1` stop path are both below it (:216-221). parallel_claude.py:241 prints `[{i}/{len(ids)}] ... last={vid}:{status}` and :190 prints `{len(ids)} videos to index`, so "429" can appear from the counter alone, and an auth/credit failure exits 1 via the fatal path (:250-256) with a message that has no 429 in it — so it is misread as a limit. CORRECTION to the severity claim: it is not generally an infinite loop. Fatal-status videos `continue` before the progress print (parallel_claude.py:208-218), so a relaunch that fails immediately on auth prints no [i/N] lines; its output then usually contains no "429", falls through to `after < before` (false) and the run stops with log.error. The loop is genuinely endless only when "429" recurs in every attempt's output — e.g. len(ids) is 429/1429/4290-something in the "N videos to index" line, or a printed vid contains 429, or (combined with BROLL-IDX-1) the fatal string itself is a JSON error reading "(char 429)". So: one guaranteed spurious 15-minute sleep plus a misleading log on every permanent failure, and a real infinite loop in the recurring-429 cases.

### BROLL-IDX-4 (major, logic) — run_pipeline never queries status 'organised', so reopen_if_now_indexable can never fire on the serial path and flipping index:true strands the clips it was written to rescue
`broll/indexer/broll_index/pipeline.py:648`

`reopen_if_now_indexable` exists precisely so that `organised` is "RESTING, not terminal" — the comment at pipeline.py:68-72 promises it "puts these clips straight back into the queue the moment the flag is flipped. Without that, a share indexed later would sit here forever: nothing else in STAGE_PREREQ_STATUS accepts 'organised', so the clips would be invisible to every runner while looking finished." But `run_pipeline` builds its work list as `statuses = ["discovered", "probed", "proxied"]` (+ indexed/sorted only when transcribe/embed is requested) and `videos_by_status` filters on exactly those, so an `organised` row is never fetched and `_process_video`'s call to `reopen_if_now_indexable` at line 500 is unreachable from the CLI. `parallel_local.eligible_ids` does include 'organised' (parallel_local.py:120) and its comment says so explicitly — the two runners disagree. The test suite hides this: tests/test_share_not_indexed.py::test_reopened_clips_get_their_sheets_and_index calls `_process_video` directly, and test_the_local_runner_can_see_an_organised_clip only exercises parallel_local.eligible_ids; nothing asserts that `run_pipeline` can see one.

**Failure scenario:** The mofa-disaster share is set `index: false`, its 554 clips are proxied and land at 'organised'. The decision is revisited (exactly the scenario creator_shares' docstring records for MOFA) and `index: true` is set in config.queue.yaml. The operator runs `broll-index run --config config.queue.yaml`, which prints "run: processed 0 video(s)" and does nothing — the clips are invisible. They only come back if someone happens to run parallel_local.py instead, and `run_queue.py --stages claude` (which uses parallel_claude, keyed on status 'proxied') will never see them either.

**Suggested fix:** Add `ORGANISED_STATUS` to run_pipeline's base `statuses` list (the reopen is a dict lookup and an immediate return for shares still opted out, and any row that stays 'organised' is declined by every status-gated stage), and add a test that drives `run_pipeline` — not `_process_video` — over an organised row on a re-enabled share.

**Verifier:** Verified. pipeline.py:648 builds `statuses = ["discovered", "probed", "proxied"]`, widened only with ["indexed","sorted"] for transcribe/embed, and storage.videos_by_status (sqlite_backend.py:93-105) filters `status IN (...)` exactly — ORGANISED_STATUS ('organised', pipeline.py:73) is never in that list, so _process_video's reopen_if_now_indexable call at :498-500 is unreachable from run_pipeline. parallel_local.eligible_ids (parallel_local.py:113-121) does include 'organised' with a comment saying why, so the two runners genuinely disagree. Test inventory checks out: the only run_pipeline tests are test_pipeline.py:129/166 and test_pipeline_embed.py:297 (none organised), and test_share_not_indexed.py drives _process_video directly (:208-226) or only parallel_local.eligible_ids (:229-236). MITIGATION the hunter did not mention: the normal overnight entry point run_queue.py runs parallel_local for the whole local phase before the API phase, so an organised clip on a re-enabled share IS reopened there; the dead path is `broll-index run` (and `run_queue --stages ...`, which bypasses the local phase). Also note the live DB currently has zero 'organised' rows (statuses: discovered 151, error 47, excluded 5304, indexed 7108, skipped 2493), so nothing is stranded right now.

### BROLL-IDX-5 (major, logic) — dedupe's filename claims are only stable if the eligible set grows at the tail; a row entering at a lower id renames an already-published clip and overwrites its archive file with different footage
`broll/indexer/build_archive.py:254`

`dedupe` resolves collisions against this run's claims only, and the docstring asserts "Because `videos` is walked in id order, the same clip resolves to the same name on every run. That stability is a requirement, not a nicety: the path is stored on the row and ends up in editors' timelines, so it must not move underneath them." That holds only while `eligible()` is monotone at the head, and it is not: `eligible()` deliberately excludes rows that will later join at their own (low) id — Downloads rows at status 'proxied' with a NULL category (the BROLL-7 carve-out at :326-330), plus every 'discovered' and 'error' row that is later re-run to 'indexed'. On the live DB there are 151 'discovered' and 47 'error' rows scattered below the maximum indexed id (15100), and 208 archive destinations already collide on (folder, safe_name(stem)) among currently-eligible rows — so the `_2` suffix is in active use for 208 clips. When a lower-id row joins, it claims the base name, every later colliding clip shifts by one, `write_paths` rewrites their `archive_path` (:429-431), and the copy loop then writes the new occupant's bytes over the file at the base path — a path already recorded on a row, already served by the web app, and already imported into editors' timelines.

**Failure scenario:** Clip 3120 (Downloads/society/government-politics/"How dictatorship built Taiwan's democracy".mp4) is indexed and shipped; clip 9840 has the same title in the same category and was shipped as `..._2.mp4`. Clip 2050, sitting at status 'error' from a failed claude call, is retried and reaches 'indexed' with the same category and title. The next `build_archive.py --apply` walks 2050 first: 2050 claims the base name, 3120 becomes `_2`, 9840 becomes `_3`. write_paths rewrites all three rows, and the copy loop overwrites the base-name file (clip 3120's footage, already cut into a timeline) with clip 2050's. The editor's cut silently shows different footage, and the two `_N` copies are new files while the old `_2` is stranded.

**Suggested fix:** Stop deriving the on-disk name from a per-run ordinal. Either (a) make the claim sticky: seed `taken` from the `archive_path` values already stored in the DB before planning, and never re-derive a name for a row that already has one; or (b) make the suffix content-derived and id-stable (e.g. `<stem>__<video_id>` on collision) so a clip's name depends only on itself. Either way, never rewrite an existing non-NULL `archive_path` to a different value without an explicit migration flag.

**Verifier:** Verified. dedupe (build_archive.py:254-278) resolves only against `taken`, which main() seeds empty each run (:376) and fills in eligible()'s id order; write_paths (:340-348) then unconditionally UPDATEs archive_path for every planned preview (:426-431), and the copy loop rewrites any destination whose size differs (`todo` filter at :434-435, then `shutil.copy2` + `tmp.replace(dst)` at :456-467) — so a shifted claim overwrites an already-published file in place. The monotonicity assumption really is violated: eligible() (:280-330) admits indexed/proxied/organised/audio-only-skipped and explicitly carves out Downloads rows at 'proxied' with NULL category (BROLL-7), while the live DB has 151 'discovered' and 47 'error' rows ALL below the max indexed id (15100) that can later reach 'indexed' at their own low id. Collisions are already live: 61 stored archive_paths end in _2.._9. The docstring's stability claim ("the same clip resolves to the same name on every run") is therefore only true if no lower-id row ever becomes eligible, which is not enforced anywhere. Only caveat: if the new occupant happens to match the old file's byte size the copy is skipped, leaving the row pointing at the other clip's file instead of overwriting it — still wrong, just silently rather than destructively.

### BROLL-IDX-6 (major, inefficiency) — do_scan re-reads and re-hashes 16 MiB of every already-indexed file on every rescan, over the network shares
`broll/indexer/broll_index/scanner.py:197`

`do_scan` calls `hash_file_partial(sf.path)` unconditionally for every file it walks, before it even looks up whether the row already exists. `hash_file_partial` reads the first 8 MiB and the last 8 MiB of the file (hashing.py:22-34), so a rescan of a share transfers ~16 MiB per file over the wire purely to recompute a digest that is already stored on the row and cannot have changed unless the file did. The size is available for free from the same `stat()` two lines above and is already stored as `size_bytes`, so the cheap discriminator is right there. The shares in config.queue.yaml are 25 network roots (I:, Z:, H: — the scanner's own docstring cites "a share rooted at a whole project over the NAS" and stage_proxy cites "source media on a 46 MB/s network share"), and the live DB holds 15,100 rows.

**Failure scenario:** Re-running `broll-index scan --share ff4-nuclear --root Z:/...` after adding a handful of new downloads walks the whole tree and re-hashes every file already in the index. On a 4,482-file share that is ~70 GB read at 46 MB/s — roughly 25 minutes of pure network IO — to discover that nothing but the new files changed. Multiplied across the 25 configured shares, a full rescan pass is hours of avoidable transfer.

**Suggested fix:** Fetch `existing = storage.get_video_by_path(...)` first, and skip `hash_file_partial` when `existing is not None and existing["size_bytes"] == size` (optionally also comparing mtime), reusing the stored hash. Keep the unconditional hash behind an explicit `--rehash` flag for the rare in-place-modification case.

**Verifier:** Verified at scanner.py:196-198: `size = sf.path.stat().st_size` then `digest = hash_file_partial(sf.path)` is computed unconditionally, two lines BEFORE `existing = storage.get_video_by_path(share, sf.rel_path)` (:200), and the stored hash/size are then just refreshed. hash_file_partial (hashing.py:22-34) reads 8 MiB head + 8 MiB tail (CHUNK_SIZE = 8*1024*1024), so ~16 MiB per file over the wire for every already-known file. No documented decision defends the unconditional re-hash — the do_scan docstring only says a rescan "refreshes hash/size_bytes/in_inbox" and never resets status; nothing in CLAUDE.md, KNOWN_BUGS.md or docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md addresses it, and no test pins the behaviour (test_hashing.py only checks determinism). config.queue.yaml is indeed 25 network roots and the DB holds ~15,100 rows, so the size-first shortcut is a real and safe win; the only thing the suggested fix loses is detection of a same-size in-place edit, which is exactly what the proposed --rehash flag covers.

### BROLL-IDX-7 (major, upgrade) — UNCERTAIN — Frame extraction spawns one ffmpeg process per timestamp on top of a full scene-detect decode; the same graph could produce sprite, poster and every sampled frame in one decode pass
`broll/indexer/broll_index/ffmpeg_tools.py:509`

Goal: turn one clip into a sprite sheet, a poster, and the scene-sampled JPEGs that become contact sheets. Current approach, per clip: `build_sprite` decodes the proxy end to end (one process), `build_poster` seeks (one process), `detect_scene_timestamps` decodes the proxy end to end AGAIN and throws every decoded frame away, keeping only showinfo's pts_time values (one process), then `extract_frames` spawns a fresh `ffmpeg -ss ... -frames:v 1` for EACH timestamp, then `build_contact_sheets` spawns one more per 9 frames. `fill_gaps` guarantees at least duration/max_gap_s points, and with max_gap_s=4.0 and the live archive's mean duration of 222 s that is ~56 extraction processes plus ~7 tiling processes — roughly 65 process spawns and 2 full decodes per clip, ~460,000 ffmpeg invocations across the 7,108 indexed rows. Better approach: the scene-detect pass already has the frames in hand — write them out in the same pass (`-vf select='gt(scene,T)',showinfo -vsync 0 frames/scene_%04d.jpg` gives timestamps and images together), and use one `-filter_complex` with `split` to emit the sprite tile, the poster and the gap-fill frames from that single decode. Only the gap-fill timestamps that scene detection did not already cover would need any extra work, and those can go in the same graph via a `select` expression rather than N separate seeks.

**Failure scenario:** Indexing the remaining queue: for each of ~7,000 clips the local pipeline pays two full decodes of the 540p proxy plus ~63 process creations. On Windows a python->ffmpeg spawn costs ~50-100 ms before any work happens, so process overhead alone is ~4-6 s per clip (~8-11 hours across the archive) on top of the duplicated decode, and `parallel_local.py --workers 6` multiplies the spawn storm rather than removing it. The measured serial rate the module docstrings quote (0.7 videos/min) is dominated by exactly this.

**Suggested fix:** Fold scene detection and frame extraction into one ffmpeg invocation that writes the selected frames while it detects them (parse showinfo's pts_time to label them, keeping extract_frames' (timestamp, path) pairing contract), and emit sprite + poster from the same decode via `split`. Keep the current per-timestamp path as a fallback for the gap-fill points that the single pass cannot cover, and keep the existing "only keep frames ffmpeg actually produced" guard.

**Verifier:** The factual description is accurate — build_sprite (ffmpeg_tools.py:389-397) does a full fps=1/N decode, detect_scene_timestamps (:441-451) does a second full decode discarding frames, extract_frames (:509-528) spawns one `ffmpeg -ss ... -frames:v 1` per timestamp, and fill_gaps at max_gap_s=4.0 yields ~duration/4 points, so ~50-60 spawns per clip is right. But I could not establish the proposal as viable as written, on three counts. (1) build_sprite and build_poster are called from stage_proxy (pipeline.py:177-180), a DIFFERENT status-gated stage from stage_frames (:208-231); the split is load-bearing for the index:false/'organised' design, which deliberately produces proxy+poster+sprite and NO contact sheets (pipeline.py:62-72), so folding sprite/poster into the frames decode crosses a documented boundary. (2) The gap-fill timestamps are derived FROM the detection output (fill_gaps runs after detect), so they cannot be emitted by the same graph as detection; on a typical clip with few real scene cuts most of the ~56 points are gap-fill, so the single-pass detect+write saves only the minority of spawns — a batched second pass with one select expression would be the actual win, which is not quite what is proposed. (3) The quantified payoff is overstated: the live DB is already 7,108 indexed rows with stage_frames marker-idempotent (sheets_dir/_complete, pipeline.py:210-212) and only ~200 runnable rows left, so the "~7,000 clips / 8-11 hours" figure describes work already done, not pending work. Also extract_frames uses input-side `-ss` (seek before -i), which is not a full decode per frame. Real inefficiency, unproven upgrade.

### BROLL-IDX-8 (minor, inefficiency) — stage_embed commits one SQLite transaction and one search-generation bump per row instead of per video
`broll/indexer/broll_index/storage/sqlite_backend.py:263`

`update_search_norm` (:235-243) and `upsert_embedding` (:251-264) each write one row, call `bump_search_generation`, and `commit()`. `stage_embed` calls them in a loop over every segment and every transcript cue of a video, so a first embed pass over the live corpus (53,226 segments + 29,783 transcript cues) is ~83,000 separate transactions, each with its own fsync and its own UPDATE of the single `meta` row. The already-batched twin of this work, `embed_transcripts.py:59-72`, does exactly the right thing — one `executemany` plus one `bump_search_generation` plus one `commit` per 256-row chunk — so the correct pattern is already in the repo; the pipeline stage just does not use it. The per-row `meta` UPDATE also makes that one row a hot spot that every concurrent writer contends on.

**Failure scenario:** Changing `embedding.model` in config.queue.yaml and running `broll-index run --stages embed` re-embeds the whole archive: 83,000 commits, each fsyncing the WAL and rewriting the same `meta` row, when ~7,100 (one per video) would do. On the base rig this turns a vector computation that is minutes of ONNX work into tens of minutes dominated by transaction overhead, and it serialises against parallel_local.py's own row updates through the shared hot `meta` row.

**Suggested fix:** Give the backend batch entry points (`update_search_norms(list)`, `upsert_embeddings(list)`) that do one `executemany`, one `bump_search_generation`, and one `commit` for the whole video — the shape `embed_transcripts.py` already uses — and have `stage_embed` accumulate per video and flush once. Keep the single-row methods for the web-ingest path if it needs them.

**Verifier:** Verified. update_search_norm (sqlite_backend.py:235-243) and upsert_embedding (:251-264) each do one row write + bump_search_generation(conn) (which UPDATEs the single meta row, :30-45) + self.conn.commit(), and stage_embed (pipeline.py:460-484) calls them inside per-segment and per-cue loops with no batching. The batched twin exists exactly as claimed: embed_transcripts.py:55-72 does one executemany + one bump + one commit per --batch chunk. Live counts match the finding: 53,226 segments, 29,783 transcript_segments, 82,889 embeddings — so a model-change re-embed really is ~83k commits where ~7.1k would do. One refinement: search_norm is only written when missing (`if not seg.get("search_norm")`), so on a model change the 83k transactions are the upsert_embedding calls alone, not search_norm too. Severity 'minor' is fair — correctness is unaffected, bump_search_generation is still in the same transaction as its write either way.

## MUSIC — Music platform (web + indexer)

### MUSIC-1 (critical, bug) — The text-encoder artefact is written to disk BEFORE it is verified, so a failed check leaves a loadable, drifting artefact behind
`music/indexer/export_text_encoder.py:284`

`export()` writes `text_encoder.onnx` (line 241), `tokenizer.json` (line 260) and `manifest.json` (line 284) into the real output directory, and only then runs `verify_tokenizer()` (287) and `verify()` (293). Both of those raise `SystemExit` on drift. The module docstring's promise — "The exporter refuses to write an artefact whose embeddings differ from ClapModel.get_text_features" and "`export_text_encoder` refuses to write an artefact that is not [the same numbers]" (musicweb/text_encoder.py:23-24) — is therefore false: the refusal happens after the write, not instead of it. Worse, `musicweb.text_encoder.artefact_available()` only checks that the manifest parses, that `artefact_version == 1`, and that both files exist (text_encoder.py:308-317) — it never looks at `manifest['check']`, which is exactly the key that is missing on a failed export. So the rejected artefact loads cleanly on the next boot, `Index.encoder` picks it over the CLAP fallback, and every `/api/search` result is silently reordered by a model the exporter itself declared wrong. A re-export that fails also destroys the previously-good artefact it overwrote, with no backup.

**Failure scenario:** Base rig, after a `transformers` upgrade that changes `ClapProcessor`'s tokenizer file or the pooler layout: `python export_text_encoder.py` writes text_encoder.onnx + tokenizer.json + manifest.json, then `verify_tokenizer` finds 3 of 49 queries tokenize differently and exits 1 with "Not writing the artefact." The operator reads that message and moves on. `music/web/data/text_encoder/` now holds a complete, version-1 artefact with no `check` key. `--music-data encoder` ships it to the NAS; `artefact_available()` returns True; `OnnxTextEncoder` loads; `POST /music/api/search {"query":"tense driving synth pulse"}` returns a plausible-looking but wrongly-ranked top-60 with no error anywhere in the log, and the good artefact it replaced is gone.

**Suggested fix:** Export into a temp directory (or `<out>.new`), run `verify_tokenizer()` and `verify()` against that directory, and only `os.replace`/rename it into `out_dir` after both pass — so a failed export leaves the previous artefact untouched. Belt and braces: have `artefact_available()` also require `manifest.get('check', {}).get('min_cosine', 0) >= MIN_COSINE`, so an artefact that was never verified cannot be loaded even if it reaches the disk some other way.

**Verifier:** Read export_text_encoder.py in full. The ONNX file (line 241-248), tokenizer.json (260) and manifest.json (284) are all written straight into the real out_dir (235-236, no temp/rename), and verify_tokenizer (287) / verify (293) run only afterwards, both raising SystemExit ('Not writing the artefact' / 'Not writing it') AFTER the write. The 'check' key is only added by the second manifest write at 297, so a failed run leaves a manifest with artefact_version=1 and no 'check'. musicweb/text_encoder.py:308-317 artefact_available() checks only manifest parse + artefact_version==1 + both files present — it never looks at 'check', so load() (328-330) returns OnnxTextEncoder for the rejected artefact and Index.encoder (search.py:55-57) picks it over the CLAP fallback. No guard elsewhere: tests/test_text_encoder.py:190-195 does assert m['check']['min_cosine'] >= 0.999, but only against the artefact that happens to be on disk and only when it exists (@artefact skip), so it cannot stop a bad artefact loading at runtime. No documented decision anywhere (KNOWN_BUGS.md, docs/bug-hunt-2026-08-11.md, SPEC.md) covers this; the module docstring at 43-46 and text_encoder.py:23-24 assert the opposite of what the code does. Overwrite-destroys-previous is also real (torch.onnx.export + shutil.copy2 overwrite in place, no backup).

### MUSIC-2 (major, bug) — /api/ingest is an async endpoint that runs the whole blocking ingest on the event loop, freezing the entire fleet dashboard
`music/web/musicweb/routes_ingest.py:282`

`ingest_files` (line 267), `_ingest_queued` (275) and `_ingest_inline` (288) are all `async def`, so FastAPI runs them directly on the event loop rather than in the threadpool. `queue_one(up.filename, data, c)` at line 282 is a plain synchronous call that does, per file: `staged.write_bytes(data)`, `_probe()` (a `subprocess.run` with `timeout=120`), `db.find_reencode()` (a table scan), `_transcode_to_mp3()` (a `subprocess.run` with `timeout=900`), `db.content_hash()` plus `db.find_content_duplicate()` (which blake2b-hashes every library file whose byte count matches, reading them over the share mount), and `shutil.move()` across two different bind mounts (`/music-data/staging` → `…/Assets/Music`). None of it yields. This app is mounted in-process inside the dashboard (CLAUDE.md, DEPLOY.md), so for the whole duration the dashboard's event loop is blocked — sync routes cannot even be dispatched to their threadpool, because the loop that dispatches them is stuck. CLAUDE.md's own rule is that "the dashboard is what tells everyone whether their footage is syncing" outranks any feature. `_ingest_inline` has the same shape and additionally holds the loop through `library_hashes()` and a full CLAP embed + `retag()`.

**Failure scenario:** An editor drags eight 55 MB .wav cues onto /music. `_ingest_queued` loops eight times on the event loop: each iteration writes 55 MB to staging, shells out to ffprobe, hashes the file, hashes the one or two library files with a matching byte count off the NAS mount, then copies 55 MB across mounts (staging and the share are different binds, so `shutil.move` degrades to copy+unlink). For the ~30–60 s that takes, every other request to the dashboard — sync-status pages, `/api/report` from every companion in the fleet, the container healthcheck — is queued behind the loop and nothing is served. Drop a single .ogg and the 900 s ffmpeg timeout becomes the worst-case freeze.

**Suggested fix:** Make the endpoint synchronous (`def ingest_files(...)`, reading uploads with `up.file.read()`), so FastAPI dispatches it to the threadpool; or keep it async and push every blocking step through `starlette.concurrency.run_in_threadpool` (`await run_in_threadpool(queue_one, up.filename, data, c)`). Same treatment for `_ingest_inline`'s `library_hashes()`, `ingest_one()` and `index_music.retag()`.

**Verifier:** routes_ingest.py:266-313: ingest_files, _ingest_queued and _ingest_inline are all `async def`, so Starlette runs them on the event loop, and queue_one (196-263) is a plain sync call doing write_bytes, subprocess.run ffprobe (timeout=120), db.find_reencode, _transcode_to_mp3 (timeout=900), content_hash + find_content_duplicate (which hashes matching-size library files off the mount), and shutil.move — none of it awaited or dispatched. Nothing in the tree wraps it: `grep run_in_threadpool` over music/ returns zero hits, while dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1841,1864 and ui.py use run_in_threadpool for exactly this reason, so the pattern is established elsewhere in the repo. Single event loop is confirmed: dashboard/deploy/run.sh:128-129 runs `uvicorn --workers 1`, and CLAUDE.md:40-46 pins the music app as an in-process mount, so the block is the whole dashboard. CLAUDE.md:151-153 states the dashboard-availability rule the hunter cites. No comment or doc defends the async shape.

### MUSIC-3 (major, logic) — Nothing ever removes a tracks row whose file left the library, so a renamed cue survives as a ghost that previews fine and only fails at "send to Resolve"
`music/indexer/index_music.py:407`

`main()` builds `todo` from `iter_files(root)` (line 403-408) and `upsert()` keys on `ON CONFLICT(rel_path)` (index_music.py:107). There is no reverse sweep: nothing in music/ ever issues a `DELETE FROM tracks` (grep confirms the only prune in the tree is `make_proxies.py --prune`, and that deletes *proxies* whose id is no longer in `tracks` — it is a no-op for a row that is still there). So renaming or deleting a file under `W:\Creators_Club\Assets\Music` leaves the old row, its `windows`, its `tags`/`axes` and its `peaks` in place forever. SPEC.md says "roughly a third of the library is named only by a numeric library ID or a UUID", i.e. renaming files to something human is an expected operation on this library. The ghost row is not inert: `db.load_matrix` feeds it into `Index.track_mat`/`sim_mat`, so it shows up in search and in `/api/similar`; `retag()` includes it in the library-relative percentile computation, skewing every other track's `pct`; and because proxies are keyed by track **id** and chosen on existence alone (`routes_media.audio_source`, config.py:75-82), the stale row still streams its old 128k proxy perfectly. The only place the ghost fails is the last one — the companion's `/music/send`, which resolves (share, rel_path) against P: and answers "file not found".

**Failure scenario:** On the base rig, `12345_67.aac` in the flat library is renamed to `Neon City.aac`. `python index_music.py` indexes the new name as id 401 and leaves row id 4 (`12345_67.aac`) untouched. The next `--music-data db` push ships both. An editor searches "retro neon synthwave", gets two hits that sound identical, plays both (id 4 streams from `proxies/4.mp3`, which is still on disk and still correct audio), picks id 4, clicks "insert at playhead" — and the companion answers `file not found at P:\Assets\Music\12345_67.aac — is the share mounted?`, which sends them to debug a mount that is fine.

**Suggested fix:** After the sweep in `main()` (before `retag()`), delete rows whose `rel_path` is not in the set yielded by `iter_files(root)` — guarded so it only runs on a full, unlimited, unfiltered sweep (never under `--limit`, `--queue` or `--db` against a partial copy), and behind a printed count or a `--prune` flag if silent deletion is unwanted. `windows`/`tags`/`axes`/`peaks` already cascade on `ON DELETE CASCADE`; follow it with `make_proxies.py --prune` to reclaim the orphaned proxies.

**Verifier:** index_music.py:401-441: `known` is keyed on rel_path, todo comes from iter_files(root), upsert (98-132) is ON CONFLICT(rel_path); there is no reverse sweep and no DELETE FROM tracks anywhere in music/ (grep over the tree returns only make_proxies.py --prune, which at :54 deletes proxy FILES whose id is absent from tracks — a no-op for a live ghost row). db.load_matrix (db.py:194-203) selects every row with an embedding, so the ghost is in Index.track_mat/sim_mat and in search and /api/similar; retag (135-158) re-scores over all ids, so the ghost skews the library-relative percentiles. routes_media.audio_source (79-100) returns proxies/{id}.mp3 on existence alone, so the ghost previews correctly; the only failure is the companion's message, which is verbatim in music_server.py:284 ('file not found at … — is the share mounted?'). config.py:64-70 even documents that proxies are id-keyed so 'a rename changes rel_path but not the id' — but nothing implements rename detection, so a rename produces a second row and orphans the first. No doc treats the absence of a prune as deliberate.

### MUSIC-4 (major, bug) — eval.py --sweep writes its scratch tag scores into the live shipped index and leaves it calibrated at alpha=1.0
`music/eval/eval.py:109`

`main()` opens the real index (`db.connect()` with no argument → `config.DB_PATH` = `music/web/data/music.db`, the file that ships to the NAS) and the sweep loop calls `tagging.write_scores(con, ids, tags, axvals)` once per alpha. `write_scores` is not a read: it does `DELETE FROM tags`, `DELETE FROM axes`, re-inserts every row, and commits (tagging.py:77-100). The comment directly above the loop claims "the sweep only needs to re-score tags **in memory**" (line 98-99), which is exactly what the code does not do. The loop iterates `(0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1.0)` and never restores anything, so when it finishes the live index's `tags.score`, `tags.pct`, `tags.rank` and every `axes` row correspond to `CALIBRATION = 1.0`, while `vocab.CALIBRATION` is 0.5 (vocab.py:142). Nothing warns, and the corruption is invisible — the rows are all present and well-formed, just scored under a setting nobody chose. `vocab_hash()` will not catch it either: it hashes CATEGORIES/AXES/TOP_K/TEMPERATURE and deliberately not CALIBRATION.

**Failure scenario:** Run `python eval.py --sweep` on the base rig to compare calibration strengths, read the table, ctrl-C nothing. `music/web/data/music.db` now holds alpha=1.0 tags. The next routine `install_dashboard_app.py --music-data db` pushes it to the NAS, and every facet chip count, every `pct` the axis sliders filter on, and every rank-1 label in `/music` is the sweep's last iteration rather than the tuned 0.5 — until somebody happens to run `index_music.py --retag` again. If the sweep is interrupted mid-loop (a 200-label CLAP text pass per alpha is slow), the index is left at whichever alpha it reached.

**Suggested fix:** Have the sweep operate on a throwaway copy — `shutil.copy2(config.DB_PATH, tmp)` then `db.connect(tmp)` — or compute the rank-1 concentration `bias_report` needs directly from the in-memory `tags['score']` arrays instead of round-tripping through SQLite. Failing that, re-run `write_scores` with `vocab.CALIBRATION` restored in a `finally:` and make `--sweep` refuse to run against `config.DB_PATH` without an explicit `--i-know-this-writes` flag.

**Verifier:** eval/eval.py:86 opens db.connect() with no path → musicweb.config.DB_PATH = music/web/data/music.db (config.py:53-58), the exact file install_dashboard_app.install_music_db ships. The sweep at 106-112 sets vocab.CALIBRATION and calls tagging.write_scores(con, ids, tags, axvals) per alpha; write_scores (music_index/tagging.py:77-100) starts with `DELETE FROM tags` / `DELETE FROM axes`, re-inserts and con.commit()s — so the comment at eval.py:98-99 ('the sweep only needs to re-score tags in memory') is false. The loop ends at alpha=1.0 and nothing restores vocab.CALIBRATION=0.5 (vocab.py:142) or re-runs write_scores; there is no try/finally and no copy. vocab_hash (vocab.py:159-163) hashes [CATEGORIES, AXES, TOP_K, TEMPERATURE] only, so it cannot detect the drift, exactly as claimed. README.md:118 documents `eval.py --sweep` with no warning that it writes the live index.

### MUSIC-5 (major, logic) — Index.encoder's lazy model load is unguarded, so two concurrent first searches build two ONNX sessions (or two full CLAP models) at once
`music/web/musicweb/search.py:56`

`Index.encoder` (55-57) and `Index.clap` (73-77) are plain check-then-assign lazy properties with no lock, while `index()` guards only the *construction* of the Index object (147-158). FastAPI dispatches the sync `/api/search` route across a threadpool, so two requests that arrive before the first load completes both observe `self._encoder is None` and both call `text_encoder.load()`. Each `OnnxTextEncoder` mmaps/loads the ~500 MB `text_encoder.onnx` and opens its own `InferenceSession` with 4 intra-op threads; the loser's session is only reclaimed when the last reference drops. On the CLAP fallback path (dev checkout, or a NAS where the artefact never shipped) each loser is a full 777 MB `ClapModel`. This is the one place the whole design is built around memory — the module docstring, SPEC.md and DEPLOY.md all justify ONNX purely on "54 MB unpacked vs 675 MB" in a container that also runs the fleet dashboard — and the peak is quietly double.

**Failure scenario:** The dashboard container is restarted after a deploy. Two editors hit /music within the ~2 s cold-load window (or one editor types a query and immediately clicks a different example button, since `runSearch` does not cancel the in-flight request — `state.seq` only suppresses the *render*). Both threadpool threads enter `Index.encoder` with `_encoder is None`, both construct `OnnxTextEncoder`, and RSS peaks at ~1 GB of onnxruntime arenas plus 8 intra-op threads in a container that is simultaneously serving every companion's status report.

**Suggested fix:** Guard both properties with a lock — a module-level `threading.Lock` (or `functools.cache`d module function) taken around the check-and-assign, re-checking `self._encoder is None` inside it, exactly the pattern `index()` already uses for `_index`.

**Verifier:** search.py:44-57 and 59-77 are unguarded check-then-assign; index() (151-158) takes _index_lock only around construction and returns inside the with, so the lock is released before `.encoder`/`.clap` is touched. /api/search (routes_api.py:133-138) is a sync def, so FastAPI dispatches it across the anyio threadpool and two cold requests can both see _encoder is None and both call text_encoder.load(). Nothing pre-warms it: no startup hook in musicweb/main.py, and the only index() callers are routes_api.py:138,153 and routes_ingest.py:290. Each loser is a full ort.InferenceSession over the ~500 MB artefact (text_encoder.py:256-257, intra_op_num_threads=4) or, on the fallback, a full ClapModel (clap_model.py has no module-level cache — every Clap() calls ClapModel.from_pretrained). The lock the class does own (text_encoder.py:258, 273) guards only the BPE cache inside embed_text, not construction.

### MUSIC-6 (minor, upgrade) — The "reveal" button drives Explorer on the SERVER, so it is a permanently dead control for every editor — the same mistake port step 8 fixed for the Resolve actions and 2026-08-11 fixed for ytdl
`music/web/musicweb/routes_ingest.py:353`

`GET /api/reveal/{track_id}` runs `subprocess.Popen(['explorer', f'/select,{path}'])` on the host serving the page. Port step 8 moved `/api/resolve*` off this server for precisely this reason — the block comment at lines 332-342 spells it out: "They drove Resolve on whatever host served the page, which is right on the base rig and useless-to-wrong once this app is mounted on the NAS." Reveal was left behind. On the NAS container `os.name != 'nt'`, so the route falls straight to `return {'ok': False, 'path': str(path)}` (line 360) — and `app.js`'s handler is `rev.onclick = () => api('api/reveal/'+t.id).catch(() => {})` (static/app.js:178), which discards the result entirely. The editor clicks a button in the player pane and *nothing happens, ever*, with no message. The response also hands the browser the NAS's absolute filesystem path, which the (share, rel_path) model exists to keep out of the payload. The precedent for the fix already exists in this repo: the ytdl page's reveal was moved onto the companion loopback as `POST /ytdl/reveal` on 2026-08-11 (broll_server.py:13-14, 710-720), next to the `/music/send` and `/music/status` this same pane already calls.

**Failure scenario:** An editor on /music opens a cue's pane and clicks "reveal" to find the file on P: before importing it by hand. The browser GETs `/music/api/reveal/137`; the container returns `200 {"ok": false, "path": "/mnt/tank/.../Assets/Music/ES_Tense Pulse.wav"}`; app.js swallows it; no window opens and no error is shown. Repeat on any machine, forever.

**Suggested fix:** Add `POST /music/reveal` to `ccsync_companion/music_server.py` taking `{share, rel_path}` (translated by `local_path_for`, same as `/music/send`) and have `app.js` call `companion('/music/reveal', …)` instead of `api('api/reveal/…')`, reporting failures into the existing `msg` element. Delete `/api/reveal` from routes_ingest.py with the same comment the removed `/api/resolve*` routes got.

**Verifier:** routes_ingest.py:345-360 still runs subprocess.Popen(['explorer', …]) on the serving host, and falls to `return {'ok': False, 'path': str(path)}` when os.name != 'nt' — a 200, so static/app.js:62-66 api() does not throw and :178 `rev.onclick = () => api('api/reveal/'+t.id).catch(()=>{})` discards it silently. The port-8 precedent is right above it (332-342) and the ytdl precedent exists: companion broll_server.py:13-14 and :710-720 dispatch POST /ytdl/reveal to ytdl_server.build_reveal_response with the mount map, beside /music/send (music_server.build_send_response, :700-708) — so adding POST /music/reveal to the same single 8899 listener does not conflict with the 'one listener on 8899' constraint. Stronger than the hunter states: music/web/DEPLOY.md:322-324 already claims '/api/resolve* and /api/reveal — port step 8 landed: they live in ccsync_companion/music_server.py', which is false — grep shows no 'reveal' in music_server.py at all. tests/test_reveal.py's docstring acknowledges 'on the NAS container it answers ok: False and touches nothing', but nothing documents the dead button as intended.

### MUSIC-7 (minor, upgrade) — Inline ingest hashes the entire 9.5 GB library on every drag-and-drop, when the queued half already has a bytes-indexed check that gives the same answer in one or two file reads
`music/indexer/music_index/ingest.py:40`

`_ingest_inline` calls `_ingest.library_hashes()` once per request (routes_ingest.py:292), and that function `rglob`s the whole share root and blake2b-hashes every audio file it finds. The library is 9.5 GB over 376 files (SPEC.md), and on the base rig `W:` is an SMB mount of the NAS, not local disk — so that is a 9.5 GB network read to answer "is this one dropped file already here". The queued half solved this properly: `db.find_content_duplicate` (db.py:329-360) notes that identical content implies an identical byte count, so it only opens the rows whose `bytes` already match, backed by `idx_tracks_bytes` — "the same answer, one or two file reads instead of 376". The two halves are supposed to reject the same duplicates from the same shared module (db.py's docstring), and here the base rig is the one running the naive version. The stated reason ("which is fine over a local W:") is not true of an SMB-mounted W:, and the base rig is exactly where the correct implementation already lives on the import path.

**Failure scenario:** An editor drops one 4 MB mp3 onto /music running standalone on the base rig. Before ffprobe is even consulted, `library_hashes()` reads and hashes all 376 files — ~9.5 GB across SMB at ~700 MB/s is ~14 s of pure IO for a single small upload, and far worse whenever the share is contended by a proxy run or a sync lane. The one caveat `find_content_duplicate` has (files in the library that no `tracks` row knows about are missed) is already the documented trade-off, and the sweep catches those anyway.

**Suggested fix:** Have `ingest_one` call `db.find_content_duplicate(con, staged, digest)` instead of the `known` dict, dropping `library_hashes()` and the `known` parameter from `_ingest_inline`. If the "files the DB does not know about" case is wanted on the base rig specifically, keep `library_hashes()` behind an explicit opt-in rather than paying for it on every request.

**Verifier:** routes_ingest.py:292 calls _ingest.library_hashes() once per request, and music_index/ingest.py:40-54 rglobs config.share_root() and blake2b-hashes every audio file. The premise the existing docstring rests on is checkable and false here: db.py:329-341 says the base rig 'answers this by hashing every file under the share root … which is fine over a local W:', but `net use` on this rig shows W: = \\192.168.0.102\TheCreatorsPool (SMB to the TrueNAS), not local disk — so the 9.5 GB/376-file read is over the same mount the container was spared. db.find_content_duplicate (329-360) is importable from the inline half (music_index/ingest.py already imports content_hash/find_reencode/unique_dest from musicweb.db at line 28) and is backed by the bytes index, and its one documented gap (library files with no tracks row) is already accepted as the base rig's job. Within-batch duplicates still get caught because the inline path upserts each accepted track immediately (ingest.py:130) before the next file is read. Viable upgrade, no documented constraint against it.

### MUSIC-8 (minor, inefficiency) — refresh() builds a brand-new Index, throwing away the loaded text encoder and the loaded CLAP model with it
`music/web/musicweb/search.py:169`

`Index.__init__` sets `self._clap = None` and `self._encoder = None` (search.py:41-42), and `refresh()` (161-172) replaces the module singleton with a fresh `Index`. Neither model depends on anything the Index holds — the encoder is a pure function of the artefact directory, and `Clap` is a pure function of `CLAP_MODEL` — but both are cached *on the Index*, so every swap discards them. `refresh(c)` is called at the end of every successful inline ingest (routes_ingest.py:306) and by `POST /api/reload` (routes_api.py:169). So on the base rig, dropping file A loads the full 777 MB CLAP through `index().clap`, then `refresh()` drops it, and dropping file B reloads it from scratch — 2.6 s and a full GPU/host allocation per drop, plus the same again for the ONNX text encoder on the container after any reload.

**Failure scenario:** Base rig, drag-and-drop of three cues one at a time (the normal way an editor adds a track they just licensed). Each drop pays a fresh `ClapModel.from_pretrained` + `.to('cuda')` — three full model loads instead of one — because the `refresh(c)` at the end of drop N nulled the `_clap` that drop N+1 then re-creates at routes_ingest.py:290. On the NAS, `POST /api/reload` after a data push means the next `/api/search` pays the 2 s ONNX cold load again for no reason.

**Suggested fix:** Move `_encoder` and `_clap` out of `Index` into module-level lazily-initialised singletons in search.py (guarded by the lock from MUSIC-5), and have `Index` read them through module functions. The Index then holds only the DB-derived matrices, which is the only thing `refresh` actually needs to swap — and `refresh` becomes as cheap as its docstring implies.

**Verifier:** search.py:41-42 nulls _clap/_encoder in __init__, and refresh (161-172) constructs a whole new Index and rebinds the module singleton, so both cached models are dropped on every swap. refresh is called at routes_ingest.py:305 after each successful inline ingest and by routes_api.py:169 (/api/reload). music_index/clap_model.py has no module- or class-level cache: every Clap() runs ClapModel.from_pretrained + processor + .to(device), so the next drop's index().clap (routes_ingest.py:290) genuinely rebuilds the 777 MB model, and on the container the next /api/search rebuilds the ort.InferenceSession. Neither model depends on anything the Index holds (encoder is a pure function of the artefact dir; Clap of CLAP_MODEL), so hoisting them to module level conflicts with nothing documented — the only per-Index state that refresh actually needs to swap is the DB-derived matrices, as its own docstring implies.

### MUSIC-9 (minor, inefficiency) — Uploads are buffered whole in RAM (`await up.read()`) before being written to staging
`music/web/musicweb/routes_ingest.py:281`

Both ingest paths do `data = await up.read()` (line 281 queued, 295 inline) and then `staged.write_bytes(data)` (queue_one, line 216) / pass the bytes to `ingest_one`. Starlette already spools each multipart part to a `SpooledTemporaryFile` that rolls over to disk past 1 MB, so the bytes are on disk by then — reading them back into a single `bytes` object only to write them out again doubles the IO and puts the whole file in the dashboard container's heap. The library is 199 `.wav` files "up to 60 MB each" (config.py:60-61, DEPLOY.md), and the container is the one that also serves the fleet dashboard.

**Failure scenario:** An editor drops a 60 MB wav on /music. The container allocates a 60 MB `bytes` object on top of the spooled temp file, writes it back out to `/music-data/staging/ing-xxxx/`, then copies it again into the share — three passes over 60 MB and a 60 MB resident spike, per file, in a container sized for a FastAPI dashboard. A drop of a whole folder repeats it per file with the peak held for the duration of `queue_one`.

**Suggested fix:** Stream instead: open the staging destination and `shutil.copyfileobj(up.file, fh)` (after `up.file.seek(0)`), passing the staged `Path` into `queue_one`/`ingest_one` rather than a `bytes`. It removes the heap spike and one full copy, and pairs naturally with moving the work off the event loop (MUSIC-2).

**Verifier:** routes_ingest.py:281 and :295 both do `data = await up.read()`, and queue_one writes it out again at :216 (staged.write_bytes(data)) / music_index/ingest.py:90 does the same for the inline half, before shutil.move to the share (:246 / :121). Starlette's spooling is as described: formparsers.py:147 spool_max_size = 1024*1024 with SpooledTemporaryFile at :230, so anything over 1 MB is already on disk when read() copies it into a single bytes object. config.py:60-61 / DEPLOY.md confirm the 199 wav files up to ~60 MB, and the dashboard is the same single-worker process (run.sh:128-129). The streaming fix has in-repo precedent (dashboard api.py:1841 writes upload chunks through run_in_threadpool). Accurate, minor.

### MUSIC-10 (minor, logic) — UNCERTAIN — POST /api/reload cannot pick up a replaced music.db — every thread's cached connection still holds the old, unlinked file
`music/web/musicweb/routes_api.py:169`

`reload_index()` promises to "pick up a fresh index without restarting the server", but it only rebuilds the numpy matrices from `con()` — and `con()` returns a connection cached for the lifetime of the thread (`db.py:147-171`). The deployment ships a new index by *replacing the file*: `install_music_db` renames the live `music.db` to `music.db.old.<ts>` and moves the staged copy into place (server/install_dashboard_app.py:956-959, and DEPLOY.md's "the previous file is kept as music.db.old.<ts>"). A SQLite connection is bound to an inode, so after that swap every one of the app's per-thread connections is still reading the unlinked old database — and `refresh(con())` faithfully rebuilds the index from it. `/api/reload` therefore returns a 200 with stats from the *old* index and there is no code path in the app that can ever see the new one. Today this is masked by the deploy always doing `docker restart` (install_dashboard_app.py:2494); the route is a trap for the manual pull/drain/push loop in DEPLOY.md, where the whole point is to avoid a restart.

**Failure scenario:** Operator follows DEPLOY.md's "Draining the NAS ingest queue": pulls music.db, drains 3 queued uploads on the base rig, scp's the drained copy back over `/mnt/tank/apps/ccsync-dashboard/music-data/music.db` by hand (rather than re-running the full installer), then curls `POST /music/api/reload` to avoid restarting the dashboard. It returns `{"tracks": 376, …}` — the old count — and every subsequent /music request keeps serving the pre-drain index from the deleted inode until something restarts the container.

**Suggested fix:** Give `db` an explicit invalidation: bump a module-level generation counter and have `con()` close and re-open its thread-local connection when its stored generation is stale; call it from `reload_index()` before `refresh()`. Cheaper alternative: make `reload_index()` open a fresh short-lived `db.connect()`, build the Index from that, and close it — which at least makes the route honest about the matrices even if the read routes still need the generation bump.

**Verifier:** The two code facts check out: db.con() (db.py:147-171) caches one connection per thread for that thread's life, reload_index (routes_api.py:166-170) only calls refresh(con()), and install_music_db swaps by atomic rename to music.db.old.<ts> (install_dashboard_app.py:956-960 + build_db_swap_script), i.e. a new inode. But two things undercut the failure scenario as written. (1) The documented paths never hit it: DEPLOY.md's drain loop ends at `python install_dashboard_app.py --music-data db`, and the installer always restarts the container (install_dashboard_app.py:2492-2504); the route's documented use (README.md:58, 'after re-indexing while the server is running') is the base rig, where index_music.py rewrites the SAME file in place, so the cached connection sees the new rows and reload works correctly. The hand-rolled scp-then-curl variant is the hunter's own invention, not a step in DEPLOY.md. (2) 'no code path in the app can ever see the new one' is wrong: anyio's WorkerThread has MAX_IDLE_TIME = 10 (verified in the installed anyio at Lib/site-packages/anyio/_backends/_asyncio.py), so idle threadpool workers are retired and the next request opens a fresh connection — to the new file. The stale read is therefore a timing-dependent window on threads kept warm by other dashboard traffic, not the permanent trap described. Real enough to be worth a note, not established as the deterministic failure claimed; I could not establish how warm the pool actually stays on the NAS.

### MUSIC-11 (minor, inefficiency) — compute_directions rebuilds set(members) once per candidate index, making the source-axis pass O(n·m) per group
`music/indexer/music_index/debias.py:73`

`others = [i for i in range(mat.shape[0]) if i not in set(members)]` evaluates `set(members)` inside the comprehension's condition, so the set is constructed afresh for every one of the `n` candidate indices rather than once. Per group that is O(n·|members|) element insertions instead of O(n + |members|). This runs on every `--retag`, and `--retag` is the documented cheap path ("re-scores every track in seconds; no re-embedding") that the tuning workflow leans on.

**Failure scenario:** Today, at 376 tracks with ~5 source groups and a largest group of ~180, this is ~340k redundant set insertions — tens of milliseconds, invisible. The failure is one of growth: SPEC.md already flags that the library growing substantially forces a re-rank, and at 10,000 tracks the same expression becomes ~5,000 × 10,000 = 50M insertions per group, turning a sub-second step into tens of seconds inside every retag.

**Suggested fix:** Hoist it: `member_set = set(members)` above the comprehension, then `others = [i for i in range(mat.shape[0]) if i not in member_set]` — or drop the list entirely and compute the complement mean with a boolean mask (`mask = np.zeros(n, bool); mask[members] = True; mat[~mask].mean(axis=0)`), which also avoids materialising an index list of length n.

**Verifier:** debias.py:73 is `others = [i for i in range(mat.shape[0]) if i not in set(members)]` — set(members) sits in the comprehension's condition, so CPython rebuilds it on every one of the n iterations; hoisting is behaviourally identical (members is a plain list of ints built at :68 and not mutated in the loop). compute_directions is called from index_music.retag:145 on every --retag, which README/CLAUDE position as the cheap tuning path. The finding itself concedes it is invisible at 376 tracks, so this is a growth/cleanliness item, not a live failure — but the inefficiency is real and the fix is a one-line hoist or a boolean mask.

## SHIP — Installer, onboarding, tools, bench

### SHIP-1 (critical, bug) — A failed -RebuildOnboard PyInstaller run still publishes the PREVIOUS onboard.exe under the NEW installer version (OPS-7 was only fixed for the companion half)
`installer/build_editor_package.ps1:266`

The companion rebuild records its PyInstaller exit code in `$script:PyInstallerExit`, refuses to restamp the manifest on failure (line 206), and hard-refuses to publish (line 472-479) — that is the OPS-7 fix. The onboard rebuild at lines 264-271 does none of it: a non-zero exit is `Write-Warn2` only, no variable is kept, `Set-Failed` is never called, and the publish path never consults it. The only thing standing between a failed onboard build and an upload is the mtime comparison at line 553 (`onboard.exe older than the companion exe`), which is satisfied whenever the companion exe was NOT rebuilt in the same run — precisely the R13 recovery invocation `build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildOnboard -Publish -MakeCurrent`.

**Failure scenario:** R13 recovery: 0.7.6's companion published, the installer 409'd, so INSTALLER_VERSION is bumped 1.0.24→1.0.25 and the admin re-runs `installer\build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildOnboard -Publish -MakeCurrent` (no -RebuildExe). PyInstaller fails (a locked dist\onboard.exe, a missing hook, an OOM). The script prints one yellow WARNING and carries on. `onboarding\dist\onboard.exe` is yesterday's build, whose mtime is NEWER than `companion\dist\ccsync-companion.exe` (built earlier), so no skip reason is set; `$onboardSha` is hashed off the stale exe and it is PUT as installer v1.0.25 with make_current=1, exit code 0. The dashboard's [ INSTALLER ] download now serves an onboard.exe that bundles the OLD companion, labelled 1.0.25 — and because the channel refuses to reuse a version for different bytes, fixing it needs another version bump. Every new editor onboarded from that point gets a companion that cannot self-update, which is the 2026-07-25 rollout failure verbatim.

**Suggested fix:** Mirror the companion path exactly: capture `$script:OnboardPyInstallerExit = $LASTEXITCODE` at line 266, `Set-Failed` when it is non-zero, and add it to the `$onboardSkipReason` chain (before the mtime test) so a failed build can never be published.

**Verifier:** installer/build_editor_package.ps1 lines 258-271 (onboard rebuild): `& $onboardPython -m PyInstaller build_onboard.spec` -> `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { Write-Warn2 ... }` and nothing else — no $script: variable, no Set-Failed. Contrast the companion path at 182-207 which stores $script:PyInstallerExit, refuses the restamp, and hard-refuses the publish at 471-479. The $onboardSkipReason chain (540-557) tests only: version parse failure, three-way installer version drift, missing onboard.exe, and `onboard.exe LastWriteTime -lt companion exe LastWriteTime`. A failed -RebuildOnboard in a run with no -RebuildExe leaves the companion exe older than yesterday's onboard.exe, so no skip reason is set and the stale exe is hashed (line 552) and PUT (line 683). The report section at 400-415 does compute the real staleness (onboard.exe vs onboarding/*.py + windows_bootstrap.ps1 + companion exe) — but it is Write-Warn2 only, never Set-Failed, and it is not in the publish chain. ship.ps1's own path (step 2b, line 333, -RebuildOnboard after release.ps1 rebuilt the companion) is protected only incidentally by the mtime test; the R13 recovery invocation is not.

### SHIP-2 (major, logic) — The relaunch is fire-and-forget: a new build that dies on startup still reports "Upgrade complete" and exit 0, so ship prints "ship complete" with no companion running
`installer/windows_upgrade.ps1:312`

OPS-4 was closed by making a failed COPY exit 1. The relaunch was not: `Start-Process` succeeding only means CreateProcess succeeded. There is no "is it still alive N seconds later" confirmation, even though every other launcher in this repo has one — `onboarding/steps.py:1906-1907` sleeps 3 s and checks `proc.poll() is None`, and `windows_bootstrap.ps1`'s companion step "confirms it's still running a few seconds later" (quoted in `steps.run_bootstrap`'s docstring). `$copySucceeded` is the only input to the exit code (line 346), so a companion that exits 1 s after launch produces "Upgrade complete -- now running v0.7.8" and exit 0. `ship.ps1:395` gates solely on that code and then prints "ship complete. Editors' trays will offer v<X>" — `check_deploy_drift.ps1` runs at line 405 but its output is never read and it always exits 0 by design.

**Failure scenario:** Ship 0.7.8 on the base rig. Step 3 kills the running 0.7.6, copies the new exe, and Start-Processes it. The new build exits immediately — R11's exact scenario is on record (a companion hitting the single-instance guard 1.1 s after spawn and standing down; `_acquire_mutex_win32`'s hand-off wait only arms when `CCSYNC_REPLACES_PID` is set, which this script never sets), or a frozen-build DLL/config failure. windows_upgrade prints "Upgrade complete -- now running v0.7.8", exits 0; ship prints "ship complete"; the fleet is already offered 0.7.8 as CURRENT. The base rig now has NO companion at all — no lanes, no Resolve bridge, no proxy generator — and nothing retries, because the Run key is logon-only. This is R8/R11 reached from the admin script instead of the tray.

**Suggested fix:** After `Start-Process`, keep the returned process object (`-PassThru`), `Start-Sleep -Seconds 3`, and re-check `Get-Process -Name ccsync-companion`; if it is gone, warn loudly and `exit 1` alongside `$copySucceeded`. Also have `ship.ps1` treat a `DRIFT` verdict from `check_deploy_drift.ps1` as something other than silence (e.g. re-read the installed-version line) rather than printing "ship complete" unconditionally.

**Verifier:** installer/windows_upgrade.ps1 305-317: `Start-Process -FilePath $CompanionExePath -WorkingDirectory $BinDir` with no -PassThru, no sleep, no Get-Process re-check; the only thing in the exit code is `if (-not $copySucceeded) { exit 1 }` (line 346). tools/ship.ps1 393-407 gates step 3 solely on $LASTEXITCODE, then runs check_deploy_drift.ps1 whose result is never read (it ends `exit 0` at line 472 with the comment 'Drift is NOT signalled here -- read the VERDICT line') and prints 'ship complete' unconditionally. The liveness pattern does exist elsewhere: onboarding/steps.py:1906-1907 (`sleep(3.0); return proc.poll() is None`). CAVEAT on the failure scenario: the R11 mutex race is a poor illustration here — step 1 (lines 118-129) force-kills every ccsync-companion and waits 800 ms, so the named mutex is released by the OS before the new exe launches, and R11 itself is fixed in 0.7.5 (KNOWN_BUGS.md:158). The gap is real for any other startup death (frozen-build DLL/config failure, a bad config.toml), which is enough.

### SHIP-3 (major, bug) — The macOS release has no vendored-ytdl_common parity gate, and the suite that pins that parity is not the one it runs
`tools/release_macos.sh:273`

`tools/release.ps1:333-359` makes vendored-file drift a hard refusal: `companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_common.py` must be byte-identical to `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/ytdl_common.py` below the marker, because "the exe about to be built BAKES IN whatever is in companion/src". `release_macos.sh` — described in its own header as "the Mac-side twin of tools/release.ps1" — stops at version parity (line 273) and has no equivalent check (grep for `ytdl_common` across `tools/` matches only release.ps1). The byte-parity assertion lives in `server/tests/test_cross_component.py:368-389`, and release_macos.sh runs only the COMPANION suite (line 377), never server/. `ship.cmd` is what runs server/, and it cannot run on a Mac.

**Failure scenario:** Someone edits `ytdl/web/ytdlweb/ytdl_common.py` (bumping the outtmpl or the sidecar shape) and forgets to re-copy it below the marker in the companion. On Windows `tools\release.ps1` refuses to build. On the Mac, `git pull && ./tools/release_macos.sh --publish --make-current` sails through version parity, runs the companion suite (which does not cross-check the pair), builds, and publishes a macOS companion whose local ytdl downloader writes a different filename for the same clip than the NAS worker does. Both write into the one canonical Youtube tree that syncs fleet-wide, so the same video appears twice under two spellings — the exact failure docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §5 and the Windows gate exist to prevent, discovered months later.
