**Suggested fix:** Port `Get-VendorParityProblem` into release_macos.sh as a `cmp`-based check (strip the marker line from the vendored copy, `cmp -s` the remainder against the source; a missing/marker-less file is a failure, not a skip) and run it in step 2/6 alongside the version parity, with the same `fail` semantics.

**Verifier:** `grep -rn ytdl_common tools/` matches only tools/release.ps1 (lines 26, 102-103, 338-360) — release_macos.sh has no vendored-parity gate. release_macos.sh:260-273 does version parity plus the semver regex and stops there; step 3/6 (line 377) runs only the companion suite (`cd $COMPANION_DIR && CCSYNC_REQUIRE_RCLONE=1 pytest -q`). The byte-parity assertion is indeed in server/tests/test_cross_component.py (line 33-34 names YTDL_COMMON_SRC and cites docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §5), and companion/tests/test_ytdl_executor.py only imports the vendored module — it never compares it to ytdl/web's copy. Partial mitigation worth noting: TEMPLATE_VERSION/SIDECAR_VERSION let an older companion decline a claim (docs §171), but only if the editor bumps them, which is the same thing they forgot to do.

### SHIP-4 (major, upgrade) — Ship pre-flights the companion version against the server but not the installer version — the one that must be bumped on every single ship
`tools/ship.ps1:156`

Lines 156-167 refuse to start when `companion/<VERSION>` is already published, precisely so a five-minute build and a password prompt are not wasted. No such check exists for the installer version, even though R13 records that `onboard.exe` bundles the companion exe, so "a companion release changes the installer's bytes by itself" and an unbumped INSTALLER_VERSION is a guaranteed 409 — "third time it has bitten". Step 0's onboarding suite and release.ps1 both only check the four copies agree with EACH OTHER, never that the number is unused on the server. The endpoint to ask is the same shape already used at line 158 (`/api/v1/companion/package/windows/<v>`, kind=onboard), and the token is already in hand.

**Failure scenario:** Bump `VERSION` 0.7.8→0.7.9 in config.py + pyproject.toml, forget `$InstallerVersion`/`INSTALLER_VERSION`/the .spec (four sites, all still 1.0.27, all mutually consistent so every parity check passes). ship.cmd runs: dashboard deployed, both suites green, PyInstaller builds, companion 0.7.9 PUT and made CURRENT for the whole fleet — then the onboard upload 409s with different bytes, `Set-Failed` fires, build_editor_package exits 1, and ship hard-stops before step 3. Final state: fleet offered a companion the base rig is not running, installer channel still serving the 1.0.27 bytes that bundle 0.7.8, and the only supported recovery is the hand-run R13 documents. A 200 ms HEAD-equivalent before anything moved would have caught it.

**Suggested fix:** In the same `if (-not $DashboardOnly)` block, parse `$InstallerVersion` from windows_bootstrap.ps1 and `Invoke-CurlWithToken` the onboard package URL for it; refuse to start with the same message build_editor_package prints ("bump $InstallerVersion in windows_bootstrap.ps1 AND INSTALLER_VERSION in steps.py AND macos_bootstrap.sh AND build_onboard_macos.spec").

**Verifier:** tools/ship.ps1 156-167 pre-flights only the companion version (`GET /api/v1/companion/package/windows/$CompanionVersion` with DASH_REPORT_TOKEN); grep for InstallerVersion in ship.ps1 finds it only inside the advice string at line 164. The suggested check is viable: the download route (dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1936) takes `kind` as a query param and `_require_package_read` accepts the same report token, so `.../package/windows/<iv>?kind=onboard` is a 200/404 existence probe with the token already in hand. The failure chain is as described: step 2b (ship.ps1:333) runs build_editor_package -RebuildOnboard -Publish -MakeCurrent, which publishes the companion FIRST (line 683 area) and only then hits the installer 409 -> Set-Failed (line 701) -> exit 1 -> ship stops before step 3. Because onboard.exe bundles the companion exe, a full ship always changes the installer bytes, so an unbumped INSTALLER_VERSION is a guaranteed different-bytes 409 (KNOWN_BUGS.md R13, line 339: 'third time it has bitten').

### SHIP-5 (major, logic) — A suite whose interpreter is the bare string "python" is never existence-checked, and a missing python leaves $LASTEXITCODE from the PREVIOUS suite — printing PASS for a suite that never ran
`tools/run_all_tests.ps1:82`

Line 82 skips the interpreter existence check exactly when `$s.Py -eq "python"`, which covers the `onboarding` and `broll/indexer` suites. Line 95 then runs `& $s.Py -m pytest tests -q`. Under `$ErrorActionPreference = "Continue"` (line 31) a CommandNotFoundException is non-terminating and, critically, does NOT update `$LASTEXITCODE` — PowerShell only sets it when a native process actually runs. Line 96 reads that stale value, which is whatever the preceding suite left, and prints PASS. This is the same class of defect OPS-6 fixed in this very file ("from PowerShell with no bash they SKIP SILENTLY and the table prints PASS"). `ship.ps1:248-254` has the identical shape for step 0b.

**Failure scenario:** On a machine where `python` is not on PATH (a fresh dev box, a PATH clobbered by a venv activation, python moved to `py -3`), `run_all_tests.ps1` runs: companion PASS, dashboard PASS, server PASS (exit 0). The onboarding suite's `& python` errors out to the console mid-scroll, `$LASTEXITCODE` is still 0 from the server bash invocation, and the table prints `onboarding      PASS`. The final line says "0 of 11 suites failed" and the script exits 0. The onboarding suite holds the ONLY check on the fourth installer-version copy (`build_onboard_macos.spec`'s CFBundleShortVersionString) — per ship.ps1:239-245 — so a bump that missed the .spec ships an onboard.exe whose bundle disagrees with itself, with a green table saying otherwise.

**Suggested fix:** Resolve `"python"` through `Get-Command python -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue` up front and record `NO INTERPRETER` when it is absent; and set `$LASTEXITCODE = 9999` (or `$global:LASTEXITCODE = $null` and test for null) immediately before every `&` invocation so a command that never ran can never inherit the previous suite's success.

**Verifier:** tools/run_all_tests.ps1:82 — `if (-not (Test-Path $s.Py) -and $s.Py -ne "python")` skips the NO INTERPRETER record precisely for the two bare-"python" suites (onboarding, broll/indexer); line 95 then runs `& $s.Py -m pytest tests -q` and line 96 reads $LASTEXITCODE. I measured the premise on this box (Windows PowerShell 5.1.26100): with $ErrorActionPreference='Continue', `cmd /c "exit 0"` then `& 'definitely-not-a-command-xyz' -m pytest` prints a CommandNotFoundException and $LASTEXITCODE is STILL 0 — the stale value is read and the table prints PASS, and `$failed` counts it as a pass so the script exits 0. tools/ship.ps1:248-252 has the identical shape (`& python -m pytest tests -q; $obRc = $LASTEXITCODE`), and ship.ps1:239-245 confirms this suite is the only check on the fourth installer-version copy.

### SHIP-6 (major, logic) — Resume treats skipped and failed rows as "already ran this combo", so installing the missing tool and re-running silently measures nothing
`bench/ccbench/matrix.py:155`

`existing_keys(results_file)` (result.py:138) builds its set from EVERY row in results.jsonl, including rows with `skipped=True` (missing binary, unsupported syncthing major, unsupported direction) and `ok=False` (timeout, rclone non-zero, short transfer, runner crash). `RunResult.key()` does not include `ok`/`skipped`. So line 155's `if ($key in known_keys and not rerun)` skips combos that were never actually measured. `--rerun` is the only escape and it re-runs everything, including the good rows — and because the file is append-only, that also compounds SHIP-7.

**Failure scenario:** Run the matrix on a machine without syncthing installed: every lane-C combo is recorded as `skipped=True, reason="'syncthing' not found on PATH"`. Install syncthing, re-run `ccbench run --config bench.toml`. Every lane-C combo prints `[skip-cached]`, nothing is measured, `run_matrix` returns 0 new rows, and `ccbench report` still renders lane C as "No successful runs recorded for this lane yet." (`report.py:225`). Same for a lane-A upload that timed out once against a busy NAS: the retry never happens. The operator's reasonable conclusion — "resume picked up where it left off" — is wrong.

**Suggested fix:** Build the resume set from successful rows only: `existing_keys` should filter `r.ok and not r.skipped` (or take a predicate), so a resumed matrix re-attempts anything that did not produce a measurement while still skipping completed work.

**Verifier:** bench/ccbench/result.py:138 `existing_keys` = `{r.key() for r in read_results(path)}` — every row, and key() (line 58-59) is engine+lane+dataset+direction+params+repeat_index only, no ok/skipped. matrix.py:155 `if key in known_keys and not rerun` therefore treats skipped and failed rows as done. Skipped rows really are written to results.jsonl: runners return make_skipped(...) (syncthing.py:544/555/564/576, robocopy_smb.py:119/162, _rclone_common.py:297/332, iperf3.py:91/95) and matrix.py:181 appends whatever the runner returned. The only test on this path (bench/tests/test_matrix_resume.py:35-77) uses a fake runner that always returns ok=True, so nothing pins skipped/failed-row semantics; bench/README.md:85 says only 'combos already recorded are skipped', which is a description of the bug rather than a decision documenting it. report.py:224-225 does print 'No successful runs recorded for this lane yet.' when every row in a lane is not-ok.

### SHIP-7 (major, logic) — The report medians every historical row for a combo with no time window, so a re-run after a tuning change is blended with the numbers from before it
`bench/ccbench/report.py:127`

`summarize_repeats` groups by `combo_key_no_repeat` over the whole of results.jsonl (`_cli_run` reads the entire file at line 341) and takes `statistics.median` of every `ok` row in the group. `RunResult.timestamp` is recorded but never read anywhere in report.py, and `ccbench report` has no `--since`/`--run-id` flag. `matrix.py` is append-only by design and `--rerun` appends rather than replacing, so the ONLY documented way to re-measure a combo guarantees the old and new measurements end up in the same median.

**Failure scenario:** Lane A is measured at 380 MB/s over 2 repeats. The NAS is then tuned (recordsize=1M + compression=off, the change recorded in the TrueNAS perf notes) and `ccbench run --rerun` measures 760 MB/s over 2 repeats. `ccbench report` groups all four rows: median ≈ 570 MB/s, `n 4/4`, min 380 / max 760 — a number that was never measured, presented as the lane-A result, and `_winner` picks flags from whichever representative row happens to sit nearest that fabricated median (`report.py:140`), which may be the pre-tuning `--transfers` value. The docstring's rule 1 ("median, not max") is what makes this silent: a bimodal distribution looks like a wide-but-honest spread.

**Suggested fix:** Add a run/session id (or `--since <iso8601>` using the existing `timestamp` field) and default the report to the most recent complete run per combo; at minimum warn in the leaderboard's Notes column when a combo's rows span more than a few hours.

**Verifier:** bench/ccbench/report.py:118-123 groups by combo_key_no_repeat (line 68-69: engine, lane, direction, dataset, params — no repeat_index, no time), then medians every ok row in the group (line 138-140) and picks the representative as `min(ok_rows, key=|MB_s - median|)`. `_cli_run` (line 341) reads the whole file, and the report subparser (330-335) offers only --results/--out/--baseline-mbps — no --since/--run-id. RunResult.timestamp exists (result.py:28) and is never referenced in report.py. matrix.py is append-only and --rerun appends (test_matrix_resume.py:77 pins that), so re-measuring after a tuning change does blend old and new into one median with n reported as the combined count. Mitigations short of a code change: point --results at a fresh file, or rotate results.jsonl — neither is documented anywhere I could find, so the default path is the blended one.

### SHIP-8 (major, bug) — The CRLF guard on macos_bootstrap.sh only runs under -Publish, but the file is copied into the editor package on every run
`installer/build_editor_package.ps1:576`

`macos_bootstrap.sh` is entry 6 of `$Files` (line 288) and is copied to `$Destination` unconditionally in the loop at 317-344. The byte-level CR scan that `Test-FileHasCr` exists for is invoked only at line 576, inside `if ($Publish)`. So the documented non-publish invocation — `.\build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildExe`, the script's own .EXAMPLE at line 84, and the CLAUDE.md line `installer\build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildExe -RebuildOnboard` — pushes the .sh to `P:\Assets\Software\CC_Sync` with no check at all. CLAUDE.md's own convention note says a working copy checked out before a `.gitattributes` rule was added stays CRLF until re-checked-out, and that MSYS grep cannot find the CR — which is exactly why this byte scan exists.

**Failure scenario:** A checkout predating the `eol=lf` rule (or a hand-edit in a Windows editor) leaves CRs in `installer/macos_bootstrap.sh`. An admin refreshes the share with `build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildExe -RebuildOnboard` (no -Publish — the fleet publish is done separately, or this is a package-only refresh). The CRLF copy lands on P:. A Mac editor is pointed at the share and runs it; bash reads the CR as part of the last token on the first line (`set -eu` → "Illegal option -"), the install dies at line 1, and the failure surfaces on the editor's Mac with nothing in this repo having warned — the same shape that crash-looped the dashboard container on 2026-07-26.

**Suggested fix:** Move the `Test-FileHasCr` / missing-file checks on `$bootstrapSh` out of the `if ($Publish)` block to just before (or inside) the `$Files` copy loop, so an LF-dirty script fails the package assembly whether or not this run publishes.

**Verifier:** installer/build_editor_package.ps1: macos_bootstrap.sh is $Files entry 6 (line 288) and the loop at 317-344 copies it to $Destination on every non-DryRun run; the only Test-FileHasCr call in the whole file is at line 576, inside `if ($Publish)` (block opens at 450). Confirmed by grep: Test-FileHasCr appears at 132 (definition) and 576 (sole call). The non-publish invocation is documented in two places — the script's own .EXAMPLE at line 84 (`.\build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildExe`) and CLAUDE.md:128 (`installer\build_editor_package.ps1 -RebuildExe -RebuildOnboard   # editor package (add -Publish -MakeCurrent to ship it)`) — so the package-refresh-without-publish path is a supported, documented one, and it pushes an unchecked .sh to P:. The function's own comment (132-141) states the rationale (dashboard/deploy/run.sh crash-loop, 2026-07-26) and that a stale checkout or a Windows hand-edit reintroduces CR.

### SHIP-10 (minor, logic) — The lane-C clock has 0.5 s of slop on both ends: transfer starts before the timer and completion is detected only at a poll boundary
`bench/ccbench/runners/syncthing.py:624`

`_wait_for_peer_connection` returns at the first 0.5 s poll that observes `connected` (line 463-467) — the connection, and therefore the start of indexing and transfer, can be up to `POLL_INTERVAL_S` older than that. The Timer at line 624 only starts afterwards, so that window is untimed transfer. At the other end, `_wait_for_sync` returns `time.monotonic() - start` measured at the poll that first sees `needBytes == 0`, overstating by up to another 0.5 s. The module's own docstring cites a real measurement of 2.047 s for a completed lane-C sync, so the error is on the order of ±25% of the quantity being reported.

**Failure scenario:** Lane C (small files, loopback pair) syncs in ~2 s. Repeat 1 happens to have the connection observed promptly and completion detected 0.45 s late → 2.45 s → 20.4 MB/s. Repeat 2 has 0.4 s of transfer already done before the clock starts and completion seen 0.05 s late → 1.65 s → 30.3 MB/s. `summarize_repeats` medians two numbers 50% apart and `_winner` (report.py:209) picks the lane-C recommendation from that, while the min/max columns make it look like real run-to-run variance rather than instrument error.

**Suggested fix:** Tighten the poll interval for the completion loop (e.g. 0.05 s, the REST call is local), and start the timer from the moment the connection was last observed absent rather than after the confirming poll — or subtract the known poll granularity and record it on the row so the report can show the measurement's resolution.

**Verifier:** Verified in bench/ccbench/runners/syncthing.py: POLL_INTERVAL_S = 0.5 (line 80). The dataset is seeded into the source folder at line 590, BEFORE _wait_for_peer_connection at 615, so once the peers actually connect the transfer can start while the connect poll (463-467, checks then sleeps 0.5 s) has not yet observed it — that window is untimed, and base.Timer only starts at line 624. _wait_for_sync (498-520) likewise checks then sleeps 0.5 s, so completion is detected at a poll boundary, overstating by up to 0.5 s. Against the module docstring's own measured 2.047 s completion (line 22) that is a ~±25% instrument error on a ~2 s quantity, and the row carries no resolution field so report.py cannot show it. CAVEAT on the stated consequence: syncthing rows are written loopback=True, and report.py:209-217 excludes loopback rows from _winner while any non-loopback candidate exists, and the leaderboard flags them 'YES -- not comparable' — so the wrong-recommendation half only materialises when syncthing is the only engine that produced lane-C rows. The measurement defect itself is real regardless.

### SHIP-11 (minor, logic) — Windows release accepts a VERSION the dashboard will reject; the macOS twin catches it before building
`tools/release.ps1:331`

`release_macos.sh:270-272` refuses to build when VERSION does not match `^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$`, with the reason spelled out: "The dashboard rejects anything else (_PACKAGE_VERSION_RE), so a 0.4.5-dev would build fine here and 422 at the publish -- after the whole build." `release.ps1`'s parity block (lines 298-331) only checks that config.py and pyproject.toml agree; a non-semver value that agrees in both places passes.

**Failure scenario:** Set VERSION = "0.7.9-rc1" in both config.py and pyproject.toml to test a candidate. `tools\ship.cmd` deploys the dashboard, runs the server/onboarding suites, then release.ps1 runs the editable install, both suites and a five-minute PyInstaller build, stamps a manifest — and only then does build_editor_package's PUT come back 422 from `_PACKAGE_VERSION_RE`. Ten minutes and a fleet dashboard deploy spent on something the Mac script refuses in 20 ms.

**Suggested fix:** Add the same regex gate to release.ps1's step 1, right after `$Version` is parsed, with the same message — it is a two-line port and keeps the two release scripts honest twins.

**Verifier:** tools/release_macos.sh:269-272 has the regex gate with the stated reason ('The dashboard rejects anything else (_PACKAGE_VERSION_RE), so a 0.4.5-dev would build fine here and 422 at the publish -- after the whole build'). tools/release.ps1's parity block (298-331) accumulates $mismatches from pyproject-vs-config.py equality and the three installer-version copies only; there is no format check, and Get-Capture's pattern is '^VERSION\s*=\s*"([^"]+)"' — anything in quotes passes. The server side is as described: dashboard/src/ccsync_dashboard/api.py:1656 `_PACKAGE_VERSION_RE = re.compile(r"^\d+\.\d+\.\d+$")` and line 1808-1809 raises HTTPException(422, 'version must look like 1.2.3'). So '0.7.9-rc1' set consistently in both files survives ship.ps1's dashboard deploy, both suites and the PyInstaller build and only 422s at build_editor_package's PUT.

### SHIP-12 (minor, upgrade) — The macOS wizard publish has no already-published pre-flight, unlike its sibling — the whole build, sign, zip and password prompt happen before the 409
`tools/build_onboard_macos.sh:417`

`release_macos.sh:570-597` learned this lesson explicitly ("discovering that after a password prompt wastes the run — tools/ship.ps1 learned the same lesson on 2026-07-26") and probes `/api/v1/companion/package/macos/$VERSION` with the report token from `~/.ccsync/config.toml` before doing anything. `build_onboard_macos.sh` — which shares the byte-identical password-hygiene block with it — has no equivalent: the 409 handling at line 428 is the first time the server is consulted, after PyInstaller, two codesign passes, a ditto zip and a zip round-trip verification.

**Failure scenario:** The installer version was bumped and published from the Mac, then something else in the ship failed and the admin re-runs `./tools/build_onboard_macos.sh --publish --make-current`. Several minutes of build + sign + zip + round-trip verify, then a password prompt, then HTTP 409 telling them to bump the shared installer version across four files and start over. Worse, because R13's identical-bytes case is not distinguished here (unlike `build_editor_package.ps1:687-705`), a re-run that produced functionally identical content still reads as a hard failure.

**Suggested fix:** Lift release_macos.sh's `PRE_CODE` pre-flight (report token from ~/.ccsync/config.toml, `-K -`, `-r 0-0`) into this script before step 4/5, keyed on `macos/$ONBOARD_VERSION?kind=onboard`; and on a 409, compare the server's sha256 the way build_editor_package.ps1 now does so an unchanged republish says so instead of failing.

**Verifier:** tools/build_onboard_macos.sh consults the server for the first time at the PUT (line 419); the 409 branch at 428-433 is a bare `fail` with no sha comparison. Ordering verified: read_dashboard_password is only DEFINED in the shared hygiene block (lines 155-168) and is CALLED in the publish section after step 4/5 — PyInstaller build (283), codesign -dv (290) and --verify --deep --strict (305), ditto zip (325), and the ditto -x -k round-trip re-verify (341-351) all precede the login at 385-413. The sibling's pre-flight it should copy is tools/release_macos.sh:570-597 (report token from ~/.ccsync/config.toml, `-K -` on stdin, `-r 0-0`, treats 200/206 as already-published, 404 as clear, anything else as a warning). The endpoint supports it: api.py:1936 takes `kind` as a query param and _require_package_read accepts the report token. The identical-bytes distinction the finding wants exists on the Windows side at build_editor_package.ps1:687-705 (installer) and 640-665 (companion, the R13 fix) and has no counterpart here.

## Refuted (archived so the next hunt skips them)

- **COMP-CORE-1** — R11's predecessor wait (20 s) is shorter than the predecessor's own bounded teardown (>44 s), so a self-upgrade can still leave the machine with no companion (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:162`)
  - Refutation: The premise that the parent holds the mutex until shutdown()'s bounded joins finish is wrong on Windows. shutdown() runs on the tray daemon thread (tray.py:1523 _spawn -> apply_upgrade -> upgrade._apply_inner -> request_shutdown), and its FIRST action after the latch is self._stop_event.set() (app.py:3974). On Windows ui_dispatch.start() returns None (ui_dispatch.py:491-501, uses_main_thread is darwin-only), so run()'s main thread is parked in `while not self._stop_event.is_set(): self._stop_event.wait(1.0)` (app.py:4254-4255). It therefore wakes within <=1 s, run()'s finally calls self.shutdown() which returns immediately on the _shutdown_started latch (app.py:3969-3973 -- the lock is released before the teardown, so it never blocks), _tray_icon.stop() only joins pystray's already-finished setup thread (.venv pystray/_base.py:244-259), run() returns and the interpreter finalises. I enumerated every threading.Thread( construction in companion/src/ccsync_companion (script over the AST-ish text): every one passes daemon=True, watchdog's BaseThread also sets daemon (watchdog/utils/__init__.py:40), and there are no atexit hooks -- so the 44 s of bounded joins carry on inside a daemon thread that process exit kills. The named mutex is released ~1-2 s after request_shutdown(), an order of magnitude inside PREDECESSOR_WAIT_SECONDS = 20.0, which also matches the R11 evidence in KNOWN_BUGS.md:158-180 (the child lost by ~1.1 s, not by tens of seconds). Residual worth noting, but not the reported mechanism: a fusionscript call holds the GIL for its full native duration (tray.py:1630-1634 comment), so a wedged Resolve can freeze the whole interpreter -- in that state no PREDECESSOR_WAIT value helps and the join ceilings are irrelevant.
- **COMP-GUARD-2** — The NON_CANONICAL auto-relink gets exactly one un-retried ReplaceClip, then the path is burned for the process (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/app.py:1511`)
  - Refutation: The two premises are individually accurate (tries=1 at app.py:1511; the latch is added at watcher.py:255-257 with no reset), but the conclusion — one attempt, then burned for the process — is wrong on two counts. (1) The latch is a DELIBERATE, test-pinned decision, not an oversight: watcher.py:122-129 documents it ("the latch only stops a REFUSED relink being retried every poll"), app._classify_pool_once's docstring repeats it verbatim ("once per path -- a fixed path never reappears, a refusal must not retry every pass"), and two tests pin it, including the refused case: tests/test_watcher.py:759 and tests/test_app.py:4117 (which explicitly pretends the relink reverted and asserts no second ReplaceClip). tries=1 for canonicalization batches is the same documented repo pattern as resolve_bridge._canonicalize_imported (:1371-1379: "One ReplaceClip attempt per item... Refusals are logged and left alone"). (2) The clip is not abandoned after one attempt: the media-pool sweep (app.py:2389-2437) classifies the WHOLE media pool every media_tree_refresh_interval (default 120s, app.py:747) with its own independent latch `_pool_offered_non_canonical` (app.py:756), so a clip whose watcher relink failed transiently gets a second, independent attempt ~120s later — exactly covering the "Resolve mid-conform" scenario. And the _SCRIPTING_ERROR_MESSAGE scenario is covered too: when the Resolve the companion was connected to exits, app._maybe_restart_for_stale_bridge (app.py:2340-2377, R12's stale-fusionscript recovery) restarts the companion, clearing both latches.
- **COMP-GUARD-3** — A torn `_save_both` permanently wedges the LUT-location reconcile, because `has_lut_location` reads only config.dat (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/resolve_prefs.py:336`)
  - Refutation: The mechanical observations hold (_save_both at resolve_prefs.py:518 writes dat then data with no rollback; has_lut_location at :336 reads lut_locations() which reads only LUT_COUNT_KEY_DAT/LUT_ITEM_KEY_DAT from config.dat; the data_count!=count guard at :357 is unreachable while has_lut_location returns True). But the claimed permanent, self-perpetuating wedge is refuted by the very Resolve behaviour the finding relies on. The module docstring (:1-30), SPEC.md flaw 7 and docs/GOTCHAS.md:468-480 all state that config.dat is a mirror that Resolve rebuilds from the GUI form, and that Resolve rewrites BOTH files from memory on exit. So the torn state cannot persist: the moment Resolve next launches/quits, the config.dat-only `Custom.LUT.Path.<n>` entry is dropped — at which point has_lut_location returns False, add_lut_location runs again with count==data_count==N and succeeds (or, if the files genuinely diverge, falls through to the FORMAT_UNRECOGNISED guard at :357, which is exactly what it is for). The failure scenario is internally inconsistent: it asserts the entry evaporates from config.dat AND that has_lut_location keeps answering True. The window is also gated: add_lut_location refuses when resolve_is_running() (:352), so the only torn write is the narrow race between that check and a launch. Underlying suggestion (make has_lut_location consult both files, write .config.data first) is reasonable hardening, but no silent permanent wedge is established.
- **SYNC-2** — Lane A's filter has no .ccsync-trash exclude, so it re-uploads everything lane B trashed (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:372`)
  - Refutation: The missing TRASH_EXCLUDE_RULE in build_filter_rules_up (rclone_lane.py:372-377) is real, but no reachable path feeds it. (1) The stated trigger cannot happen: '+ /Youtube/' / '+ /Youtube/**' (rclone_lane.py:428-429) are ROOT-anchored at the run's transfer root, and in legacy whole-tree mode that root is local_root (config.example.toml:24 local_root="C:\\Creators_Club"; projects live under Projects/…), so a legacy lane B pass never even considers Projects/2026/FF5/Energy/Youtube/*, let alone trashes it. Legacy lane B can only trash Proxy/ content, and the backup dir mirrors the source layout (proved by test_rclone_filters.py:1077-1099), so those paths keep their Proxy component and are excluded from lane A by '- **/Proxy/**'. (2) In managed mode lane A only ever runs per-subpath (sequencer.py:1005/1038-1040), and build_up_command's source is local_root/<subpath> (rclone_lane.py:1038-1039) while trash sits at local_root/.ccsync-trash/… (rclone_lane.py:1784) — outside the transfer root entirely. (3) The express claim is wrong for managed mode: _maybe_trigger only calls notify_path_changed inside the 'rel is not None' branch (rclone_lane.py:2646), and _project_rel_for_path rejects anything whose first component is not 'projects' (rclone_lane.py:1508). (4) The one residual shape — a legacy machine doing a per-subpath lane B via consolidate (app.py:2115) — is gated by consolidate.lane_b_allowed (consolidate.py:304-312), which refuses the real run whenever the dry run reported any deletion. Worth adding as hardening; not a live bug.
- **SYNC-4** — Youtube originals are delivered by folding an include into lane B's `sync`, which hands rclone delete authority over editor-born files (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/sync/rclone_lane.py:428`)
  - Refutation: This is an explicitly documented, dated trade-off, not an overlooked consequence. build_filter_rules_down's docstring (rclone_lane.py:409-416) states it outright: 'Known trade-off (tracked in KNOWN_BUGS carryover item 22): lane B is a `sync`, so an editor-local file under Youtube/ that the NAS lacks is swept to .ccsync-trash until its upload lands (videos via lane A, everything else via lane C/Syncthing). The windows are the lanes' settle intervals; the trash toast surfaces any sweep, and nothing is deleted outright.' KNOWN_BUGS.md:417-419 carries the same item. And the very redesign the finding cites reviewed this interaction rather than missing it: docs/YTDL_LOCAL_DOWNLOAD.md §7 'Sync interactions (all already in place, verified 2026-08-14)' walks the executor→lane A path (lines 229-242) and records no change to lane B. Mitigations in code: --backup-dir is always supplied by RcloneLane (build_down_command docstring, rclone_lane.py:1233-1241) so nothing is deleted outright, _notify_trash toasts every sweep (rclone_lane.py:2307+), and lane B carries --min-age 120s (rclone_lane.py:1263-1266). The proposed split-into-a-second-copy-pass is a defensible redesign, but it re-litigates a documented decision rather than fixing an unnoticed defect.
- **COMP-MEDIA-10** — The proxy gap re-walks the whole tree even though the manifest walk 5 minutes earlier already enumerated every original and every Proxy/ file (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/proxy_scan.py:317`)
  - Refutation: The duplicate walk is real but it is a documented, deliberately mitigated cost, and the proposed derivation does not work against the manifest's actual data shape. proxy_scan's own module docstring and scan_missing_proxies:409-418 name it ("this is a SECOND full walk on top of the manifest's"), and scan_once repeats it while stating the mitigation: "The SECOND full walk this companion does (ManifestCache owns the first), which is the whole reason the cadence is 900 s and the project count is capped" (proxy_gen.py:915-921) — both walks are already narrowed by the same manifest.prioritize_project_rels/MAX_PROJECTS=64. The finding's premise that the manifest "already enumerated every original and every Proxy/ file" is wrong as a source of data: scan_local_manifest keeps per-file lists ONLY for selected_rels projects (originals/proxies stay None otherwise, manifest.py:136-141), truncates those lists at MAX_PER_FILE_ENTRIES=2000 (:36, :164-176), keeps only relative path + SIZE via size_fn=os.path.getsize with no mtime (so the settle window and the newest-first ordering cannot be derived), and does not prune Proxy//dot-dirs the way scan_project does (:256-273). Feeding the gap from that walk would mean changing the manifest — a dashboard report contract — to retain full path+mtime data for every project uncapped, which is a bigger cost than the walk it saves; and a shared cached listing across a 300 s and a 900 s thread reintroduces staleness the scanner deliberately re-verifies for.
- **COMP-BROLL-7** — Fresh locally-downloaded originals sit under lane B's `+ /Youtube/**` sync for at least one lane turn before lane A can upload them, and lane B is a delete-ing sync (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/ytdl_executor.py:953`)
  - Refutation: This exact behaviour is documented as a known, accepted trade-off at the cited code site. rclone_lane.build_filter_rules_down's docstring (:414-421) says verbatim: 'Known trade-off (tracked in KNOWN_BUGS carryover item 22): lane B is a `sync`, so an editor-local file under Youtube/ that the NAS lacks is swept to `.ccsync-trash` until its upload lands (videos via lane A, everything else via lane C/Syncthing). The windows are the lanes' settle intervals; the trash toast surfaces any sweep, and nothing is deleted outright.' That text is about Youtube/ files generally, not proxies, so the hunter's argument that KNOWN_BUGS.md:417-419 ('was item 22', mitigated by the tri-state proxy_gen_enabled default) no longer covers the case misses the broader in-code statement. Mechanics also soften the scenario: build_down_command passes --min-age 120s plus --max-delete/--max-delete-size caps and always runs with --backup-dir, so a fresh file is filtered out entirely for its first 120 s and any sweep is a recoverable move into .ccsync-trash with a toast, not a delete. The residual point is fair — once swept, lane A can no longer upload it, so 'until its upload lands' is optimistic and recovery is manual — but this is a tracked, documented risk with a named recovery path, not an undiscovered critical.
- **COMP-BROLL-11** — Every /music/send and every b-roll insert walks the entire media pool with one Resolve IPC call per clip, inside the 90 s timeout (`companion/src/ccsync_companion/music_worker.py:170`)
  - Refutation: Half the premise is false and the fix is a no-op. perform_insert does NOT walk the pool: resolve_bridge.py:1152 calls _find_existing_clip(broll_bin, ...) which iterates only that one bin's GetClipList (:1089-1116) — no recursion, no root walk. And the suggested optimisation saves nothing: existing_item already makes exactly ONE IPC call per clip (clip.GetClipProperty('File Path'), music_worker.py:172); calling GetClipProperty() with no argument — which is precisely what _find_existing_clip:1111 does — is still one call per clip, just with a bigger payload, so the round-trip count is unchanged. The pool-wide scope is also a documented deliberate decision, cited by name: resolve_bridge.py:1322-1326, 'skipped_existing is POOL-WIDE, not bin-wide (music_worker.existing_item's precedent): a clip the editor dragged out of Master/Youtube/<term> into their own bin must never be re-imported, and the pool walk that answers that question is the same one either way' — and the bin-first short-circuit does not help the miss case the finding is built on, since a miss still falls back to the full walk.
- **YTDL-WEB-6** — clip_status is not idempotent: a retried status post double-counts dl_done/dl_failed and re-writes the ledger (`ytdl/web/ytdlweb/routes_fleet.py:421`)
  - Refutation: Both halves of the failure scenario fail on the actual client and the actual close-out. (1) No retry exists. The only caller is the companion: FleetClient.clip_status (ytdl_executor.py:594-614) documents 'Failures other than 410 are logged and swallowed... a clip whose done never landed is simply retried once server-side' and its transport default_request (ytdl_executor.py:154-185) is bare urllib.request.urlopen with no Retry adapter, no loop and no re-post. A lost response therefore under-counts (handled by the second-chance sweep), it does not double-count. (2) The last-clip variant is impossible: _hand_back_to_the_server calls db.end_lease (db.py:679-683), which sets download_mode='server' and lease_expires_at=NULL, so any further post hits _leaseholder_or_410 (routes_fleet.py:87-102) and 410s - pinned by tests/test_local_download.py:445 test_the_companion_is_told_to_stop_after_the_hand_back. (3) The stated symptom is also wrong: app.js:786 clamps the bar with Math.min(100, ...), so no 107% is renderable. The endpoint is non-idempotent in principle, but no shipped path produces a duplicate post.
- **SHIP-9** — No explicit exit 0: the suite leaks `net use`'s exit code, which windows_bootstrap.ps1 itself documents as non-zero noise (`installer/tests/Test-DriveMapParser.ps1:72`)
  - Refutation: The premise — that a PowerShell script ending without `exit` leaks the last native exit code through `powershell -File` — does not hold on Windows PowerShell 5.1. Measured on this box (PSVersion 5.1.26100.9168), three ways: a script whose LAST statement is `cmd /c "exit 5"` -> parent $LASTEXITCODE 0 and cmd's %ERRORLEVEL% 0; the same via -Command -> 0; a script running `git rev-parse` in a non-repo (exit 128) then Write-Host -> 0. run_all_tests.ps1:105 invokes the suite exactly that way (`powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File ...`), a separate process, so net use's code cannot reach line 106. The check_deploy_drift.ps1:467-472 evidence the finding leans on is about the OTHER invocation form: $LASTEXITCODE is session-wide, so `& .\script.ps1` in-process does leak git's 128 — that is not how this file is called. I also checked the two ways the script could genuinely exit non-zero and both are closed: $ErrorActionPreference='Stop' is set (line 21) but Get-DriveMapping redirects stderr INSIDE cmd and wraps everything in try/catch (windows_bootstrap.ps1:397-416), and both Invoke-Expression extraction markers exist in order in the bootstrap (ConvertFrom-DriveMapReport at 343, the 'Reads the CURRENT USER's DOS device map' comment at 373, Get-DriveMapping at 388, Test-IsElevated at 427), so no Substring throw. Adding `exit 0` is harmless hygiene, but the described FAIL (exit 2) cannot occur.
